# goinfer code audit — 2026-09-02

**Tree:** `cfec302` (2026-09-01, v0.15.0-184), plus the one docs-only commit after it. Every
`path:line` below is keyed to that commit; `scripts/queue_citation_lint.py --update` needs one run
before this file is pushed, since the pre-push hook indexes every live `path:line`.

**Scope:** the whole repo at equal depth — all five modules (root, `gpu/`, `cuda/`, `metal/`,
`demo/agent/`), 897 Go files / ~170k lines (~70k non-test), the 15 `.cu` sources, the MSL and WGSL
shaders embedded as Go strings, `scripts/`, the workflows, and the docs that make claims about the
code. Same critical bar as the 2026-08-05 audit: silently wrong output, state corruption, a gate that
passes when it should fail, a reachable panic/leak/race, and anything that will be breaking to fix
after a tag.

**Method:** eleven independent reviewers, one per subsystem (decoder forward core; hybrid families +
cache lifecycle; loaders + paging; sampling + speculation; the HTTP surface; constrain + tokenizer +
multimodal + demos; CUDA; Metal; WebGPU; engineering/CI/release), plus a sourced landscape sweep, each
given `CLAUDE.md`, the prior audit's dispositions and the four queues so that deliberate design
(bit-identity, pinned reduction widths, frozen PTX, per-machine fixtures, "measure, don't assume") was
not misread as defect. Every finding was confirmed by reading the lines it cites; the Critical and
gate items were then re-verified by hand from the source. Findings reached independently by two or
three reviewers are marked, and count once.

**Limitations:** static only — the review sandbox has go1.24.7 against a `go 1.27.0` requirement, no
module proxy and no GPU, so nothing was built, run, fuzzed or raced. The audit copy also excluded
`*.safetensors`, `*_golden.json` and `docs/assets/`, which produced two false claims (that
`testdata/lfm2-tiny` and the tokenizer goldens were uncommitted); both were checked against the Mac
and corrected before this was written. aikit internals were not readable; anything that depends on
them is marked aikit-side.

**Relationship to the 2026-08-05 audit:** nothing closed there was found reopened wholesale. What
recurs is the *shape* of three of its themes — recurrent state with no lifecycle (now for a fourth
family), errors dropped at the GPU boundary (a handful of new sites), and gates that cannot fail (six
new instances, four of them in the Metal release ritual). The tree grew ~60k lines and ~400 commits
in the four weeks between; the concentration of defects is in that new code, and in the places where
a fix landed on one backend or one route and not its siblings.

---

## Summary

| class | count | of which reached independently by ≥2 reviewers |
|---|---:|---:|
| Critical (C) | 12 | 2 |
| Gates that cannot fail (G) | 10 | 2 |
| Major (M) | 35 | 3 |
| Minor (N) | 42 (many multi-item) | — |
| Performance observations (P) | 22 | — |
| Landscape + improvement proposals (L) | 19 | — |

**Headline.** The numerics of the fresh default-ON work are sound: P18 expert-major MoE prefill is
bit-identical by construction, the P19 fused kernel and the A3 head fan-out are correct and their
gates can fail, and the recurrent lifecycle fixes from August still hold for Mamba-2 and DeltaNet.
The serious defects are concentrated in four places:

1. **LFM2 (2026-08-31) fell through seven hand-maintained family lists** — `canBatchN`, the
   `TruncateTo`/`reconcile` recurrent guards, `specRollbackSafe`, `Snapshot`, the `.giw` writer, the
   capture allow-lists and the layer pager. The first of those crashes the server on any prompt of
   two or more tokens (C-01); the rest are the August C-01/C-05/R3 defects reproduced for a new state
   kind (C-02, C-03). The family's parity gate is green because it drives the forward one token at a
   time.
2. **Two new batched-prefill defects on warm sessions**: the attention tile is recomputed per layer
   from that layer's key count while the worker pool was sized from the global one, which panics on a
   sliding-window model after the ring wraps (C-04); and a cancelled sweep on a wrapped ring leaves
   rows the next turn reads as history, because `reconcile` discards `TruncateTo`'s `exact` (C-05).
3. **The Metal release ritual vouches for tests it never runs**: `gate gpu`'s lifecycle and prefill
   groups print PASS with zero tests executed (G-01), no Metal cell passes `-tags goinfer_testhooks`
   so none of the 64 resident-parity gates are ever compiled by the ritual or by CI (G-02), and the
   "cgo-free" check builds the root binary (G-03). The paged gpt-oss path those gates would cover
   applies expert 0's biases to every expert (C-09).
4. **The serving surface has three unbounded request dimensions and one data race** — the
   decoder-as-embedder has no token bound (C-07), `top_logprobs` is unclamped (C-08), a client that
   stops reading pins the decode worker forever (M-17), and the heartbeat goroutine writes the same
   `ResponseWriter` the tool-call prose deltas write (C-06).

Two more are worth calling out because they are silent: the byte-level pre-tokenizer implements one
regex shape and applies it to every family, so gpt-oss, GPT-2 and the Llama-4/Kimi/DeepSeek GGUFs
tokenize differently from HuggingFace on digits, contractions and combining marks (C-10) — every
real-model gate uses a prompt with none of those; and `ResidentWeightBytes` omits whole tensor
classes, so the new Metal memory-fit guard refuses the paged 35B-A3B that fits and admits the
stacked 26B that does not (M-01, M-02).

On performance: the P19 default-ON flip allocates a fresh fused scratch (including an `hd×nKeys`
block) per pool slot, per layer, per *decoded token*, on a path that can never use it (M-03) — up to
~1.4 GB of zero-filled allocation per token at 16k context on a 1.5B; and the field has moved the bar
for the 8 GB CUDA MoE case: FreeToken's 39.3 tok/s and llama.cpp's 27–41 tok/s on 8 GB laptop cards
both come from executing missing experts on the CPU from host RAM rather than streaming them over PCIe
(L-01), which is the repo's own Lead 5 in `docs/task-freetoken-techniques.md` with the field's numbers
now behind it.

---

## §0 — Cross-cutting themes

1. **One predicate, seven consumers.** `canBatchN`, `specRollbackSafe`, `TruncateTo`'s recurrent
   guard, `Session.reconcile`, `Snapshot`, `ForwardCapture`/`ForwardSubCapture`/`HiddenLast` and
   `newLayerPager` each hand-list the families that have their own forward or non-positional state.
   LFM2 is the fourth family to need all of them and the first to miss six. The August lesson
   recorded in `CLAUDE.md` for descriptor fields ("check at the single chokepoint every family passes
   through") applies to lifecycle predicates too: derive `ownForward()` and `recurrentState()` once.
2. **A fix on one backend, one route or one converter is not a fix.** C-15's canonical f16
   rounding reached `cuda/` but not `gpu/` (N-04); d9829ce's bias-by-slot fix reached CUDA and the
   same bug ships on Metal (C-09); G-01's tagged parity group was built for CUDA and not Metal (G-02);
   M25's segment-rendering reached the text route and not vision or tools (M-22); C-21's input bound
   reached the aikit encoder and not the decoder embedder (C-07); R-11's error shaping reached the
   OpenAI decoder and not the Anthropic one (N-16); G19's heartbeat reached two of three
   buffer-then-stream sites (M-19).
3. **Gates that skip, count, or pass by construction.** Ten instances (§2). Four sit in the release
   ritual itself.
4. **Guards keyed on the wrong quantity.** `ResidentWeightBytes` is a lower bound that omits the
   dominant terms for three families (M-01); the Metal guard runs before the slot budget it should be
   computing from (M-02); the snapshot header bound (queue item M17) divides by blob-declared dims and multiplies by the
   model's (M-04); `noteIfEmpty` counts a skip as a run (G-01); `whyNoResult` diagnoses from a cell
   list production never passes (N-41).
5. **Default-ON flips shipped ahead of their registry, docs and gates.** `--cpu-fast-attention`,
   P19, P18 and A3 are all default ON; none of their escape hatches is in `docs/env-vars.md`, which
   `docs/api-tiers.md` names as Hard-tier contract (N-42); `docs/server.md` still promises exact
   session reuse and EAGLE prefills with the exact kernel while `Generate` prefills with the fast
   one (M-07).
6. **Per-token allocation in fresh code.** The fused scratch per decoded token (M-03), ~50 MB per
   512-row chunk per layer in P18 (P-03), full-vocab sort per token under `logprobs` (P-13), and
   the two full-vocab float64 vectors per verify position in sampled n-gram speculation (P-14).
7. **Bounds that stop at bytes.** The 1 MiB embed cap, the body cap and the text cap all bound
   bytes; positions, logprob entries and write time are unbounded (C-07, C-08, M-17).

---
## §1 — Critical

### C-01 · LFM2 is admitted to the generic batched prefill, which has no conv mixer — every prompt of ≥2 tokens panics *(three reviewers, independently)*

- **Where:** `decoder/forwardn.go:106` (`canBatchN`), `decoder/forwardn.go:1185-1179`
  (`prefillLogits`), `decoder/forwardn.go:418-401` and `decoder/forwardn.go:439-423` (the generic
  layer loop), `decoder/weights.go:694-702` (conv layers load no q/k/v/o and no QK-norm),
  `decoder/registry.go:1351` (`QKNorm: true`), `decoder/rmsnorm.go:32`, `decoder/model.go:807-809`
  (no `recover` in the `Generate` goroutine).
- **Code** (as it stood at the audit; the line references point at the FIXED code, since the
  citation lint keys on live lines):
  ```go
  // decoder/forwardn.go:106 — every own-forward family EXCEPT lfm2 is excluded
  return K > 1 && m.w.Embed.Rows() != 0 && !a.NonGatedMLP && !a.LearnedPosEmbed && a.gemma4 == nil && a.qwen35 == nil && a.granite == nil && a.nemotron == nil && a.mla == nil && a.llama4 == nil && a.gptoss == nil
  // decoder/rmsnorm.go:32 — lw.QNorm is nil on a conv layer
  row[i] = (v * inv) * weight[i]
  ```
- **Mechanism:** `runLayers` (sequential) dispatches LFM2 to `runLayersLFM2`, but `prefillLogits`
  asks `canBatchN` first, and `forwardn.go` contains no reference to `lfm2`, `shortConv` or
  `isConvLayer`. Every 2+-token prompt therefore runs the dense attention stack over the 22 conv
  layers, whose `QProj/KProj/VProj/OProj/QNorm/KNorm` were never loaded. `rmsNorm(qi, nil, …)`
  indexes a nil slice at the first conv layer (layer 0 in LFM2.5) — an unrecovered panic inside the
  `Generate` goroutine, i.e. the process exits; `net/http`'s handler recover does not reach a
  sibling goroutine. If aikit's `MatmulBT` on a zero-value `WeightMat` were to run instead of
  panic, the conv history would never be pushed and the output would be garbage. The same batched
  path backs `forwardN` (every speculative verify) and `NewCPUBlockSpec`.
- **Why the gate is green:** `TestLFM2_textParity` (`decoder/lfm2_test.go:178-182`) drives
  `m.forward` one token at a time and never touches `prefillLogits`; the HF differencing during
  bring-up went through the same sequential forward. `PrefillPath()` (`decoder/residency.go:739`)
  reports "batched shape" for LFM2 at serve startup — the same omission, published as a fact.
- **Trigger:** `serve --model <any LFM2/LFM2.5 checkpoint>`, first chat request. Or
  `Model.Generate(ctx, []int{a, b}, …)`.
- **Fix:** `a.lfm2 == nil` in `canBatchN` (the family's scoping doc, `docs/scoping-lfm2.md:46`,
  already said "no batched prefill"); add a `Generate` test on the committed `testdata/lfm2-tiny`
  fixture with the golden's multi-token prompt. The durable fix is §0 theme 1.
- **Confidence:** high. **Known?:** new (7098769, 2026-08-31).
- **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02.** Reproduced first, on the committed `testdata/lfm2-tiny` with
  a 4-token prompt: `index out of range [0] with length 0` at `decoder/rmsnorm.go:32`, through
  `prefillLogits` → `forwardLayersN` → `runLayersFromEmbedN`, with `PrefillPath()` reporting
  "batched shape" for the same model.

  Fixed as §0 theme 1 rather than as `a.lfm2 == nil`: `ownForwards` is now ONE table carrying each
  family's dispatch function, and `runLayers` and `canBatchN` both read it, so "runLayers dispatches
  here" and "the batched path must not touch this" are the same fact. A family added to the table
  gets the exclusion for free.

  The regression test asserts the two paths AGREE, not that the crash stopped: `prefillLogits`
  documents itself as bit-identical to the sequential prefill, and a fix that stopped the panic by
  taking some third path would satisfy the weaker claim. A source scan
  (`TestOwnForward_tableNamesEveryFamilyForward`) fails if a `runLayersXxx` is written that the table
  does not name — the check that notices a SECOND list being born, which is what this defect was.

### C-02 · LFM2's conv window is recurrent state that none of the lifecycle plumbing knows about *(three reviewers)*

- **Where:** `decoder/kvcache.go:458-463` (the guard), `decoder/kvcache.go:408-416`
  (`resetRecurrent` *does* clear `c.conv`, but is only reached through that guard),
  `decoder/session.go:98` (`reconcile`), `decoder/forwardn.go:125` (`specRollbackSafe`),
  `decoder/kvsnapshot.go:71` (`Snapshot`'s refusal list), `decoder/forward_lfm2.go:111-147` (no
  pos-0 reset of its own), `decoder/model.go:708` and `decoder/model.go:738`
  (`ForwardCapture`/`ForwardSubCapture` allow-lists), `decoder/embed.go:43`,
  `decoder/layerpaging.go:64`.
- **Code** (as it stood at the audit; the line references point at the FIXED code):
  ```go
  // decoder/kvcache.go:458-463 — only mamba/delta reset on 0 or report inexact
  if c.mamba != nil || c.delta != nil {
      if pos == 0 { c.resetRecurrent() } else if pos < c.pos { exact = false }
  }
  // decoder/session.go:98
  if rolledBack && (s.cache.mamba != nil || s.cache.delta != nil) {
  // decoder/forwardn.go:125
  if a.granite != nil || a.nemotron != nil || a.qwen35 != nil { return false }
  // decoder/kvsnapshot.go:71
  if c.delta != nil || len(c.mamba) > 0 || len(c.mlaLatent) > 0 { return nil }
  ```
- **Mechanism:** `shortConvState.convWin` is mutated in place per token exactly like the Mamba-2
  and DeltaNet windows, and is absent from every predicate that treats those as non-positional.
  (a) `TruncateTo(0)` — `Session.Reset`, `sessionLRU.fresh()` — never clears it, so conversation
  B's first K−1 tokens at every conv layer convolve over conversation A's last `Bx` vectors: the
  cross-conversation leak audit C-01 closed for Mamba/DeltaNet. (b) A partial rewind reports
  `exact = true`, so `rewindForReuse` warm-reuses a prefix whose windows still hold the dropped
  positions; the attention layers then append K/V computed from that residual, so the corruption
  is permanent for the session. (c) `specRollbackSafe` admits LFM2 (no sliding window), so all six
  speculative entry points would "roll back" a rejected draft by a `TruncateTo` that touches only
  K/V. (d) `Snapshot` serializes an LFM2 cache; `LoadSession` rebuilds it via `NewCache(pos)` with
  empty windows and the session comes back "warm" — the C-05 shape, through `-session-dir` and
  `-kv-idle-demote`. (e) `ForwardCapture` returns nil rows for LFM2 (`runLayersLFM2` never calls
  `captureResidual`), the outcome `decoder/model.go:703-700` promises to fail loudly on; EAGLE's `fuseAt`
  would panic on them.
- **Trigger:** today everything multi-token dies at C-01 first; (a) and (b) are reachable now on the
  server's session path with a one-token divergent suffix (the sequential path). The rest goes
  live the moment C-01 is fixed.
- **Fix:** `c.conv != nil` in the three guards and in `Snapshot`'s refusal (or persist the window —
  it is `(K−1)×convDim` floats per conv layer, cheaper than one KV row); `a.lfm2 != nil` in
  `specRollbackSafe`, the two capture lists, `HiddenLast` and the pager exclusion; a conv-only case
  in `TestTruncateTo_resetsRecurrent` (`decoder/kvcache_recurrent_test.go:13-14` sets `mamba` and
  `delta` together, so a guard that checks either stays green and a third kind is invisible) and
  `"lfm2"` in `TestSpecRollbackSafetyGuard` (`decoder/spec_ngram_test.go:83-91`).
- **Confidence:** high. **Known?:** new — C-01/C-05/R-14 class, closed for two state kinds only.
- **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02.** `KVCache.hasRecurrentState()` (mamba | delta | conv) is read
  by `TruncateTo`, `Snapshot` and `Session.reconcile`; the `ownForwards` table's `Recurrent` bit is
  read by `specRollbackSafe`; its `Captures` bit by `ForwardCapture`; and `ForwardSubCapture`,
  `HiddenLast` and `newLayerPager` refuse every own-forward family outright. Seven hand-written
  lists, none now.

  Worth recording: `resetRecurrent()` ALREADY cleared `c.conv` and was simply unreachable, because
  the guard that calls it named only mamba and delta. The fix was one word in a predicate; finding
  it was not.

  `HiddenLast`'s list had drifted by TWO, not one — **gpt-oss was missing from it as well**, so that
  seam was reachable for gpt-oss too. It refuses both now, which is the contract its own doc comment
  states.

  The new test uses a cache carrying ONLY `conv`, because `TestTruncateTo_resetsRecurrent` sets
  `mamba` and `delta` together — a guard checking either stays green, which is exactly why a third
  kind was invisible to it. `TestOwnForward_recurrentBitMatchesTheCacheKinds` pins the arch-side and
  cache-side views against each other, since a family recurrent in one sense and not the other is
  this same defect with the halves swapped.

### C-03 · LFM2 is serialized without its conv weights: a CRC-valid `.giw` that loads clean and nil-derefs at the first forward (regression of R3)

- **Where:** `decoder/serialize.go:993-1025` (writer), `decoder/serialize.go:1360-1403` (reader),
  `decoder/serialize.go:113-120` (`canSerialize` returns nil for every family since v6),
  `decoder/weights.go:96` (`shortConv *shortConvWeights`), `decoder/forward_lfm2.go:126` and
  `decoder/forward_lfm2.go:32`, `internal/prequant/prequant.go:128-173` (`selfCheck` is `Load`
  only), `decoder/serialize_census_test.go:135-146`.
- **Code:** `grep shortConv decoder/serialize.go` → 0 matches; the v6 tail writes GProj, AttnSinks,
  expert biases, `mla`, `mamba` and nothing for `shortConv`.
- **Mechanism:** `cmd/prequant -o x.giw <lfm2-dir>` loads the model, serializes every field except
  `shortConv`, appends a valid CRC, and `selfCheck` passes because it only loads. Serving the
  bundle, the first token reaches conv layer 0 with `lw.shortConv == nil` → nil-pointer panic in the
  decode goroutine. This is the C2/R3 class (`docs/queue-release.md:44` records it closed by v6:
  "every registered family is representable, `canSerialize` is EMPTY"). The recurrence guard,
  `TestSerializeCensus_noSilentFieldDrop`, reflects over `LayerWeights` and would catch this — but
  its fixture list is hand-maintained and stops at 21 paths; `../testdata/lfm2-tiny` is committed
  (ed112b0) and not on it. The census test's own preamble says the previous guard failed because
  it "asks only 'is this family on the list?'" — the replacement still asks a list.
- **Trigger:** any LFM2 safetensors checkpoint → `cmd/prequant` → `serve --model <that .giw>`.
- **Fix:** a v8 tail with `shortConv` (presence byte + three f32 tensors, the mamba block's shape),
  `validateShapes` entries for its three lengths, `canSerialize` returning an error for
  `arch.lfm2 != nil` until then; and a registry-driven census (every `model_type` in `registry`
  maps to a fixture or an explicit skip) or a reflection-built synthetic `LayerWeights` with every
  field populated, round-tripped without a fixture. Also exclude lfm2 in `newLayerPager`
  (`decoder/layerpaging.go:64`) — `runLayersLFM2` never calls `enterLayer` (the 2026-08-05 audit's N-13 class).
- **Confidence:** high. **Known?:** regression of R3 (closed 2026-08-19).
- **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02.** Reproduced by putting the committed fixture into the census:
  `SILENT DROP: layer 0 field shortConv was populated (1) and is empty after a .giw round-trip`, on
  layers 0/2/3, with the round-tripped model returning no tokens at all.

  A v8 tail (presence byte + `inProj`/`convW`/`outProj`, the v6 Mamba-2 block's shape) written
  unconditionally, plus a `validateShapes` refusal for an lfm2 conv layer with no mixer. Both halves
  are needed: the tail makes a correct bundle round-trip, and the refusal makes every EXISTING
  (pre-v8) bundle fail at load instead of at the first forward. `canSerialize` stays empty — the
  family is representable now, which is the better answer than refusing it.

  **The guard was itself a hand-maintained list, which is the finding.** The census's own preamble
  says the guard it replaced failed because it "asks only 'is this family on the list?'" — and it
  asked a list too, 21 paths, and `../testdata/lfm2-tiny` had been committed since ed112b0 without
  being on it. `TestSerializeCensus_everyFixtureIsListedOrExcluded` now requires every committed
  model fixture to be censused or excluded with a written reason; it found **eleven** the census had
  never mentioned, all eleven are censused now (30 fixtures, 0.19 s), and the five exclusions are
  measured cost, not judgement (gpt2 529 MB, gemma4-dense-scaled 449 MB, mellum-mellum2-slice 4.0 GB
  — still running after 90 s where all 30 listed together take 0.19 s — plus a vision encoder and a
  config-only fixture that `Load` refuses). Glob discovery was tried first and **rejected on
  measurement**: 152 s against 0.19 s, because it dragged those three in.

### C-04 · Batched prefill sizes its attention scratch from the global key count but tiles from the per-layer one — a warm sliding-window session panics

- **Where:** `decoder/scratch.go:328-353` (`newHeadWorkerPool`), `decoder/scratch.go:194-204`
  (`attnRowTile`), `decoder/forwardn.go:233`, `decoder/forwardn.go:337`, `decoder/forwardn.go:462`,
  `decoder/forwardn.go:733`, `decoder/forwardn.go:784-771`; `decoder/kvcache.go:515-552`
  (`batchReadLocal`).
- **Code** (as it stood at the audit; the line references point at the FIXED code):
  ```go
  // decoder/forwardn.go:233 and :298 — the pool is sized ONCE from the global key count
  maxKeys := startPos + K
  attnPool := newHeadWorkerPool(prefillAttnWorkers(K, maxKeys, hd, arch.maxHeads()), K, maxKeys, hd)
  // decoder/scratch.go:236-245 — slot buffers sized for t = attnRowTile(K, maxKeys) rows
  t := attnRowTile(K, nKeys)
  qh: make([]float32, t*hd), ... scores: make([]float32, t*nKeys), ch: make([]float32, t*hd)
  // decoder/forwardn.go:733 and :745-751 — but each call tiles from ITS OWN key count
  nKeys := len(keys) / kvDim
  tile := attnRowTile(K, nKeys)
  for t0 := 0; t0 < K; t0 += tile {
      kt := min(tile, K-t0)
      for i := range kt { b := (t0+i)*qDim + qhead*hd; copy(qh[i*hd:i*hd+hd], q[b:b+hd]) }
  // decoder/kvcache.go:521-522 and :533 — a local layer's assembled window is SHORTER than maxKeys once startPos ≥ W
  base = max(startPos-r.w+1, 0); hist := startPos - base; ... return base, hist + K
  ```
- **Mechanism:** `attnRowTile(K, n) = min(K, 2,097,152/n)`. For a ring layer with `startPos ≥ W`,
  `nKeys = W−1+K < maxKeys`, so the per-layer tile is *larger* than the slot was sized for. Whenever
  `K·maxKeys > 2,097,152` (so `t < K`) the gather at `decoder/forwardn.go:789` slices `qh` past its
  length — `panic: slice bounds out of range`, in a worker goroutine on the fan-out arm and in the
  `Generate` goroutine on the serial one; neither is recovered. Independent of
  `--cpu-fast-attention` (the acc64 arm uses the same `qh`). A cold prefill always has
  `nKeys == maxKeys`, which is why every golden and benchmark misses it.
- **Trigger:** `serve --kv-sessions 4` with Gemma-3-1b (W=512, hd=256): turn 1 leaves the session at
  600 tokens, turn 2 appends 2048. `maxKeys=2648 → t=791`; local layers `nKeys=2559 → tile=819`;
  `qh[791*256:792*256]` on a 791·256 slice. Gemma-3-4b (W=1024): a 1200-token session + an
  1800-token suffix. Mistral-7B (W=4096) needs an 8k+ session and a 1k+ suffix. Any windowed family
  through `Session.Generate` or n-gram speculation (both prefill via `prefillLogits` →
  `forwardLayersN` with `startPos = cache.Pos()`).
- **Fix:** make the tile a property of the pool — compute it once in `newHeadWorkerPool` /
  `headWorkerPool`, store it on `headWorkerScratch`, and have `attendOneHead` read it (or clamp
  `tile = min(attnRowTile(K, nKeys), len(ws.qh)/hd)`). Gate: `forwardLayersN` on a ring cache with
  `startPos ≥ W` and `K·(startPos+K) > 2Mi`, tiny `hd` keeps it cheap and asset-free.
- **Confidence:** high. **Known?:** new (G20 tiling landed 2026-08-26).
- **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02. Reproduced first, asset-free and sub-second: a W=512 ring at startPos=600 with K=2048 gives `slice bounds out of range [:3168] with capacity 3164` at the Q gather. `attendTileFor` clamps the tile to what the slot holds — the invariant, not a workaround: the tile is a memory-locality choice and the slot's capacity is the binding constraint, so the slot decides, and clamping qh satisfies every other buffer by construction. The gate asserts the clamped tile is BIT-IDENTICAL to an unclamped pool sized from the layer's own key count, not merely that the panic stopped, and fails its own premise first if the two pools happen to choose the same tile.**

### C-05 · A cancelled batched prefill on a wrapped ring leaves stale K/V that the next warm turn reads as history — `reconcile` discards `TruncateTo`'s `exact`

- **Where:** `decoder/forwardn.go:393-376` (per-layer cancel), `decoder/forwardn.go:461-445`
  (`commitBatch` per layer, before the sweep completes), `decoder/forwardn.go:661`
  (`advanceTo` only at the end); `decoder/kvcache.go:235-242` (`ring.truncate`),
  `decoder/kvcache.go:470-480`; `decoder/session.go:86-105` (the discard is at `:104`),
  `decoder/session.go:73-80`; `internal/serveapp/openai.go:913-915` (`genErr` treats
  `context.Canceled` as a clean end), `internal/serveapp/sessions.go:136-157`.
- **Code:**
  ```go
  // decoder/kvcache.go:239 — a rewind of >1 position on a wrapped ring is INEXACT
  exact := r.count <= r.w || p >= r.count-1
  // decoder/session.go:98-104 — only recurrent families reset on rollback; the ring answer is thrown away
  if rolledBack && (s.cache.mamba != nil || s.cache.delta != nil) { s.cache.TruncateTo(0); s.tokens = nil; return }
  s.tokens = seq
  s.cache.TruncateTo(len(seq))
  ```
- **Mechanism:** G18 (3a16a4b, 2026-08-25) made the sweep abortable per layer. Layers below the
  abort point have already `commitBatch`'d positions `[P, P+K)` into slots `(P..P+K−1) % W`,
  overwriting the live window rows `[P−W, P−W+K)`. `reconcile` sees `cache.Pos()==P < len(seq)`,
  truncates to `P` — `ring.truncate` returns `false` (wrapped, `K>1`) and `TruncateTo` returns
  `exact=false`, which line 104 ignores. `s.tokens` is now the prefix, so the next request that
  extends it is matched by `sessionLRU.acquire` → `rewindForReuse` → `TruncateTo(P)` is a no-op
  (`p >= r.count`) → reported exact → the suffix prefills at `startPos=P` and `batchReadLocal` reads
  slots holding the aborted turn's rows (RoPE'd at positions `P..`) as history for positions
  `P−W+1..`. Silently wrong attention on every local layer below the abort point; plausible text.
- **Trigger:** `serve --kv-sessions 4`, Gemma-3-4b (W=1024, layers 0-4 local): turn 1 = 1500
  tokens; turn 2 appends 800 and the client disconnects mid-prefill (`drive`'s ctx cancelled,
  `genErr` says clean); turn 3 continues the same conversation. Also reachable via `--spec ngram`.
- **Fix:** consume the bool in `reconcile`: `if !s.cache.TruncateTo(len(seq)) { s.cache.TruncateTo(0); s.tokens = nil }`
  (the shape `rewindForReuse` already has). Rings cannot restore evicted rows, so cold reset is the
  only exact answer.
- **Confidence:** high on the mechanism; medium-high that serve keeps the session (nothing evicts
  on cancel). **Known?:** new — the 2026-08-05 audit's C-04 disposition ("no inexact rewind reaches the rollback
  sites") predates G18 making a mid-sweep abort reachable; post-audit R-14 fixed the recurrent half
  only.
- **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02. `reconcile` consumes the bool, with the shape `rewindForReuse` ten lines above always had. Mutation-checked in BOTH directions, which matters more than usual here: discarding the bool again fails the cancelled-sweep gate, and going cold UNCONDITIONALLY fails a second gate pinning that an exact rewind stays warm — without which a fix that threw every session's KV reuse away would have passed.**

### C-06 · Two goroutines write the same `http.ResponseWriter` on the incremental tool-call stream

- **Where:** `internal/serveapp/tools.go:116`, `internal/serveapp/tools.go:125-134`,
  `internal/serveapp/tools.go:129`; `internal/serveapp/helpers.go:208-228` (the heartbeat
  goroutine), `internal/serveapp/helpers.go:172-176` (`sseSend`).
- **Code:**
  ```go
  stopBeat = sseHeartbeat(w, f)                    // internal/serveapp/tools.go:116 — goroutine writes ": ping" to w every 10s
  finish, nComp, _, _, gerr := lm.drive(r.Context(), gr, func(t string) {
      ...
      if out := prose.Push(t); out != "" {
          sseSend(w, f, chatChunk(...))            // internal/serveapp/tools.go:125 — handler goroutine writes w
      }
  })
  if stopBeat != nil { stopBeat() }                // internal/serveapp/tools.go:129 — the join happens AFTER drive returns
  ```
- **Mechanism:** G19 started a heartbeat goroutine that owns `w` while the handler is silent; G21
  then made the handler write prose deltas to the same `w` during that window (the comment at
  `internal/serveapp/tools.go:112-116` says heartbeats "still run" on incremental families — deliberate).
  `net/http`'s `response`/`bufio.Writer` is not safe for concurrent `Write`/`Flush`. Outcomes range
  from `: ping\n\n` spliced into a `data: {...}` line (the client drops or mis-parses the chunk) to
  a `bufio` slice-bounds panic, which in the heartbeat goroutine is outside `net/http`'s recover and
  kills the process. `sseHeartbeat`'s own doc (`internal/serveapp/helpers.go:217-220`) promises "no risk of
  interleaving two writers"; that holds on the non-incremental paths only, where the callback
  appends to a builder.
- **Trigger:** any ChatML/Mellum2/Gemma-4 model, `stream:true` with `tools` and `tool_choice`
  auto, a generation that produces prose and lasts ≥10 s — the default agent shape on a CPU
  backend. `internal/serveapp/heartbeat_test.go:161-171` states its model "never emits prose", so the gate never
  exercises the overlap and `-race` sees nothing.
- **Fix:** one `sync.Mutex` per SSE response wrapping `sseSend`/`sseEvent`/heartbeat writes, or
  stop the heartbeat before the first prose delta and restart it at the opener.
- **Confidence:** high. **Known?:** new (G19/G21 closed; the interaction was not considered).
- **DISPOSITION — **FIXED 2026-09-02.** An `sseWriter` owns each SSE response: every frame in the package, both protocols, goes through one `frame()` under a mutex. The existing gate could not see the overlap — heartbeat_test.go's model "never emits prose" — so a new test drives it directly and is mutation-checked: removing the mutex produces DATA RACE under `-race`.**

### C-07 · The decoder-as-embedder has no position bound: one in-bounds `/v1/embeddings` input runs a ~500k-position sequential O(n²) forward under the embed mutex (the 2026-08-05 audit's C-21, closed incompletely)

- **Where:** `internal/serveapp/decoder_embedder.go:84` (`maxTokens: 0`),
  `internal/serveapp/decoder_embedder.go:226-255` (no truncation when 0; shifted from `:193-222` by
  the 2026-09-03 R-07 double-tokenize fix, which added the `EncodeBatchCounted`/`CountTokens`
  methods above this function),
  `internal/serveapp/decoder_embedder.go:211-220` (shifted from `:182-188`) → `decoder/embed.go:85-106`
  (`NewCache(len(ids))`, one `runLayers` per token, no `ctx`, no `MaxPositions` check);
  `internal/serveapp/embeddings.go:34-35` (`maxEmbedInputBytes = 1 << 20`).
- **Mechanism:** C-21 capped a single input at 1 MiB of *bytes*. The embedder never caps *tokens*
  against `MaxPositions`, and `HiddenLast` preallocates KV for `len(ids)` positions and runs a
  sequential per-token forward with no context. 1 MiB of short words is ~500k tokens: for
  Qwen3-Embedding-0.6B (28 layers, kvDim 1024) that is ~114 GB of KV as the cache fills and
  attention over up to 500k keys per token — hours of one pinned core, during which every other
  `/v1/embeddings` request blocks, until the process OOM-kills. Uncancellable, and un-queued (per
  C-21's own note). Even a legitimate 200 KB document (~45k tokens) silently exceeds
  Qwen3-Embedding's 40k window and returns an embedding pooled from out-of-range RoPE positions —
  plausible, wrong. The C-21 comment (`internal/serveapp/embeddings.go:31-32`) acknowledges "one multi-MiB string
  prefills ~1M positions" and answers with a byte cap that still admits ~250k–1M positions.
- **Trigger:** `POST /v1/embeddings {"input": "<1 MiB of short words>"}` against
  `-embed-model <qwen3-embedding gguf>`.
- **Fix:** `maxTokens = m.Config().MaxPositions` in `loadDecoderEmbedder` (HF's `truncation=True`
  semantics — `tokenize` already truncates-before-append), a `contextLengthError` in `HiddenLast`,
  and route `HiddenLast` through the batched prefill (P-17).
- **Confidence:** high. **Known?:** incomplete closure of the 2026-08-05 audit's C-21 (recorded fixed in
  `docs/queue-engineering.md:1154`). The aikit-encoder path truncates correctly.
- **DISPOSITION — **FIXED 2026-09-02.** Two halves, because they want different answers: the serving embedder TRUNCATES to `m.Config().MaxPositions` (HF's `truncation=True`, what the aikit encoder path already did), and `HiddenLast` REFUSES, stated where the cost is incurred so another caller cannot reintroduce it. I keyed that guard on `Architecture.MaxPositions` first — the GPT-2 learned-position table, 0 for every RoPE family — so it would have been silently inert almost everywhere; the test pins the distinction by asserting its own fixture has that field at 0.**

### C-08 · `top_logprobs` is unbounded — one request allocates `max_tokens × top_logprobs` entries and sorts the full vocabulary per token

- **Where:** `internal/serveapp/openai.go:670` (`TopLogprobs: deref(sm.TopLogprobs, 0)`, no range
  check), `internal/serveapp/openai.go:1154-1163` (one `map[string]any` per entry for the body),
  `decoder/sampler.go:376-399` (full-vocab `sort.Slice` per token), `decoder/model.go:1134-1067`.
- **Mechanism:** `prepare` validates temperature/top_p/max_tokens and passes `top_logprobs`
  through. `logprobs:true, top_logprobs:150000, max_tokens:4096` on a 152k-vocab model retains
  4096 × 150k `TokenLogprob` (~9.8 GB) and then materializes a map per entry before writing a byte —
  a fatal Go OOM. `top_logprobs: 20000, max_tokens: 2000` reaches ~6 GB. Independently, the per-token
  `sort.Slice` over V is O(V log V) (P-13).
- **Trigger:** one non-streaming chat request (streaming + logprobs is already 400).
- **Fix:** reject `top_logprobs` outside `[0, 20]` (OpenAI's contract) in `prepare`; use the
  k-bounded heap (`topKByLogit`, `decoder/sampler.go:655`) that already exists.
- **Confidence:** high. **Known?:** new.
- **DISPOSITION — **FIXED 2026-09-02.** Clamped to OpenAI's documented `[0,20]` in `prepare`, beside every other sampling field's validation. The gate pins both directions: a fix that clamped everything would pass the rejection half alone.**

### C-09 · Paged gpt-oss on Metal applies expert 0's biases to every routed expert

- **Where:** `metal/moe.go:676-678` (paged dispatch), `metal/moe.go:242` and `metal/moe.go:209`
  (kernel bias addressing), `decoder/residency.go:840-835` and `decoder/residency.go:863-857`
  (bias tables are expert-major, indexed by expert id).
- **Code:**
  ```go
  // AS SHIPPED, the paged dispatch — now `metal/moe.go:676-678`, where the bias index reads
  // `mo.biasIdx()`. idxZeros stands in for rIdx so the weight GEMVs read row 0 of a one-expert slot…
  e.Dispatch(mo.pActGptOss, 256, 256, r.gu, r.gu.At(mo.inter*4), r.dq, r.dSc, mo.uInter,
      ml.expGuBias, mo.idxZeros, mo.uSlot[j], mo.uHasBias, mo.uAlpha, mo.uLimit)
  e.DispatchTG(mo.pDownWaccBias, r.H*32, 256, mo.inter*2, s.dW, s.dS, r.dq, r.dSc, r.x, mo.uInter, mo.idxZeros, mo.rWgt, mo.uSlot[j], r.uH, ml.expDBias)
  ```
  ```c
  // AS SHIPPED — one `idx` in both roles. The down-projection line is now `metal/moe.go:209` and
  // reads `bias[bidx[slot]*…]`; quoted here as the defect, not as current source.
  // swiglu_quant_gptoss                       // gemv_w4a8_moe_wacc_bias
  uint biasOff = hasBias != 0u ? idx[slot]*2u*I : 0u;
  if (lane==0) out[row] += wgt[slot]*(acc*asc[0] + bias[idx[slot]*rowsPerExpert + row]);
  ```
- **Mechanism:** the paged path substitutes `idxZeros` for `rIdx` so the reused kernels read weight
  row 0 of a single-expert slot (correct), but the same `idx` indexes `ml.expGuBias` /
  `ml.expDBias`, which stay the stacked all-expert tables. With `idx[slot]==0` every selected expert
  gets expert 0's gate‖up bias and down bias. Finite, plausible, wrong — the class CUDA fixed in
  d9829ce ("indexed its per-expert bias table by SLOT id under expert caching"), on Metal indexed by
  a constant zero.
- **Trigger:** gpt-oss-20b/120b, `--backend metal`, `GOINFER_METAL_MOE_SLOTS=N` with
  `topK ≤ N < nE`, on a Mac the memory guard admits (or with `GOINFER_NO_RESIDENT_MEM_GUARD=1`).
  `TestMoEPaging_matchesNonPaged` uses the qwen3_5_moe fixture; the gpt-oss parity tests never set
  the slots var.
- **Fix:** pass `mo.rIdx` to `swiglu_quant_gptoss` (its `idx` only feeds the bias offset); give
  `gemv_w4a8_moe_wacc_bias` a second index buffer for the bias row, or stage each expert's two bias
  rows into its slot with the weights so the paged and stacked paths index identically. Add a paged
  arm to the gpt-oss resident parity test.
- **Confidence:** high. **Known?:** new.
- **DISPOSITION — **FIXED 2026-09-02.** `gemv_w4a8_moe_wacc_bias` takes a second index buffer for
  the bias row (`bidx`), and the four bias-consuming dispatches route through a new
  `moeResident.biasIdx()` — the naming discipline `cuda/resident.go` already uses for this exact
  class (`expIdx` / `expertBiasIdx`, added when the same defect was caught on CUDA in d9829ce).
  Adopting the CUDA shape rather than inventing one is the point: audit §0 theme 2 is that a fix
  on one backend is not a fix, and the converse is that the backend which already solved it owns
  the vocabulary.

  **A symmetric `weightIdx()` was written and deliberately removed**, and that is the more useful
  finding. Keying it on `mo.paged` is wrong, because paging is decided PER LAYER —
  `forwardLogitsMoEPaged` branches on `L.moe.pool != nil`, so a layer without a pool encodes
  through the ordinary path while `mo.paged` is true, and the accessor would have handed it
  `idxZeros` for its weights. The symmetry that makes the pair look complete would have
  reintroduced the same silent-plausible-logits failure one level over. The weight index stays
  explicit, where which encoder you are in settles it.

  **Three gates, each mutation-checked, because they fail independently:** a kernel-level paged
  dispatch (weights at slot row 0, bias at expert 2, expert 0's bias a decoy) — reverting `bidx`
  to `idx` gives cosine **-0.332**, maxRel 1760, while the pre-existing non-paged kernel test stays
  green, which is why that test could never have caught this; a `go/ast` guard over `moe.go`
  pinning that every `ml.exp*Bias` argument is immediately followed by `mo.biasIdx()`, counted at
  4 so it cannot silently watch nothing (its first line-wise draft matched the pipeline
  ASSIGNMENTS and half of each wrapped call — a guard on the wrong thing, caught only because the
  count was pinned); and the end-to-end arm the fix text asked for.

  **The end-to-end arm is the one that shows why this was missed.**
  `TestGptOssPaging_matchesNonPaged` drives the tiny gpt-oss fixture (nE=4, topK=2, so slots=2
  evicts every token) and requires EXACT logit equality against the non-paged build. Restoring the
  shipped defect fails it at both slot counts — tok 0 logit 0 reads 0.0350803 paged against
  0.0185128 resident, a factor of 1.9 rather than noise — **while `TestMoEPaging_matchesNonPaged`
  passes unchanged**, because its qwen3_5_moe fixture has no per-expert biases and cannot express
  the difference. The existing paging gate was green on broken code for a structural reason, not
  an oversight in what it asserted.

  Not measured on the real 20B: `TestGptOssResidentParityReal20B` correctly skips on this 16 GB
  machine (11.28 GB checkpoint needs ~16.9 GB with Metal's mmap wiring). The fixture arm is
  bit-exact, which is a stronger bar than that test's cosine floor, but it is a 4-layer hand-built
  shape and does not retire the real-checkpoint run.**

### C-10 · The byte-level pre-tokenizer implements one regex shape and applies it to every family; gpt-oss, GPT-2 and the Llama-4/Kimi/DeepSeek GGUFs tokenize differently from HF, silently

- **Where:** `tokenizer/bytelevel.go:27-31` (only the digit cap and the Digits node are read from
  `pre_tokenizer`), `tokenizer/bytelevel.go:256-269` and `tokenizer/bytelevel.go:281-393`
  (`splitGPT2`, the hand-rolled cl100k/Llama-3 walker), `tokenizer/gguf.go:276-295`
  (`byteLevelKnobs`: four known `pre` names, silent default).
- **Code:**
  ```go
  default: // "gpt-2", "default", "", and unrecognized
      return 1, norm.NFC, false, false, false                          // tokenizer/gguf.go:280-281
  ```
- **Mechanism:** the walker is exactly the cl100k/Llama-3 alternation, parameterised only by
  `\p{N}{1,N}`. The regex in `tokenizer.json` is never compared against that shape, and a GGUF
  `tokenizer.ggml.pre` outside {llama-bpe/llama3/llama-v3, qwen2/qwen2.5/qwen, mellum2} falls to a
  default that is still the cl100k walker with `maxDigits=1`. Families this repo ships and gates
  do not use that regex: **gpt-oss** (o200k_harmony: contractions attach to the word, so `don't` is
  one pre-token where goinfer emits `don` + `'t`; combining marks `\p{M}` are word content; digits
  `{1,3}` — via GGUF the cap becomes 1, so `2024` is four single-digit pre-tokens where HF and
  llama.cpp give `202`,`4`); **GPT-2** (` ?\p{N}+`, ` ?\p{L}+`, no `[\r\n]` clauses: ` 2020` is one
  pre-token in HF, `Ġ 2 0 2 0` here); **Llama-4 / Kimi-K2 / DeepSeek-V3 / GLM4** GGUFs (not in the
  switch → digit cap 1 and the cl100k shape). `ByteLevel{add_prefix_space:true}` is also not
  honoured. Every real-GGUF gate that tokenizes through goinfer uses the prompt "The capital of
  France is" (`decoder/gptoss_real_test.go:61`, `decoder/llama4_real_test.go:52`,
  `decoder/deepseek_real_test.go:210`) — no digit, contraction or mark, so the divergence is
  identically zero in the gate (the `CLAUDE.md` minimal-repro rule). The HF logit oracle
  (`scripts/gptoss_hf_oracle.py`) feeds `prompt_ids` from HF and bypasses the tokenizer.
- **Trigger:** serve a gpt-oss GGUF, send "What happened in 2024? I don't know."; the id stream
  differs from `AutoTokenizer` and from llama.cpp. No error, no log; `count_tokens` and usage are off
  by the same drift.
- **Fix:** canonicalise the `Split` regex at `tokenizer.json` load and reject or warn when it is not
  a shape the walker implements; add an o200k-shaped walker (case-transition + attached contractions
  + `\p{M}` + `[\r\n/]*`), keyed from the regex and from `pre ∈ {gpt-4o, llama4, kimi-k2}`; the real
  GPT-2 alternation for `pre="gpt-2"`; make the `byteLevelKnobs` default an error; a golden per
  byte-level family whose prompts contain digits, contractions, CamelCase and marks.
- **Confidence:** high on mechanism; medium on the exact `pre` strings each converter emits
  (unverifiable here — check with `gguf-dump` on the local assets). **Known?:** new. The
  `tokenizer/doc.go:26-28` id-exactness claim covers Gemma/Qwen3/Llama-3/Mellum2/TinyLlama only.
- **DISPOSITION — **FIXED 2026-09-02, in two parts, with one piece deferred.** The GGUF `pre` strings were MEASURED on this machine's assets rather than left at the audit's medium confidence: gpt-oss is `pre="gpt-4o"` and Qwen3.5 is `pre="qwen35"`, neither in the switch. **qwen35 is not in the audit** and is the quieter one — the default differs from the `qwen2` branch only in NFC being off, so it diverges on exactly the inputs that need normalising.

  `classifySplit` names the alternation from the model's own regex; `PreTokenizerDecline()` reports a shape this build cannot walk and serve prints it at startup. Two walkers landed — o200k and GPT-2's own — each validated against an INDEPENDENT ordered-alternative matcher over 4000 random strings, which caught three bugs no hand-written case would have (including one where the ORACLE was wrong about `\s+(?!\S)` backtracking, and "fixing" the walker to agree would have broken splitGPT2's identical rule).

  The audit's central claim is now asserted rather than repeated: all three alternations agree EXACTLY on "The capital of France is", the prompt every real-GGUF gate uses.

  **The deferred piece is now closed, on evidence.** A GGUF carries no Split regex, so mapping
  `pre` to a walker is the very name-to-shape guess this item is about — unless it is checked. It
  was: `~/models/gpt-oss-20b-hf/tokenizer.json`, the same model as the GGUF, declares a Split regex
  BYTE-IDENTICAL to the o200k pattern `splitO200k` implements (sha256 `2d1b8dc11e89af71` on both it
  and this repo's `reO200k` constant). Verified end to end on the real GGUF: it now selects o200k
  with a 3-digit cap and no normalizer, and `"What happened in 2024? …"` tokenizes `2024` as
  ` `+`202`+`4` — the split this item says HF and llama.cpp give — round-tripping exactly.

  `llama4`, `kimi-k2`, `deepseek-v3` and `glm4` stay unrecognised and keep declining loudly; the
  same evidence chain would settle each, and none of those checkpoints is here.**

### C-11 · `BlockSpec.generate` (`--drafter`) stops only on `config.json` EOS ids — it ignores `SamplingParams.StopIDs` and `generation_config.json`

- **Where:** `decoder/blockspec.go:185-188`, `decoder/blockspec.go:385-390` (the author's belief),
  `decoder/blockspec.go:482-492` (`GenerateStream` never forwards `sp.StopIDs`),
  `decoder/model.go:1210-1141` (`isStop` = `m.eosIDs` ∪ `sp.StopIDs`), `decoder/config.go:1101-1069`
  (`resolveEOSIDs` merges generation_config), `internal/serveapp/openai.go:668`.
- **Code:**
  ```go
  eos := map[int]bool{}
  for _, e := range m.w.Cfg.EOSIDs() {   // config.json only; not m.eosIDs, not sp.StopIDs
      eos[e] = true
  }
  ```
- **Mechanism:** every other speculative loop calls `target.isStop(tok, sp)`; this one rebuilds
  the stop set from `Cfg.EOSIDs()` alone. For the pairing this ships for (Qwen3-4B + DFlash),
  `m.eosIDs` = {151645, 151643} while `Cfg.EOSIDs()` = {151645}; `lm.stopIDs` adds the chat
  template's stops. A `<|endoftext|>` is emitted as a token and generation continues until
  `<|im_end|>` or `max_tokens`; `streamTokens` decodes it into the response. The "lossless by
  construction" contract (`decoder/blockspec.go:179-184`) is broken by the stop set, not the verify.
- **Trigger:** `serve --drafter …` on Qwen3 + `/v1/completions` (raw prompt); any model whose chat
  stop id is not in `config.json`'s `eos_token_id` on `/v1/chat/completions`.
- **Fix:** build `eos` from `m.eosIDs` plus a `StopIDs` field on `BlockSpecOptions` that
  `GenerateStream` fills from `sp.StopIDs` (or call `m.isStop`).
- **Confidence:** high. **Known?:** new.
- **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02. `blockSpecStopSet` builds from `m.eosIDs` — resolveEOSIDs' merge of config.json AND generation_config.json — plus a new `BlockSpecOptions.StopIDs` that `GenerateStream` fills from `sp.StopIDs`. The gate compares this loop's predicate against `Model.isStop` across the id range rather than re-listing what the set should contain: a list would be a second copy of the belief that drifted in the first place.**

### C-12 · The block-spec prompt seed runs the LM head over every prompt row and materializes `M×vocab` logits on device and host; the device buffer is grow-only and leaks its predecessor

- **Where:** `decoder/blockspec.go:230` (`host.PrefillLastNArgmax(embs, 0)`), `decoder/blockspec.go:238`
  (only `ids[len(ids)-1]` is used); `cuda/prefill.go:946-556`, `cuda/prefill.go:961-582`; the same
  grow-and-abandon pattern in `cuda/drafter.go:187-193`, `cuda/drafter.go:298-306`,
  `cuda/drafter.go:618-623`.
- **Code:**
  ```go
  if M > r.logitsBCap {
      r.logitsB = r.af(M * r.vocab)   // old r.logitsB is never released
      r.logitsBCap = M
  }
  ...
  host := make([]float32, M*r.vocab)
  if e := gpu.Download(r.logitsB, host); e != nil {
  ```
- **Mechanism:** for the prompt seed, M = the whole prompt. Qwen3-8B-class vocab 151,936: a
  2048-token prompt allocates 1.24 GB of VRAM for `logitsB`, a 1.24 GB Go slice, a 1.24 GB D2H, a
  head GEMV over 2048 rows and a single-threaded host argmax over 311M floats — to obtain ONE token
  id. `logitsB` persists for the model's lifetime and each larger prompt abandons the previous
  buffer (bounded by `2·ctxCap·vocab·4 B` ≈ 5 GB at the 4096 default). On an 8 GB card a 4096-token
  prompt to a 4B/8B target OOMs inside the executor, and per `cuda/backend.go:617-652` (A13) a
  context driven to refusal and kept in use can afterwards launch kernels that "return SUCCESS and
  execute NOTHING". The drafter's `ctxIn` (`M × nTaps × hidden` f32, ~335 MB at 4096) follows the
  same pattern.
- **Trigger:** `--drafter` with prompts over ~1k tokens (TTFT + GBs held); increasing prompt
  lengths leak; 4k prompts on an 8 GB card OOM.
- **Fix:** a `tailLastArgmax` mode that heads only the last row for the prompt seed (the capture
  comes from the layer loop, not the head); `r.dev.ReleaseBuf` before reallocating `logitsB` and the
  drafter scratch; drop the redundant `gpu.Download(xB, xhost)` at `cuda/prefill.go:893-502`.
- **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02, VALIDATED ON THE GPU 2026-09-02.** `ResidentSeedArgmax` is an OPTIONAL
  interface (the `ResidentCapped` / `ResidentGreedy` pattern), so no backend breaks by lacking it.
  The CUDA side needed no new kernel path: `tailLastLogits` already heads the last row only, and the
  capture the seed actually needs comes from the layer loop rather than the head — one row of logits
  is ~0.6 MB against M × vocab. `logitsB` is released before it grows, and the redundant `xhost`
  download is skipped on both argmax tails.

  **Now run on the GPU.** `TestGenerateBlockSpec_production`, `TestBlockSpecStream` and
  `TestDFlashLoop_lossless` all pass under `-tags 'cuda goinfer_testhooks'` on the 2070S (64 s
  total). The seed path is exercised by every one of them, since `BlockSpec.generate` seeds through
  it.
- **Confidence:** high (mechanism); medium on the OOM/A13 consequence (card- and vocab-dependent).
  **Known?:** new (`docs/spec/08-dspark-dflash.md` measures `PrefillLastNArgmax` only at k ≤ 16).

---
## §2 — Gates that cannot fail

### G-01 · `gate gpu` on a Mac reports PASS for the Metal lifecycle and prefill groups while running zero tests *(two reviewers)*

- **Where:** `cmd/gate/gpu.go:432-438` (the gate unsets `GOINFER_HEAVY_TESTS`),
  `cmd/gate/gpu.go:1162-1172` and `cmd/gate/gpu.go:1196-1201` (the two Metal cells set no `Env`),
  `metal/heavytest_test.go:20-25`, `metal/close_leak_test.go:46`, `metal/close_leak_test.go:46`,
  `metal/prefill_parity_test.go:21`, `metal/prefill_nan_test.go:21`; contrast `cmd/gate/gpu.go:727`
  (`cuda-heavy` sets `GOINFER_HEAVY_TESTS=1` per cell); `cmd/gate/gpu.go:219-222` (`noteIfEmpty`
  fires only when `len(res.final) == 0`; a skip is a result).
- **Mechanism:** all four tests named by `metal-lifecycle` and `metal-prefill` call
  `requireHeavyModel`, which skips unless `GOINFER_HEAVY_TESTS` is set. The gate deliberately
  unsets it and the Metal cells do not set it back. `go test` exits 0 on an all-skip package,
  `cr.RC == 0`, and the group is counted as `ran` and printed PASS with the specific claim text
  ("Close() frees — sawtooth not staircase…"). Both archived logs
  (`docs/measurements/gpu_gate_metal_v0.15.0-prep_65441a3_PASS.log:23-27` and the v0.14.0 one)
  show the PASS lines with none of the `evidence()` lines a run would print. The v0.15.0-prep
  "7 pass" verdict therefore vouches for two groups that never executed; the C5 prefill leak and
  the NaN-at-every-length bug (19ef47d) this group exists for would pass the ritual.
- **Fix:** `Env: {"GOINFER_HEAVY_TESTS": "1"}` on both Metal cells, and `cr.Pass == 0 ⇒ FAIL` for
  every filtered cell (`ranCount` counts skips today; a skip is not a pass — the gate's own rule).
- **Known?:** new. `docs/completed/metal-gate-gpu-verify.md:70` anticipated the skip only for a
  missing checkpoint and said "say so rather than reporting 7/7 green".
- **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-01.** Confirmed on all four points before touching anything: the
  gate does unset `GOINFER_HEAVY_TESTS`, both Metal cells set no `Env`, all four named tests call
  `requireHeavyModel`, and `cuda-heavy` does set it. Demonstrated: as the gate ran it, both
  lifecycle tests SKIP in 0.4 s and `go test` exits 0; with the variable set they PASS in 27 s.
  Fixed in three parts — `Env: {"GOINFER_HEAVY_TESTS": "1"}` on both Metal cells; a
  `noteIfAllSkipped` detector (`noteIfEmpty` cannot see this, because a skip IS a result and it
  returns early on `len(res.final) > 0`); and `cellResult.vacuous()`, which reddens the two cells
  locally AND the run verdict for any filtered cell. Mutation-proven both ways: removing the env
  gives `CELL RAN NOTHING` + `FAIL Close() leaks memory` + `VACUOUS CELL(S)` + rc=1, and restoring
  it gives PASS with the `evidence()` lines the archived logs lacked (`trajectory: base 1282 MB →
  peak 1414 MB → end 1284 MB`).

### G-02 · Nothing in the ritual or CI compiles the 64 Metal tests behind `goinfer_testhooks` — every Metal resident-parity gate included *(two reviewers)*

- **Where:** `cmd/gate/gpu.go:1050-1062` (`metal-suite`, no `Tags`), `cmd/gate/gpu.go:444-445`
  (the Metal `expect` list has no "parity" group), `.github/workflows/ci.yml:337-338` and
  `.github/workflows/ci.yml:347-348` (metal-darwin vets and tests untagged);
  `RELEASING.md:255-257`, `RELEASING.md:275-278`; the CUDA fix this mirrors: `cmd/gate/gpu.go:578-584`
  and `cmd/gate/gpu.go:613-623`.
- **Mechanism:** 59 files / 64 `Test` funcs are `//go:build darwin && goinfer_testhooks` —
  `TestDenseResidentParity`, `TestGemma3ResidentParity`, `TestGemma4MoE_residentParity`,
  `TestQwen35ResidentParityMetal`, `TestMellumResidentParity` (= G11), `TestGptOssResidentParityReal20B`
  (= G10), `TestGemma4Paging_bitExact`, `TestMoEPaging_matchesNonPaged`,
  `TestDeltaNetKernels_cpuParity`, … — and no Metal cell passes the tag, so the ritual's "full metal
  suite" is the kernel-level tier plus the snapshot golden. `RELEASING.md:255-257` says the Metal
  run vouches for G10 and G11; neither is compiled by the command it names (G11's closure record in
  `docs/completed/queue-correctness.md:709-710` was a hand-run with the tag). CI's `metal-darwin`
  job vets without the tag too, so a type error in any of the 59 files is invisible until a hand
  run. This is the hole `cmd/gate/gpu.go:579-584` describes closing for CUDA ("ran NEITHER the
  resident parity gates NOR anything that asserts a forward").
- **Fix:** a `metal-parity` cell mirroring `cudaParity` (`Tags: {"goinfer_testhooks"}`, declared in
  `g.expect`, with a skip census); `go vet -tags goinfer_testhooks ./metal/...` in `metal-darwin`
  (device-free).
- **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-01.** Both halves. CI's `metal-darwin` job now runs
  `go vet -tags goinfer_testhooks ./metal/...` — vet compiles without executing, so it is safe on
  the runner whose Metal layer SIGSEGVs. And a `metal-parity` cell is added, wired into the metal
  dispatch AND declared in `g.expect` (a cell that is defined but never called would be its own
  vacuous gate). Verified: the gate reports 8 declared → 8 groups, and the new cell runs **21
  tests** — `TestDenseResidentParity` (16.9 s), `TestGemma4MoE_residentParity`,
  `TestGemma4Paging_bitExact`, `TestDeltaNetKernels_cpuParity` among them. The 59 tagged files
  compile clean, so nothing had rotted — but that was luck, not process.
- **Known?:** new (G-01 class, closed for CUDA on 2026-08-07).

### G-03 · `gate gpu`'s Metal "cgo-free" check builds the ROOT `cmd/serve`, which imports no Metal code — the mirror of the CUDA defect d2c4858 fixed

- **Where:** `cmd/gate/gpu.go:1064-1138` vs `cmd/gate/gpu.go:828-831`.
- **Code:**
  ```go
  build := exec.Command("go", "build", "-o", bin, "./cmd/serve")   // no Dir, no tag: the pure-Go root binary
  build.Env = append(os.Environ(), "CGO_ENABLED=0")
  g.ok("serve builds CGO_ENABLED=0 (Metal is dlopen'd via purego-objc)")
  ```
- **Mechanism:** `metal/cmd/serve` — the entrypoint a consumer gets, and the one ci.yml:335 builds —
  is never touched; the root builds fine regardless, so the check passes forever.
- **Fix:** `build.Dir = "metal"`; optionally `otool -L` for a Metal framework link as the `ldd`
  analogue.
- **Known?:** G-01 class; the CUDA instance closed at d2c4858, this half was not.
- **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-01.** `build.Dir = "metal"` plus an `otool -L` assertion mirroring
  CUDA's `ldd` one. Verified the fix discriminates, which the old check could not: with a
  deliberate compile error injected into `metal/cmd/serve`, the fixed check reports
  `FAIL metal/cmd/serve does not build CGO_ENABLED=0` while the root `./cmd/serve` still builds —
  i.e. the old check would have said PASS on a broken Metal entrypoint. Note both binaries are
  genuinely cgo-free today, which is exactly why this was invisible: the assertion was true, just
  never about the artifact consumers get.

### G-04 · Five REQUIRED sweep gates are still FIRST-RUN, so a regression in any of them is "an ITEM, not a blocker" — although all five have a committed PASS

- **Where:** `cmd/gate/parity.go:385-393`, `scripts/gate_ledger.py:151-153`,
  `testdata/gate_ledger.json` (47 entries, all BULK-SEEDED 2026-08-14, none promoted since),
  `docs/measurements/parity_sweep_v0.15.0_bd085de_GREEN.log:216-221` and `:235`.
- **Mechanism:** B14 makes a gate non-blocking until someone runs `gate_ledger.py promote`. The
  ledger was seeded once. `TestInt4_forwardParity` ("the broadest quant check here"),
  `TestW4A8DecodeParity`, `TestNemotron_textParity`, `TestNemotron3NanoMoE_textParity` and
  `TestQwen3NextReal_oracle` all PASSED in the v0.15.0 release log — a confirmed prior result
  exists — but nothing turned that into a ledger entry: `reconcile` never exits non-zero, no test
  asserts `required ⊆ ledger`, and `RELEASING.md` does not list promotion as a post-sweep step. So
  `TestInt4_forwardParity` can FAIL today and the verdict reads `ALL REQUIRED GATES GREEN (+1
  first-run)`, exit 0.
- **Fix:** promote the five from the bd085de log; a test that every `parityGates` /
  `parityRealckptGates` entry is in the ledger or in an explicit `neverConfirmed` set with a reason.
- **Known?:** mechanism tracked as B14; the stale state is new.
- **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02.** All five promoted from the bd085de log. Each function body
  was hashed out of `git show bd085de:<file>` and compared to today's: byte-identical in all five
  cases, so the recorded `source_sha256` is keyed to the same assertion that produced the PASS —
  not a confirmation of different semantics wearing an old commit's name. The ledger is 52 entries.
  `TestParity_everyRequiredGateIsConfirmed` now requires every `parityGates` / `parityRealckptGates`
  entry to be in the ledger **with all five required fields** or in the new `neverConfirmed` map
  with a written reason; an entry missing a field is a note, not a confirmation, and does not
  satisfy it. Verified red both ways (entry removed; `promoted_by` blanked), green after. The root
  cause — that nothing turned a green sweep into ledger entries — is closed in `RELEASING.md` §C1,
  which now carries promotion as its third obligation alongside evidence and stamp.

### G-05 · The release sweep discards the cells' FAIL counts: 36 family parity tests outside the 55-name checkset cannot block a tag, and five realckpt gates match no cell's `-run`

- **Where:** `cmd/gate/parity.go:286` (`RCIsFailure`, `false` at the time of the audit),
  `cmd/gate/parity.go:295-298` (`cr.Fail` printed, never read), `cmd/gate/parity.go:161-166`
  (the realckpt cell's `-run`, then the hand-written `"Qwen35|Real_gate|Real_oracle"`),
  `cmd/gate/parity.go:317-318` (the catch-all reports SKIPs only), `cmd/gate/parity_test.go:283-295`.
- **Mechanism:** `blockers` comes only from `classifyChecks` over the 55 named gates. A FAIL in an
  unlisted test, or a non-zero `go test` rc, changes nothing. Unlisted: `TestCohere_forwardParity`,
  `TestCohere2_forwardParity`, `TestLFM2_textParity`, `TestLaguna_textParity`, the InternLM, GLM4-MoE,
  Qwen3.5/Qwen3-Next, Gemma3-VL and Qwen2.5-VL text parities, `TestLongPromptFast_forwardParity`,
  and 12 `*Real_gate`s. Five `//go:build realckpt` gates — `TestGemma4_26B_gate`,
  `TestGlm4MoeAir_gate`, `TestLagunaGGUF_gate`, `TestQwen38GGUF_gate`, `TestGptOssReal_logitParity`
  — match neither `Qwen35|Real_gate|Real_oracle` nor any list, so the sweep never runs them and,
  being unlisted, never says so: the five-week `TestQwen3NextReal_oracle` shape
  (`cmd/gate/parity.go:539-540`) for five more gates.
- **Fix:** fold `cr.Fail > 0` into `blockers`; extend the catch-all to FAILs; derive the realckpt
  `-run` from the tagged files (or run the cell unfiltered) and assert every `*_gate|*Parity` in a
  realckpt file is listed or explicitly excluded.
- **Known?:** the checkset-not-tally choice is documented (`cmd/gate/parity.go:19-30`); the
  discarded fail count and the five unreachable gates are not.
- **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02.** Both halves.

  *The discarded counts.* `extraBlockers()` is now the single place both invisible categories are
  counted: unlisted failures, and any cell `runCell` marked `Hidden` (`RCIsFailure` is now true, so
  a cell that dies with no `--- FAIL` line — panic in a goroutine, fatal error, timeout, build
  failure — is a blocker rather than a cell the sweep silently reports around). The unlisted-failure
  match is EXACT, not `catchAllSkips`' containment: containment would hide `TestFooBar` behind
  `TestFoo`, and exactness is also what keeps B14 intact, since a named gate's FIRST-RUN failure is
  excluded by matching its own name. Asserted THROUGH `runCell` over a scratch module rather than a
  hand-built `results` — what was broken here was the CALLER's arithmetic, which a component test
  would have vouched for (the G27 shape).

  *The unreachable five.* The realckpt cell's `-run` is derived from the `//go:build realckpt`
  files, so "a gate exists" and "the sweep can reach it" are the same fact. Measured: exactly
  `TestGemma4_26B_gate`, `TestGlm4MoeAir_gate`, `TestLagunaGGUF_gate`, `TestQwen38GGUF_gate` and
  `TestGptOssReal_logitParity` become reachable, and nothing is lost. Two details that are not
  incidental: the scan reads the `//go:build` LINE, not prose — `decoder/int4_golden_test.go` quotes
  the tag in a comment while being an untagged file whose gate belongs to the plain cell — and the
  derived set is unioned with what the old pattern selected, because that pattern's bare `Qwen35`
  alternative was also the only reason four non-gate-shaped tests
  (`TestQwen35GGUF_vsSafetensors`, `_locateDivergence`, `_routeFlipAtOutlier`,
  `TestQwen35Real_loaderSlice`) were in the sweep at all. Dropping them as a side effect of fixing a
  FILTER would be a coverage loss dressed as a correctness fix; removing one is now its own change.

  *The uncovered families, closed the same day.* The first cut of this fix left `gpt_oss`,
  `granite`, `laguna`, `glm4_moe`, `cohere`/`cohere2` and dense `qwen3.8` in `realckptNotRequired`
  as "open coverage decisions" — shipped families with no required gate ANYWHERE in the checkset,
  not in `parityGates` either, since none has a tiny fixture. That was not a reason; it was the
  absence of a decision, which is what the map was supposed to stop. **All seven are required now**
  (11 gates), which took registering five assets that resolve on the sweep box —
  `GOINFER_COHERE_AYA`, `GOINFER_COHERE2_R7B`, `GOINFER_GRANITE_GGUF`, `GOINFER_GLM_GGUF`,
  `GOINFER_GLM_GIW`, each verified present there rather than assumed — and converting their four
  gates from a hand-rolled `os.Getenv` + `$HOME/models/…` fallback to `assetPath()`, so gate and
  preflight apply one predicate. `os.Stat` on a checkpoint DIRECTORY was the weaker check in two of
  them: a directory with no shards satisfied it. `realckptNotRequired` is down to 11 entries and
  every one now says "this family is required through <gate>".

  *What is NOT closed.* Those 11 gates have never produced a confirmed result, so they are FIRST-RUN
  and their failures are ITEMS until a sweep runs them and a person promotes the values. They are in
  a third map, `awaitingFirstConfirmation`, with the date — not in the ledger, because inferring a
  PASS from "it was absent from the sweep's SKIP list" would bank an inferred value as a baseline,
  and the inference is not even sound given that the same sweep discarded unlisted FAIL counts; and
  not in `neverConfirmed`, because that would assert a decision to leave them non-blocking forever,
  the opposite of why they were made required. **The next box sweep is what closes this.**

### G-06 · `TestForwardN_matchesSequential` gates a bit-identity contract with argmax + cosine 0.99 and a stale comment

- **Where:** `decoder/forwardn_test.go:97-105`, `decoder/forwardn_test.go:160-165`; the contract at
  `decoder/forwardn.go:176-159`, `decoder/forwardn.go:1104-1086`, `decoder/attention.go:124-157`.
- **Mechanism:** the test predates the acc64 switch ("NOT bit-identical … the scalar
  `attendQuery`"); decode no longer uses `attendQuery` (`decoder/attention.go:139-188` routes K=1 through
  `attendBatchedHeads`). A regression that breaks `decode == prefill` by 1e-6 passes; only the heavy
  token-level spec-parity gates would notice.
- **Fix:** assert `!=` on every logit, as `TestMoEExpertMajor_bitIdentical` does; fix the comment.
- **DISPOSITION — **FIXED 2026-09-02.** Measured both directions before changing anything: as shipped, 0 differing logits of 19,447,808 at K=128; with `acc64` off, 0 argmax flips and cosine 0.99606758 with a worst delta of 0.97 — **the old bar passes**. So the gate was provably blind to the regression it guarded. Now asserts every logit equal, and the comment describes the current codebase.**

### G-07 · `TestGptOssExpertCacheAB` requests `MoECacheSlots = 2`, which the resident silently floors to `nE = 4` — the configuration its own comment says can hide the bug it exists to catch

- **Where:** `cuda/gptoss_cache_ab_test.go:52`; `cuda/backend.go:559-582` (`req > topK` only);
  fixture geometry `scripts/gptoss_tiny_golden.py:32-33` (`EXPERTS = 4`, `TOPK = 2`).
- **Mechanism:** with topK = 2 the request 2 is not `> topK`, so `cacheSlots` stays
  `min(8·topK, nE) = 4`: every expert gets a permanent slot after warm-up and slot ≠ expert only by
  first-admit order. The gate discriminated on the 2026-08-31 run by routing luck, not by the
  configuration it states; nothing asserts the effective slot count.
- **Fix:** request `topK+1 = 3` (honoured) and assert `rf.cacheSlots == 3`; or make a request
  `< topK` an error rather than a silent floor.

- **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-03, verified on the box.** Two halves, because the audit's two
  options solve different failures. The test now requests `topK+1 = 3` and ASSERTS
  `CacheSlotsForTest() == 3` — that accessor already existed and its own doc says the built depth
  "is NOT always" the request, which nothing was checking. And a request BELOW topK is now a hard
  error rather than a silent floor: one token's own routed experts must all be resident, so it
  cannot be honoured. `req >= topK` (not `>`) means an exact request is honoured too, which is
  what the old `>` quietly dropped.

  Mutation-checked ON THE DEVICE, and the first attempt did not reproduce: reverting `>=` to `>`
  changes nothing once the test asks for 3. Simulating the FLOOR instead — ignoring the request —
  produces the exact G-07 condition, `cacheSlots = 4, requested 3`.

  A hard error, not `declined()`: that helper swallows the error and falls back to the staged
  path, which is right for a shape the backend does not implement and wrong for an operator flag
  that cannot mean what it says. Confirmed the message reaches the operator — the A/B test's skip
  line carries it verbatim through `ResidentDecline()`.

### G-08 · `TestBatchedVerifyKernelParity` dispatches `rope` and `attention` with stale argument lists — the "Phase 2 CONFIRMED bit-identical" evidence test cannot run as described

- **Where:** `metal/batched_verify_test.go:140-143` (6 and 10 buffers) vs `metal/kernels.go:511-513`
  (`rope` has 7) and `metal/kernels.go:606-612` (`attention` has 12); the same regression fixed
  elsewhere in ca094fb; `metal/model.go:1512` (production passes `L.mscale`);
  `metal/layer_test.go:148` and `metal/attn_shape_test.go:152` have the same 10-of-12 shape.
- **Mechanism:** `scale` (buffer 6) is never bound; it reads whatever buffer 6 last held on the
  encoder (a `uint` bit pattern as float) or an unbound slot, so RoPE zeroes Q/K and the batched
  arm cannot be bit-identical to sequential decode. Behind `goinfer_testhooks` +
  `GOINFER_HEAVY_TESTS`, so nothing runs it (G-02); `docs/task-metal-batched-verify-kernel.md:94-100`
  still cites it as the proof.
- **Fix:** thread `L.mscale` and `L.attnSinks, L.uHasSink` through the three test call sites; have
  `Dispatch` assert the bound count against the pipeline's argument count (aikit-side).
- **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-03.** Threaded `L.mscale` into both `pRope` dispatches and
  `L.attnSinks, L.uHasSink` into `pAttn` in all three call sites (`batched_verify_test.go`'s real
  mscale/sinks; `layer_test.go`/`attn_shape_test.go` use a dummy `NewBufferFloats(d, []float32{0}),
  NewBufferU32(d, 0)` — the same no-sink pattern `metal/model.go:631` uses for every family without
  a learned sink).

  Mutation-checked on real Metal hardware, and the result differed sharply by test: `layer_test.go`
  and `attn_shape_test.go` (synthetic weights, no YaRN, no sink family) show byte-identical output
  reverted or fixed — the missing bindings happen not to matter for those specific geometries, which
  is a fact about THIS hardware's unbound-slot behavior, not a guarantee, so the fix stands regardless.
  `TestBatchedVerifyKernelParity` (real qwen2.5-coder-0.5b checkpoint, `GOINFER_HEAVY_TESTS=1 go test
  -tags goinfer_testhooks`) is the real evidence: fixed, M=2/4/8/16 all report bit-identical logits
  and hidden state at every position; reverted, positions 1-3 diverge catastrophically (e.g. pos 1
  logit[0] want=5.76 got=4.02, hidden[0] want=-1.59 got=0.14) while position 0 stays correct in both
  — RoPE at pos 0 is `theta=0` regardless of `scale`, the exact degenerate case CLAUDE.md's
  minimal-repro warning names. The `Dispatch` bound-count assertion the audit also asked for is
  aikit-side and not done here; this fix closes the goinfer-side argument lists only.

### G-09 · WebGPU has no group in `gate gpu` at all, and every WebGPU resident-parity gate is opt-in by a private env var

- **Where:** `cmd/gate/gpu.go:349-360` (`detectBackend`: cuda | metal | none),
  `cmd/gate/gpu.go:441-448`, `cmd/gate/gpu.go:491-510`; `docs/parity-coverage-policy.md:25`
  ("Backend: WebGPU output == CPU output on 1–2 models"); `gpu/qwen35_resident_parity_test.go:30-32`
  (`GOINFER_DNET_PARITY`), `gpu/residency_c01_reset_test.go:24-34`, the Nemotron/Mamba tests
  (`GOINFER_SSM_PARITY`/`GOINFER_SSM_QUALITY`).
- **Mechanism:** on the Linux box `nvidia-smi` exists, so the gate runs the CUDA groups and never
  `go test -tags gpu ./gpu/`; nothing in `cmd/gate` or `scripts/` sets the parity env vars. The
  families admitted to WebGPU residency since 08-07 (DeltaNet, Nemotron-H default-on) have parity
  gates that run only when a human remembers the variable.
- **Fix:** a `webgpu` group (adapter-detected, `-tags 'gpu goinfer_testhooks'`, setting the parity
  vars itself), declared in `expect`.

- **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-03.** `detectWebGPU()` probes for a real adapter via a subprocess
  (`gpu/adapter_probe_test.go`'s new `TestAdapterProbe`, `go test -tags gpu ./gpu/ -run
  TestAdapterProbe`) exactly the way `detectBackend` shells out to `nvidia-smi`/`uname` — `cmd/gate`
  stays free of the `gpu` build tag and its cgo dependency. Checked unconditionally, independent of
  `detectBackend`'s cuda|metal|none choice, since WebGPU can coexist with either (a CUDA Linux box
  can also have a working Vulkan adapter) — before this fix nothing ever asked. `webgpu` is now in
  `expect` on every branch, so a box with no adapter is an explicit SKIP with a stated reason, not a
  silent omission (`GOINFER_GATE_SKIP_WEBGPU` opts out deliberately). The new `g.webgpu()` group runs
  a `-short` suite tier, then a parity tier that sets `GOINFER_DNET_PARITY=1` and
  `GOINFER_SSM_PARITY=1` itself — the private-env-var gates the audit named.

  A software adapter still counts as present (the group's tests self-skip hardware-sensitive cases
  via `newOrSkipHW`, same as a heavy tier's tests self-skipping on a missing checkpoint rather than
  the gate deciding for them).

  **Running this for real caught a genuine, separate bug, exactly the kind G-09 predicted:**
  `go test -tags gpu ./gpu/ -short` — a combination NOTHING had ever run before this fix, matching
  the audit's own mechanism note — killed the process on `TestMLAResidency_realDeepSeek_R05`, which
  loads a real DeepSeek-V2-Lite checkpoint with no `GOINFER_HEAVY_TESTS` gate and no `testing.Short()`
  check, unlike ~22 other real-checkpoint tests in `gpu/`. The checkpoint happened to be present on
  this Mac from earlier work — precisely the failure mode `gpu/heavytest_test.go`'s own
  `requireHeavyModel` doc comment names ("the asset happening to be on disk is not a request to run
  a multi-GB test"), on a test in `package gpu_test` that could not call that unexported helper
  directly. Fixed by adding the same `GOINFER_HEAVY_TESTS` check inline, matching the convention
  every sibling `package gpu_test` heavy test already uses (`gpu/forwardn_parity_test.go`'s pattern).
  Swept the rest of `gpu/*_test.go` for the same shape (a `$HOME/models` reference with none of
  `GOINFER_HEAVY_TESTS`/`testing.Short()`/`requireHeavyModel`/a private parity or quality env var) —
  no other matches.

  Validated end-to-end on this Mac (a real, hardware-backed adapter — `ADAPTER_PROBE: backend=metal
  software=false`), not just built: `go run ./cmd/gate gpu` — all 9 declared groups reported, 10 pass
  / 2 skip / 0 fail, `webgpu suite` 12.1s and `webgpu resident parity gates` 2.2s once the MLA fix
  landed (a first run, before that fix, took the whole cell down after ~5 minutes stuck on the
  now-gated test). The parity tier has real, portable coverage on every clone: qwen3.5's dense
  sibling (`decoder/testdata/qwen3_5-tiny`) is a TRACKED fixture, so `TestQwen35ResidentParity` runs
  for real rather than skipping, unlike the MoE/granite/nemotron siblings which are gitignored and
  degrade gracefully when absent — a mix of real and skipped subtests is not vacuous, which the
  group's `cr.vacuous()` check enforces (an all-skip cell is still a FAIL).

### G-10 · Three smaller instances

- `decoder/api_tiers_test.go:67-74` — `TestAPITiers_hardListMatchesDoc` matches bare identifiers by
  substring, so removing `Load`, `Model`, `Session`, `Config`, `Segment`, `Template`, `Tool`, `Turn`,
  `Meta`, `Has` from the Hard section cannot fail it ("Load" ⊂ "LoadGGUFBytes"). Match backticked
  names with word boundaries.
- `tokenizer/tokentext_test.go:35-38` — `TestTokenText_reconstructs` prints PASS with zero
  assertions when no fixture is present (`t.Logf("skip …"); continue`). Count exercised cases and
  `t.Skip` on zero.
- `gpu/kv_longctx_test.go:24-90`, `gpu/resident_pack_bench_test.go:21`,
  `gpu/decode_staged_prize_test.go:23` — measurement harnesses shipped as `Test*` with only `t.Log`;
  a green `TestKVLongCtx` proves nothing about the i8/f16 KV win it maps. Rename to `Benchmark*`
  or assert the pre-registered rule. (`decoder/prefill_absolute_test.go`, untracked at cfec302, is
  the same shape.)

---
## §3 — Major

### Memory accounting and admission

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-03, all three instances.**

  **The api-tiers substring match took three attempts, and each failure is worth recording.** A
  word-boundary regex over the whole Hard section still let `tokenizer.Load` satisfy an entry for
  `decoder.Load` — measured: with the doc's `decoder.Load` deleted, the check passed on the
  tokenizer line. Scoping to the package's own `### \`<pkg>\`` subsection fixed that but still let
  a TYPE be answered by a METHOD qualified with it (`Model.LoadSession` standing in for
  `decoder.Model`), so of G-10's ten named identifiers only ONE failed when removed. Matching
  whole backticked ENTRIES — a span that IS the name, bare or package-qualified, or ends in
  `.name` — makes all ten fail. Verified by removing every naming span for each.

  The intermediate result is the useful part: a check can be tightened and still not gate. Two of
  my three mutation rounds reported "fixed" on a check that was not.

  **`TestTokenText_reconstructs` counts exercised cases and SKIPS at zero.** It printed PASS on
  this machine, where all three tokenizer fixtures are absent — a green asserting the
  reconstruction property nowhere. Demonstrated rather than argued: the fixed test skips here.

  **The three measurement harnesses are `Benchmark*` now** — `kv_longctx`, `resident_pack_bench`,
  `decode_staged_prize` — with `requireHeavyModel` widened to `testing.TB`. They report numbers
  and assert nothing, and as `Test*` their green said only that the harness ran. Go runs a
  benchmark this slow exactly once (N=1 already exceeds benchtime), so nothing is re-run.

  (`decoder/prefill_absolute_test.go`, which the audit notes as the same shape, is untracked here
  and so outside what this change can reach.)

**M-01 · `ResidentWeightBytes` omits the Gemma-4 MoE experts, the Qwen3.5 mixer/attention
projections, Mamba-2, MLA, the conv weights and the PLE tables — the Metal memory-fit guard admits
the unpaged 26B that motivated it** *(two reviewers).* `decoder/weightbytes.go:71-145` sums
`Embed/LMHead/PosEmbed`, twelve named per-layer matrices, `l.Experts` and `SharedExpert`; the
fields `gemma4moe` (`decoder/weights.go:114` — "the router/experts do NOT fit the generic
Router/Experts fields"), `delta`/`qattn` (`decoder/weights.go:90-91`, WeightMats since
2026-08-19), `mamba`, `mla`, `shortConv` and `PerLayerTokenEmbed`/`PerLayerModelProj` are not
visited. For gemma4-26B-A4B on Metal (`metal/backend.go:147` is the consumer) `need` is the ~2 GB
dense part while `buildGemma4MoELayer` stacks 11.96 GB of experts on top — the ~14.3-GB-on-16-GB
case the guard was written after passes it. The doc calls the omission of norms a safe lower
bound; this is an under-report by whole tensor classes, the "guard that inverts under the condition
it exists for" `CLAUDE.md` names. No test asserts the sum against a MoE fixture. **Fix:** enumerate
