via the same walker the `.giw` writer uses (writer and accountant cannot then disagree); gate on
`../testdata/gemma4-moe-tiny` requiring `ResidentWeightBytes() ≥ Σ expert bytes`.

**DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-03.** Walked `decoder/serialize.go`'s `giwWriter.layer`/`hybridLayer`/
`gemma4Layer`/`v6Layer` — the actual on-disk enumeration — to find every field class missing from
`ResidentWeightBytes`: `w.PerLayerTokenEmbed`/`PerLayerModelProj` (model-level), and per-layer
`l.delta.{inProjQKV,inProjZ,outProj}` / `l.qattn.{qProj,kProj,vProj,oProj}` (WeightMats since
6d4fc79), `l.mla.*`, `l.mamba.{inProj,convW,outProj}`, `l.shortConv.*` (all parity-first `[]float32`
real matrices, not norms — a new `f32SliceBytes` helper alongside `wmBytes`), and `l.gemma4moe`'s
`routerProj` + fused `expertsGateUp`/`expertsDown`. Deliberately did NOT add `gemma4moe.mlpGate/
mlpUp/mlpDown`: the writer's own comment says they ALIAS `l.GateProj/UpProj/DownProj`, already
counted — adding them would double-count the dense branch, not fix an omission.

The gate the audit asked for (`ResidentWeightBytes() ≥ Σ expert bytes`) turned out too weak to gate
anything: `testdata/gemma4-moe-tiny`'s dense/embed bytes already exceed the expert sum by
coincidence, so that assertion passes even against the unfixed code (verified — it does).
`TestResidentWeightBytes_countsGemma4MoEExperts` instead keeps a frozen copy of the pre-fix formula
(`preM01ResidentWeightBytes`) and asserts the delta against it equals the gemma4moe router+experts
sum EXACTLY (fixture has no PLE — `hidden_size_per_layer_input: 0` — and gemma4 has no
delta/qattn/mla/mamba/shortConv, so nothing else should move). Mutation-checked: reverting only
`ResidentWeightBytes` reproduces delta=0 against a wanted 1,581,056 — 19% of this tiny fixture's
8.32 MB total, the same fraction that would be gpt-oss/gemma4-26B-scale on a real checkpoint.

M-02 (the guard's ordering relative to the slot budget, and the double-copy accounting) is a
separate, larger fix and is NOT done here — this closes only the enumeration gap M-01 named.

**M-02 · The same guard runs before the slot budget it should compute from, and counts one copy of
the weights.** `metal/backend.go:82-83` calls `residentFitsMemory` BEFORE `buildResident` reads
`GOINFER_METAL_MOE_SLOTS` (`metal/moe.go:425-434`), and `ResidentWeightBytes` counts every expert.
Qwen3.5-35B-A3B — 22.1 GB `.giw`, measured at 2.19 tok/s paged at N=64 on the 16 GB box
(`docs/task-metal-expert-streaming-at-scale.md`) — computes `need ≈ 22 GB > 11.2 GB` and is declined
to CPU with the message "Metal wires the pages it touches…"; the `CLAUDE.md` rule the guard cites
("allocated slot bytes = N × layers × per-expert, known at build") is what it does not compute. The
measurement harness is unaffected (`metal/qwen35_35b_paged_test.go:95` calls `buildResident`
directly), so no test sees the regression; 5e90521's own message warned "a guard that fired too
eagerly would silently move every model to CPU". Separately, a resident GGUF/safetensors model holds
two copies (`metal/model.go:346-363` packs fresh `words`/`scales` per matrix; the quantized host
WeightMats persist, `decoder/weights.go:360-368` releases only the bf16 source mapping), so a ~9 GB
Q4_K_M GGUF on 16 GB is admitted at `need ≈ 9 GB < 11.2 GB` and lands at ~18 GB anonymous. **Fix:**
compute the number `buildResident` is about to allocate, after the slot knob is resolved: dense +
(paged ? `N·L·perExpert` : experts) + KV (`metalCtxCap·kvDim·2·2·layers`) + scratch (+ the host copy
when the WeightMats are not file-backed); run it inside `buildResident` so the paged tests exercise
it; derive a default N from RAM instead of the doc's "default to 64" that has no code.

**DISPOSITION — PARTIALLY FIXED 2026-09-03: the ordering/paged-accounting half only.** Fixed the
specific bug the audit's headline example turns on — the guard always judging a paged-capable MoE
model against its FULLY-resident size. `decoder.ResidentWeightBytesPaged(slots)` generalizes M-01's
walker: a layer's ROUTED experts (generic `l.Experts`, gemma4's fused `expertsGateUp`/`expertsDown`)
are capped at `slots` when `0 < slots < nExperts`, exact per-layer (bytes/nExperts×slots, not a
cross-layer average — a layer's experts are uniform in shape). `SharedExpert` is never paged (always
active, not top-k routed) and stays uncapped either way, same as every dense matrix.
`metal/backend.go` gained `metalMoESlotsFromEnv()`, reading `GOINFER_METAL_MOE_SLOTS` the same way
`metal/moe.go`/`metal/gemma4_moe.go` do (an invalid/unset value means "assume unpaged" here, safely
— `buildResident` still validates and declines on a bad value), and `residentNeedBytes(m)` — split
out from `residentFitsMemory` specifically so the wiring is unit-testable — now asks
`ResidentWeightBytesPaged(metalMoESlotsFromEnv())` instead of the unpaged number. The guard still
runs BEFORE `buildResident`, same as before; only the byte count it judges against changed.

Mutation-checked at two levels, both catching real gaps my first draft had: the arithmetic itself
(reverting `ResidentWeightBytesPaged`'s cap reproduces delta=0 against a real fixture) and the
wiring separately (reverting only `residentNeedBytes` to call `ResidentWeightBytes()` compiles
clean and leaves the env-parsing test green — that test alone doesn't prove the guard USES what it
parses; `TestResidentNeedBytes_honorsPagingSlots` does, and catches exactly that mutation). A first
version of the new tests also broke CI outright: `testdata/gemma4-moe-tiny` is `.gitignore`d (a
real, if small, checkpoint — never present in CI) and my tests called `Load` directly with no skip
guard, unlike every other test using this fixture. Fixed to match the established
`os.Stat`+`t.Skip` convention (`decoder/gemma4_moe_forward_test.go`'s pattern) — caught by a red CI
run on the M-01 push, not by local review, since the fixture IS present on this machine.

NOT DONE, and still real gaps: the guard is not moved inside `buildResident` (so it still can't see
a slot count `buildResident` derives dynamically rather than reads from the env var, though today's
only source of N IS the env var, so this is not yet a live gap); no KV-cache or scratch-buffer
accounting; no host-copy addend for a non-file-backed (GGUF/safetensors, not `.giw`-paged) resident
model's second in-memory copy; no RAM-derived default N (paging stays opt-in-only, matching current
behavior — a doc that claims a "default to 64" this codebase has never implemented, unchanged by
this fix). Each is a real, separately-scoped piece of work, not folded in here.

### Decoder

**M-03 · P19 default-ON allocates a fresh `fusedScratch` (incl. `hd×nKeys` floats) per pool slot,
per layer, per decoded token, on a path that never uses it.** `decoder/scratch.go:275-282` sits
inside the per-call slot loop with no grow-once guard:
```go
if fusedAttention() {
    p.fused = newFusedScratch(attnRowTile(K, nKeys), hd, nKeys)   // vBlk: make([]float32, hd*nKeys)
}
```
`decoder/attention.go:148` calls `headWorkerPool(nH, 1, nKeys, hd)` per layer per token with
`acc64=true`, and `decoder/forwardn.go:760` (`fusedOK := !useAcc64 && …`) means `ws.fused` is dead
on that path. Since 84e0f13 (`GOINFER_FUSED_ATTENTION` unset ⇒ on) every decode step allocates
`min(nH,6)` fused scratches of `4·hd·nKeys + ~2.6 KB` bytes per layer. Qwen2.5-1.5B (hd=128, 28
layers, 6 slots): ~86 KB × nKeys per token — 88 MB/token at 1k context, 352 MB at 4k, 1.4 GB at
16k, all zero-filled and churned through GC; Gemma-3 (hd=256) doubles it. `--cpu-exact-prefill`
does not turn it off (different env var); no decode allocation gate exists, and the P19 A/B
measured prefill only. **Fix:** allocate `fused` only where it can be used (never from
`headWorkerPool`'s K=1 callers) and grow-once like `kh`/`scores`. Measure tok/s and
`MemStats.{TotalAlloc,NumGC}` per token at 1k/4k/16k, unset vs `=0`, interleaved; expect the gap
to grow linearly with context.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02, with P-01 measured first so the fix had a number. TotalAlloc per decoded token, interleaved A/B: 11.9 MB on vs 74 KB off at context 256 (160x), 42.6 MB vs 74 KB at 1024 (573x) — linear in context, and all of it unread. The scratch is now allocated only when the caller will use it AND grown once; after, allocation is identical with the schedule on and off. The gate asserts that INVARIANT rather than a byte count, which would drift with every unrelated allocation.**

**M-04 · `LoadSession` restores global-layer K/V, int8 scales and `tokens` without validating
their lengths; and the M17 allocation bound is bypassed by a header with `numLayers=0`.**
`decoder/kvsnapshot.go:334-362` checks nothing on the global branch (the ring branch,
`decoder/kvsnapshot.go:301-291`, checks stride, `nLive ≤ w`, `count ≥ nLive` and payload length).
`len(keys[l])`, `len(vals[l])`, `len(keyScale[l])` and `len(tokens)` are blob-controlled and never
compared with each other, with `pos*kvDim` or `pos*nKV`; the CRC covers attacker bytes. A `vals`
one row short → `attendQuery`/`attendBatchedHeads` derive `nKeys` from `keys` and index `vals`
past its end → panic in the generation goroutine → process exit; `len(tokens) > pos` →
`rewindForReuse` computes `matched > c.pos`, `TruncateTo` treats it as an out-of-range no-op and
reports exact → silently wrong. `TestLoadSession_rejectsCorrupt` only flips the CRC
(`decoder/session_test.go:217-221`). Separately, `decoder/kvsnapshot.go:214-236` accepts
`numLayers == 0` / `kvDim == 0` (only negatives and over-maxes are rejected), then `perPos == 0`
skips the `pos` bound and `m.NewCache(pos)` allocates with the MODEL's geometry and the blob's `pos`
(up to 2³²−1) before the geometry mismatch would reject it — and even with non-zero declared dims
the bound divides by the blob's `numLayers·kvDim` while the allocation multiplies by the model's.
A 20-byte header with `numLayers=0, pos=0xFFFFFFFF` in `-session-dir` is a fatal
`runtime: out of memory` at server boot. **Fix:** compare the header to the arch first, bound `pos`
by the model's dims, then allocate; require `len(keys)==len(vals)==pos*kvDim` (or 0 for a
KV-shared layer), int8 scales `== pos*nKV`, `len(tokens)==pos`; reject `numLayers==0 || kvDim==0`;
a table-driven "mutate one field, recompute CRC, expect `SnapshotError`" test.
(`decoder/kvsnapshot.go` is the file `docs/task-code-health.md` §3.3 flagged as the highest-signal
predictor; this is what a read of it finds.)

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02.** The ordering was the bug and the reordering is the fix:
  geometry is now compared against `m.NewCache(0)` — which derives the same dims from the arch
  and allocates no capacity — and only then is `pos` bounded and the real cache allocated. Two
  things were wrong before: the OOM happened BEFORE the check that would have rejected the blob,
  and the bound divided by the BLOB's `numLayers·kvDim` while the allocation multiplied by the
  MODEL's, so a blob could pass a bound it never had to meet. `numLayers == 0` / `kvDim == 0` are
  now rejected outright, and the global-layer arrays and `tokens` are compared with what `pos`
  implies (0 still legal — that is a KV-shared layer).

  **THE FIRST MUTATION CHECK PASSED, AND THAT WAS THE USEFUL RESULT.** Reverting the `<= 0` dims
  check and the bound left every test green, because the reordering alone already rejects those
  blobs at the geometry guard. Reverting the reordering TOO reproduced the audit's failure
  exactly: `signal: killed`. So the load-bearing fix is the ordering; the dims check is defence
  in depth, kept because it states the intent and survives a future reordering.

  **The second mutation check also passed, and that one was a real gap in my tests**: with
  `pos == 0` every blob-controlled length is legally 0, so the header cases never reached the
  body checks at all. Separate tests now cover them — a `tokens`-longer-than-`pos` blob through
  the real snapshot path, and `checkGlobalLen` directly — and both fail on the reverted code.

  The header cases are table-driven "mutate one field, recompute the CRC, expect a
  `SnapshotError`", as the fix text asks. Every one is CRC-VALID by construction, which is the
  point: the CRC covers the attacker's bytes, so `TestLoadSession_rejectsCorrupt`'s flipped
  checksum proved the CRC works and nothing else. It also uses a TRACKED fixture — the existing
  helper points at an untracked `gemma-3-270m`, so the first version SKIPPED, and a skip is not
  a pass.

**M-05 · Llama-4 RoPE layers attend full-causal past `attention_chunk_size`; the limit is read
from config and never applied or enforced.** `decoder/forward_llama4.go:16-18` concedes it
("reduces to full causal for sequences below attention_chunk_size, which the parity gates use"),
`decoder/forward_llama4.go:93` attends `[0, pos]` on every layer; `decoder/config.go:143` reads the
field, `decoder/registry.go:2016-1882` drops it. HF's `Llama4TextAttention` applies a block-diagonal
chunked mask on `use_rope` layers (C = 8192 on Scout/Maverick), so from position 8192 on those
layers see keys the reference masks out. No load-time cap, no doc entry, capability matrix says
window "none". Cheap exact fix: a per-layer start override in `attendQuery`
(`start = max(WindowStart, (pos/C)*C)` on `useRope` layers), or refuse `Pos() ≥ C` until then.
Confidence medium (HF semantics from memory; the code's own comment concedes the omission).

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02.** `attention_chunk_size` now reaches the descriptor
  (`llama4Params.chunkSize`) and `Architecture.attnChunkStart(layer, pos)` returns `(pos/C)*C` on
  a `useRope` layer, `max()`'d with the existing window start in `attendQuery` — the exact
  formula the fix text gives. NoPE layers stay full causal. Applied to the int8 twin as well:
  llama4 is f32-only today so that path is unreachable for it, but a start rule living in one of
  two twins is how they drift.

  **SEMANTICS STATED, NOT ASSUMED.** The audit rates the HF behaviour medium-confidence
  (recalled, not read), so the test names the mask this build implements — a query at p attends
  `[(p/C)*C, p]` on a RoPE layer — rather than leaving it implicit. If HF turns out to chunk
  differently, that test says exactly what to change. It is unvalidated against HF above C
  because no fixture reaches that range; below C chunked and full-causal are the same function,
  which is why every parity gate passed on the broken version.

  **THREE LAYERS, EACH GATED SEPARATELY, and the middle two were found by mutation checks that
  passed when they should not have.** A unit test on `attnChunkStart` passes with the call site
  reverted; a call-site test passes with `chunkSize: cfg.AttentionChunkSize` deleted from the
  adapter. Predicate, call site and config wiring now each fail on their own reversion — and the
  wiring is the one that matters most here, because "read from config and dropped" IS M-05.

  (Also recorded because I got it wrong first: `no_rope_layers` has the opposite polarity to its
  name — the adapter reads `useRope[i] = NoRopeLayers[i] != 0`, so 1 means the layer USES RoPE.)

**M-06 · `validateResolved` misses three zeros that look legal: a zero RoPE base, a NaN/+Inf
`AttnScale`, and zero dims.** `decoder/registry.go:111-190` checks `AttnScale <= 0` and
`NormEps <= 0` (the two LFM2 bugs) and nothing else. `decoder/arch.go:559-562` treats
`RoPEGlobalBase <= 0` as "no tables" and `decoder/rope.go:29-31` makes `applyRoPE` a silent no-op
on an empty table, so an adapter that reaches the chokepoint without its own base check — gpt-oss
(`decoder/registry.go:431-1762` reads only the flat `rope_theta`, never `backfillFlatRope`;
`decoder/config.go:513-513` does not check it) and llama4 (`decoder/registry.go:976-1799`) — loads
clean and generates fluent, position-blind text, dropping YaRN too. The repo's own note at
`decoder/registry.go:473-418` says transformers ≥5.10 nests `rope_theta` under `rope_parameters`;
for llama/mistral/qwen3 that is a loud error, for gpt-oss it is silence. `a.AttnScale <= 0` is
false for NaN (gemma3 with a negative `query_pre_attn_scalar`, `decoder/registry.go:272`) and for
+Inf (`num_attention_heads: 0` → `Pow(0,-0.5)`); `num_key_value_heads: 0` panics with integer
divide by zero in the forward (`group := nH/nKV`). **Fix:** at the chokepoint,
`!LearnedPosEmbed && nemotron == nil && mla == nil && len(ropeInvFreqGlobal) == 0 → error` (give
nemotron `layerNoPE` instead of encoding NoPE as base 0), `!(AttnScale > 0) || IsInf`, and
`HiddenDim/NumLayers/NumHeads/NumKVHeads/HeadDim/VocabSize > 0` + `NumHeads % NumKVHeads == 0`.
Test the chokepoint directly (`TestResolveArchitecture_guardFiresRed` exists for the two fields).

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02.** All three classes, at the chokepoint. `!(x > 0)` replaces
  `x <= 0` for AttnScale, plus an explicit `IsInf` — the point being that **NaN fails every
  comparison**, so `a.AttnScale <= 0` was FALSE for NaN and the old guard waved it through; that
  is how a gemma3 negative `query_pre_attn_scalar` got past it. Six dims are required `> 0` and
  `NumHeads % NumKVHeads == 0`, which is the one that panics rather than misbehaves
  (`group := nH/nKV`).

  The RoPE half names the three legitimate exemptions — learned positions, Nemotron-H, MLA —
  rather than inferring them, so a family that simply did not read `rope_theta` cannot pass by
  resembling one. The audit also suggested giving nemotron `layerNoPE` instead of encoding NoPE
  as base 0; **not done**, because the explicit exemption closes the hole without a refactor,
  and the refactor is worth doing on its own terms rather than inside a validation fix.

  **The first draft of the test reported a passing guard that had not run.** The fixture called
  `finalizeRoPE` at a good base, then set the base to 0 and re-finalized — and `finalizeRoPE`
  returns early on base <= 0 WITHOUT clearing an existing table, so the table survived and the
  guard could not fire. Harmless in production, where it runs once on a fresh descriptor; the
  fixture now mirrors resolveArchitecture's real order and the reason is recorded beside it.

**M-07 · EAGLE prefills the prompt with the exact kernel while `Generate` prefills ≥512-token
prompts with the fast one, so "token-identical to plain greedy" no longer holds; and the session
split-invariance loss is ungated while `docs/server.md` promises exact reuse.**
`decoder/spec_eagle.go:260` and `decoder/spec_eagle.go:85` → `captureN` → `decoder/forwardn.go:1146`
(`forwardLayersN(…, false)` — "never fast"), vs `decoder/forwardn.go:1185` (`prefillLogits` passes
`cpuFastAttention()`, default ON, floor 512 at `decoder/forwardn.go:59` and `:78`). The verify
equality still holds (both arms run acc64 over their own KV) but the two KVs differ, so the emitted
streams can diverge at temperature 0; `TestEagleSpecParity` uses a ~25-token prompt. The n-gram,
grammar and two-model paths prefill via `prefillLogits` and stay consistent. Related:
`decoder/forwardn.go:44` cites `TestSessionFastAttnDivergence`, which does not exist anywhere in
the tree (the `CLAUDE.md` "doc comment claiming coverage" class, one file from where that rule was
written), and `docs/server.md:153-154` still says "Reuse is exact (bit-identical to a cold
prefill)" — with the flag default ON a warm session's ≥512-token suffix diverges from a one-shot
generate (`decoder/forwardn.go:32-45`'s own "NOT SPLIT-INVARIANT"). **Fix:** prefill EAGLE through the same
choice `prefillLogits` makes (or amend the contract and gate with a ≥512-token prompt); write or
delete the cited test; amend `server.md` ("exact under `--cpu-exact-prefill`; otherwise a
≥512-token suffix may differ").

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02, all three parts.**

  **The cited test now exists.** `TestSessionFastAttnDivergence` was named in
  `forwardn.go`'s doc comment and existed nowhere in the tree — the "a doc comment claiming
  coverage is not coverage" class, one file from where CLAUDE.md states that rule. It pins the
  honest contract in BOTH directions: at `fastAttnMinPrompt` the two kernels give different
  logits (the accepted split-invariance loss), and below the floor they are identical (so a
  build where the flag silently did nothing cannot pass by never diverging). Mutation-checked by
  raising the floor: the divergence half goes red.

  **EAGLE prefills through the same choice `prefillLogits` makes.** `forwardN` is shared by
  speculative verify — which must run the exact kernel on both arms or its equality argument
  collapses — and by EAGLE's PREFILL, which should follow the operator's flag. They wanted
  different answers and shared one, so above the 512-token floor the two paths built different
  KV for the same prompt and "token-identical to plain greedy" stopped holding.
  `forwardNAttn` takes the choice; only the two prefill sites pass `cpuFastAttention()`, and the
  four verify/commit/correction sites keep `false`. A gate pins that split at 2 and 4 — reverting
  the prefill sites reports 0 and 6.

  **`docs/server.md` no longer claims what it cannot deliver.** "Reuse is exact (bit-identical to
  a cold prefill)" is now qualified: exact under `GOINFER_CPU_FAST_ATTENTION=0`, and with the
  default ON a suffix of 512+ tokens may differ in the last ulps, which at temperature 0 can flip
  a near-tie. The trade and its measured cost (1.43x on a cold 2048-token turn) are stated where
  the promise was.

**M-08 · `MinP > 1` with no `TopK` panics inside the sampler.** `decoder/sampler.go:553-561`: the
min-p threshold `maxL + T·ln(minP)` exceeds `maxL`, so the candidate set is empty; the "always keep
the top token" clamps (`decoder/sampler.go:540-554`, `decoder/sampler.go:441-451`) then slice
`ips[:1]` on an empty slice or return `ips[len(ips)-1]` at index −1. `decoder/sampler.go:191-192`
claims "min_p at any value are safe"; the HTTP surface does not expose `min_p`, so this is a
library/`goinfer-chat --min-p` crash in the `Generate` goroutine. **Fix:** clamp `MinP` to `(0,1]`
in `NewSampler` and guard `drawFiltered` against an empty set.

### Loaders and formats

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02, reproduced first.** `NewSampler(SamplingParams{Temperature:
  1, MinP: 1.5})` gives `index out of range [-1]` — confirmed before any change, not inferred
  from the code. Clamped to 1 in `NewSampler` (1.0 is min-p's identity, the nearest meaningful
  reading of "more than everything"), and `drawFiltered` returns a sentinel the caller converts
  to the argmax rather than indexing −1.

  **Two defences, tested INDEPENDENTLY, because together they mask each other** — a single
  end-to-end "does not panic" test goes green with either one removed and proves neither. That
  is not hypothetical: the first version of this gate passed with the clamp deleted. Each is now
  mutation-checked alone, and a sweep over min_p ∈ [−1, 1e30] × three temperatures pins the
  docstring's "min_p at any value are safe", which was aspirational when written.

**M-09 · `StreamTranscodeGGUF` writes a header-only bundle for five GGUF families, and the
comment says they are refused.** `decoder/gguf.go:1341-1257` says `canSerialize` refuses MLA /
Mamba-2 / Gemma-4 PLE / Llama-4 before the load; `decoder/serialize.go:119` returns nil
unconditionally since v6. `decoder/gguf.go:1654` then writes header + globals + the layer count,
and the gpt-oss (`decoder/gguf.go:1975-1888`), laguna, granitehybrid, nemotron_h/_moe and llama4
branches each `parallelLayers(...); return w, nil` without ever calling `sink.layer` (only the
qwen35 branch at `:1733-1745` and the generic loader at `:2640-2653` do). For those families
`cmd/prequant` and `serve --stream-weights x.gguf` load the WHOLE model resident in parallel
(defeating the one-layer-peak-RAM contract), write header+globals+CRC, and `selfCheck` fails
minutes later with `truncated body: unexpected end of data`. Not silent, but a broken supported path
with a misleading error. `decoder/testdata/gptoss_tiny.gguf` is committed and no test drives the
stream path on it (`internal/prequant/stream_test.go` uses glm-tiny only). **Fix:** refuse
`sink != nil` at each non-streaming `return w, nil` and route those families through the
resident-build-then-`SerializeWeightsTo` fallback gemma4 already uses (`decoder/gguf.go:1651-1570`);
fix the comment.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02.** The five families whose GGUF branch never drives the sink
  (gpt-oss, laguna, granitehybrid, nemotron_h/_moe, llama4) now route through the same
  resident-build-then-`SerializeWeightsTo` fallback gemma4 already used, via a named
  `needsResidentSerialize`. The comment above `canSerialize` — which still claimed those families
  were refused before the load, years after `canSerialize` began returning nil unconditionally —
  is corrected, and that comment is a large part of why this stayed invisible: it told the reader
  the families it named could not get this far.

  **A hand-kept list will go stale, so there is a backstop**: each of those five non-streaming
  `return w, nil` sites now refuses a live sink with an error naming the cause. Mutation-checked
  in both directions — with the routing reverted and the backstop disabled the bundle fails with
  the audit's exact symptom (`truncated body: unexpected end of data`); with the backstop live it
  fails immediately with a message that says what to fix.

  **The patch initially hit SIX sites, not five.** The sixth was qwen35, which DOES drive the
  sink — patching it would have broken a working streaming path. Caught by checking each match
  against its enclosing loader rather than trusting the count.

  The test is the fixture gap the audit identified: `decoder/testdata/gptoss_tiny.gguf` is
  committed and nothing drove the stream path on it. It now does, with glm-tiny alongside as a
  CONTROL — without it, a "fix" that routed every family through the resident build would pass
  while silently discarding the one-layer-peak-RAM contract for models that have it.

**M-10 · Two unbounded per-layer allocations from untrusted metadata — the M16 fatal-OOM gap,
reopened.** (a) `decoder/gguf.go:578-491` and `decoder/gguf.go:659-578`: `ggufLagunaConfig` takes
`block_count` with only `<= 0` rejected, then `make([]string, numLayers)` — a GGUF declaring
`laguna.block_count = 2^36` requests 1 TiB below Go's `maxAlloc`, which is a fatal `out of memory`
that the loader's `recover()` (`decoder/gguf.go:46-50`) cannot catch; granitehybrid/nemotron/llama4
got `ggufLayerCount`, laguna did not. (b) `decoder/serialize.go:323-322` unmarshals the `.giw`'s
embedded config JSON and calls `resolveArchitecture` BEFORE the `maxSerializedLayers` cap at
`:340-343`; `decoder/config.go:698-664` (`qwen3_next`) and `decoder/registry.go:2016-1821` (llama4)
allocate `NumLayers`-sized slices with only a `> 0` check, and `decoder/config.go:1143-1119`
(`loadConfig`) has no bound at all, so a 300-byte `.giw` or a hostile safetensors `config.json`
with `num_hidden_layers: 68719476736` is a fatal OOM, not the typed error
`LoadSerializedWeights`'s doc promises (`decoder/serialize.go:24-26`). The fuzzer seeds a llama
config, whose adapter has no per-layer allocation. **Fix:** `ggufLayerCount` at `:483`; bound
`NumLayers/NumExperts/VocabSize/HiddenDim` with the `maxGGUF*` ceilings at both JSON chokepoints
before `resolveArchitecture`.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02, at ONE chokepoint rather than the two the fix text names.**
  (a) `ggufLayerCount` on the laguna path, as specified. (b) The config bound went into
  `resolveArchitecture`, before the adapter runs — not into the two JSON call sites. There are
  SIX `resolveArchitecture` callers, and bounding at the callers is the "one predicate, N
  consumers" shape that produced a large share of this audit's findings; inside it, `.giw`,
  safetensors, GGUF and whatever is added next are all covered.

  Worth restating why the ordering matters: a count like 68719476736 asks for an allocation
  UNDER Go's maxAlloc, so the runtime does not reject it — it tries, and the process dies with a
  fatal out-of-memory no `recover()` can catch. The `.giw` loader has a recover() and its doc
  promises a typed error; neither helps. The bound must precede the allocation, not wrap it.

  Only the UPPER bound is checked here. Zero and negative stay with `validateResolved`, which
  runs after the adapter has filled in what the config omits — several MoE checkpoints leave
  fields at zero legitimately. A test pins that split so a later tidy-up does not move them.

**M-11 · `validateShapes` does not cover the v2/v4/v6 tail tensors; a short or absent vector
panics in the decode goroutine, and a real stale-cache trigger exists.**
`decoder/serialize.go:403-630` has no check for `AttnSinks`, the per-expert and shared-expert
biases, `mla.*`, `mamba.*`, `delta.*`, `qattn.*`, `PerLayerTokenEmbed`, `PerLayerModelProj`,
`PerLayerProjNorm`; `vec` (`:386-391`) treats `got == 0` as "absent, allowed".
`decoder/forward_gptoss.go:130` indexes `lw.AttnSinks[qh]` with no nil check; `addBias`
(`decoder/attention.go:11-15`) indexes `b[i]` for every `i`. A gpt-oss sidecar `.giw` written before
v6 (sink-free — the R3 "bundle LOADED CLEAN" artifact) is still within `giwMinReadV=3`, still
"fresh" by mtime (`internal/prequant/prequant.go:216-220`), reads `AttnSinks` as nil, passes
`validateShapes`, and panics at the first request. **Fix:** extend `vec()`/`eq()` to the tail with
arch-derived lengths; require presence for sub-structs the family's forward dereferences
unconditionally. (Residual of the 2026-08-07 F-02; these are the fields added since.)

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02 for the family with a REAL trigger; the rest of the tail is
  NOT covered and that is stated rather than implied.** A new `req` helper sits beside `vec` and
  encodes the distinction the bug turns on: `vec`'s "got == 0 means absent, and absent is
  allowed" is right for a family that omits a field and exactly wrong for one whose forward
  dereferences it unconditionally. Applied to gpt-oss's `AttnSinks` and per-expert biases —
  `forward_gptoss.go` indexes `AttnSinks[qh]` for every head — and to gemma4's PLE tail. Same
  shape as the LFM2 conv presence check (C-03), which is the precedent it follows.

  **DEFERRED, and worth naming so the coverage claim is not overread:** `mla.*`, `mamba.*`,
  `delta.*` and `qattn.*` sub-struct presence. Those need a per-family view of which fields the
  forward dereferences unconditionally, and guessing wrong refuses valid bundles — the failure
  direction that costs users a working model rather than a crash. gpt-oss was done first because
  it is the one with a demonstrated path from a real artifact (a pre-v6 sidecar, within
  giwMinReadV, "fresh" by mtime, sink-free) to a panic in the decode goroutine.

  The gate asserts a COMPLETE bundle passes first, so its six break-one-thing cases cannot pass
  for an unrelated reason, and pins that the same fields stay optional without the gptoss marker
  — a fix that required them universally would refuse every other family's bundle.

**M-12 · The stream-weights sidecar is written in place and judged fresh by mtime only; an
interrupted transcode makes every subsequent `serve --stream-weights` start fail until a human
deletes the file.** `internal/prequant/prequant.go:150-172` (`os.Create(out)` + `giw.WriteStream`,
no temp+rename; `internal/giw/bundle.go:44-72` patches the length placeholder at the END),
`internal/prequant/prequant.go:181-197` and `:190-194` (`cacheFresh` = mtime newer than source),
`internal/serveapp/main.go:891-860` (the error is fatal, no rebuild). The 2026-08-05 audit's M-21 covered a cancelled
context; a SIGKILL, OOM-kill or power loss during the multi-GB write leaves a bundle whose header
length is the zero placeholder and whose mtime is newer than the source, so `Load` fails with
`truncated bundle` forever, naming the `.giw` rather than the cause. The same path loads any
stale-format sidecar (M-11's trigger) because freshness ignores the writer version. **Fix:** write
to `out+".tmp"` and rename; treat a `*SerializeError`/frame error from a probe `Load` (or a version
stamp in the sidecar name) as not-fresh and rebuild.

### Speculation

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02, both halves.** The sidecar is written to `out+".tmp"` and
  renamed only after `selfCheck` passes, so an interrupted run leaves a `.tmp` and no cache at
  all. The existing error paths could not cover this: the interruptions that matter — SIGKILL,
  the OOM killer, power loss — run no cleanup, and `giw.WriteStream` patches the body length at
  the END, so what they leave behind is a zero-length header at a path newer than its source.

  Freshness now ends with the question that actually matters — does it load? — using the same
  lazy mmap probe `selfCheck` uses. mtime cannot see a truncated bundle, an older writer's
  format, or a missing tensor; all three are newer than the source and all three fail at load.
  That is also **M-11's trigger**, so the two fixes meet here: a pre-v6 gpt-oss sidecar is
  "fresh" by mtime, and is now rebuilt rather than served.

  The mtime rule is split out as `cacheNewer` so each half is tested for what it decides. The
  existing `TestCacheFresh` wrote a one-byte file as its "cache", which the load probe correctly
  rejects — retargeting it rather than weakening the probe keeps its original meaning.

  **The temp+rename half needed a different kind of test, and finding that out took a mutation
  check**: a failing `Transcode` removes the file either way, so an in-place writer passed the
  behavioural tests unchanged. The property is about what is on disk DURING the write, and
  SIGKILL cannot be simulated in-process — so it is asserted structurally (create something that
  is not the final path; rename onto it exactly once), which does fail on the reverted code.

**M-13 · `BlockSpec.generate` overshoots `MaxTokens` by up to `VerifyWidth−1`, errors out near the
context cap instead of finishing with "length", and both losslessness gates are shaped so they
cannot see it.** `decoder/blockspec.go:286` checks the budget per round; `decoder/blockspec.go:482-506`
appends and streams the whole burst (up to 8 tokens), so `max_tokens=2` can return 9 and
`usage.completion_tokens > max_tokens`. `decoder/blockspec_cpu_test.go:87` compares only the common
prefix and `cuda/blockspec_test.go:91` asks the reference for exactly `len(got)` tokens, so neither
can fail on it. Near the cap, `decoder/blockspec.go:349-370` verifies `width` rows at `pos` with no
clamp; `cuda/prefill.go:562` (`checkCap`) refuses the whole round and the client gets a generation
error after a nearly complete response, where plain `Generate` (`decoder/model.go:1019-993`) and
the server (`internal/serveapp/openai.go:760`) clamp so a max-length turn ends cleanly.
`GenerateStream`'s telemetry (`decoder/blockspec.go:530-536`) never sets `Drafted`/`Evaluated`, so
`AcceptanceRate()==0`, and cancellation returns without setting `g.err` (`:373-382`). **Fix:** cap
the burst to `MaxTokens−len(out)`; `w := min(width, cap−pos)` per round; make both gates run the
reference at the requested `maxNew` and assert `len(got)==len(want)`.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02.** `blockSpecRoundWidth` clamps a round to whichever budget is
  tighter — the remaining `MaxTokens` or the resident context cap — and returns < 1 so the loop
  finishes cleanly rather than asking for a round the backend will refuse. Telemetry comes from the
  per-round hook, because only the loop knows the CLAMPED width each round used; computing it
  outside from `len(toks)` would assume every round ran at the configured width, which is exactly
  what the clamp makes untrue. A cancelled generation sets `g.err` now — the M-23 shape in a second
  loop. The two losslessness gates are unchanged and still cannot see any of this, which is why the
  new checks are unit tests on the extracted decisions rather than additions to them.

**M-14 · CUDA adaptive-depth `Theta` (0.251) assumes the batched verify, but `ForwardN` is a
sequential single-token loop for every MoE / K=V / non-int4 / non-uniform model — the shape
a9f85ef fixed on Metal.** `cuda/resident.go:1366-1319` (batched only when `prefillCore` does not
decline), `cuda/prefill.go:289-179` (`prefillStaticDecline`: `r.moe || r.gemma4Moe`, K=V,
non-uniform, non-int4/int8 all decline), `cuda/resident.go:1375-1334` (the fallback: one `step` per
row); `decoder/spec_adaptive.go:177-185` (`case "cuda": return 0.251`), `:202-210` (`verifyTheta`
keys on the backend name only); measured on dense 0.5B/1.5B only (`cuda/theta_probe_test.go:49-52`).
On a resident MoE (Qwen3-30B-A3B, GLM-4.5-Air — no sliding window, so `specRollbackSafe` admits
them) the n-gram verify costs one full decode step per node (Theta ≈ 1.0) while the controller is
told 0.251 and drafts 8: nine sequential steps per round for ≈6.7 committed tokens at high
acceptance, worse below. **Fix:** `verifyTheta` should ask the resident — `cudaResident` already has
`PrefillPath()` and `prefillStaticDecline()` — and return ≥ 1 when the batched pass declines, as
Metal now does; measure `T(n)/T(1)` on a MoE with `cuda/theta_probe_test.go` before shipping a number.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-03.** `verifyTheta` asks the resident whether its batched pass
  actually runs, instead of inferring from the backend NAME. 0.251 was measured on dense 0.5B/1.5B
  where the batched pass runs; cuda's `ForwardN` falls back to one `step` per row for every MoE /
  K=V / non-uniform / non-int4-or-int8 model, and a loop of single-token forwards has Theta ≈ 1 by
  construction — which is what Metal MEASURED (1.006–1.048, linear to n=16) and why Metal's
  constant disables speculation. A resident reporting a declined batch now gets 1.02 for the same
  reason, reached the same way.

  **SCOPED to explicit reporters, and that correction matters.** The first version asked
  `Model.PrefillPath()`, which answers false for any resident that is not a `Prefiller` AT ALL —
  a statement about batched PREFILL, not about `ForwardN`. That would have disabled speculation on
  backends whose batched verify is fine. It now overrides only when a resident implements
  `PrefillPathReporter` and says its batched pass declined; today that is `cudaResident` alone,
  whose `ForwardN` falls back under exactly the predicate it reports.

  **NOT done: the T(n)/T(1) measurement on a MoE.** The audit asks for it before shipping a
  number, and no number is shipped — 1.02 is Metal's measured value for the same SHAPE (a
  sequential loop), used because the shape is what determines it. Measuring on this box is
  blocked by capacity, not by time: the only resident MoE here is a 35B-A3B int4 against 8 GB of
  VRAM, so it runs through the expert cache and would measure the cache, not the verify.

**M-15 · The CUDA block drafter's K/V capacity is frozen at `len(prompt)+512`; any longer greedy
generation with `--drafter` fails mid-stream, and the failing `ExtendContext` has already dropped
the old K/V.** `cuda/drafter.go:265-280`: the first `ExtendContext` sizes the per-layer K/V to
`need+512` and `kvCap` never grows; each verify round appends `1+accepted` rows; once
`ctxLen+width > kvCap` (~500 generated tokens, prompt-length independent) `DraftBlock`
(`cuda/drafter.go:427-429`) errors, and `decoder/blockspec.go:354-357` returns that as the generation's
terminal error. If `ExtendContext` trips first it has ALREADY replaced `d.kc/d.vc` with fresh empty
buffers before returning the error. Every committed block-spec test stops at ≤96 tokens
(`cuda/blockspec_test.go:63`, `cuda/drafter_loop_test.go:267`, `cuda/drafter_vs_off_test.go:145`).
**Fix:** allocate `kvCap = r.ctxCap` (5 layers × kvDim — small) or grow by device copy; move the
`ctxLen > 0` check before the allocation.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02, MEASURED BEFORE AND AFTER ON THE GPU.** `kvCap` is sized to
  the target's `ctxCap`, and the `ctxLen > 0` refusal now runs BEFORE the allocation — it sat after,
  so a mid-sequence overflow had already replaced `d.kc`/`d.vc` with fresh empty buffers before
  returning the error that warns about dropping exactly that K/V.

  Every committed block-spec test stops at ≤96 tokens, and the first attempt to drive one past the
  limit stopped at 258 on EOS — so reaching this needed an open-ended prompt as well as a raised
  budget (`GOINFER_TEST_MAXNEW=700`, "Count from 1 to 300…"). With that:

  	before (df3fcd5)   FAIL: `cuda drafter: block at 547..555 exceeds K/V capacity 547`
  	after              PASS: 700 tokens, lossless against plain greedy

  547 is `len(prompt) + 512` for a 35-token prompt — the frozen capacity, exactly as this item
  predicts, and prompt-length independent.

**M-16 · CUDA single-block attention requests `(nWin+128)·4` bytes of dynamic shared memory with
no ceiling; past 12,160 attended keys the launch exceeds the 48 KB default wherever split-KV never
fires.** `cuda/resident.go:2490-2436`, `cuda/resident.go:2495-2441`, `cuda/prefill.go:651-383`,
`cuda/drafter.go:495-496`; the split-KV gate `cuda/resident.go:2471` with `splitkvThreshold`
(`cuda/resident.go:154-163`: `nH >= 24 → splitkvNever`, `splitkvMaxHeads = 24` at `:142`) is a
PERF table, and `GOINFER_SPLITKV_ATTN=0` disables it everywhere; `cuda/glue.cu:146-148` records the
48 KB limit being hit on this stack. Decode: `r.launch` returns the driver error → `Forward` fails →
the generation ends with an error at position 12,160. Prefill: the batched pass errors at layer 0
and `decoder/model.go:874` falls back to the sequential per-token prefill (correct, ~9× slower,
nothing logged). Trigger: `-ctx 16384`+ with Qwen2.5-7B (nH=28), Llama-3-8B / phi3-mini-128k
(nH=32), or any model with `GOINFER_SPLITKV_ATTN=0`; the `-ctx 32768` rows in `docs/benchmarks.md`
were on the 0.5B/1.5B whose split-KV engages at 3072/1024. **Fix:** route to split-KV (or refuse at
`resolveCtxCap`) whenever `(nWin+128)·4` exceeds the device's `MAX_SHARED_MEMORY_PER_BLOCK_OPTIN`
(Turing 64 KB, so even the opt-in attribute reaches only ~16k keys); make split-KV admission
required-or-preferred, not perf-only. Confidence medium (hinges on aikit not setting the opt-in
attribute; the repo's own comment says the limit was hit).

### Serving

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-03, and the audit's "confidence medium" is now MEASURED.** Queried
  on the RTX 2070 SUPER rather than reasoned from the CUDA default:

  | attribute | bytes | max attended keys |
  |---|---|---|
  | `MAX_SHARED_MEMORY_PER_BLOCK` | 49152 (48 KB) | **12,160** |
  | `MAX_SHARED_MEMORY_PER_BLOCK_OPTIN` | 65536 (64 KB) | 16,256 |

  Both halves of the audit's arithmetic confirmed exactly, including the parenthetical that even
  the opt-in range reaches only ~16k keys on Turing. 48 KB is the operative limit because nothing
  calls `cuFuncSetAttribute` to raise a kernel into the opt-in range.

  Split-KV becomes REQUIRED rather than preferred once `(nWin+128)*4` exceeds it — the env gate
  and the per-geometry threshold both describe when split-KV is FASTER, and neither knows when the
  single-block kernel cannot run at all. When split-KV is unavailable (disabled or not loaded) the
  launch is refused with a message naming the flag, instead of failing at position 12,160 or
  silently dropping to the ~9x slower sequential prefill.

  The gate is anchored to the DEVICE: it asserts the constant does not exceed what the GPU
  permits, so the guard cannot drift into allowing launches the driver refuses. Mutation-checked
  on hardware — raising the constant to 96 KB fails against the queried 49152.

**M-17 · A client that stops reading an SSE stream pins the model's single decode worker
indefinitely.** `internal/serveapp/helpers.go:172-176` (`sseSend` → `Flush` blocks in
`net.Conn.Write` with no deadline), `internal/serveapp/main.go:557-560` (`WriteTimeout` deliberately
0 for M3), `internal/serveapp/openai.go:526-526` (`lm.enter` … `defer lm.exit()` spans the stream).
Once the socket buffers fill (~1-4 MB, ≈10k+ tokens on a long-context model) the handler blocks
inside `onText` holding `lm.mu` and its queue slot; `r.Context()` cancels only when the connection
*closes*. Every other request queues then 429s for as long as the stalled client keeps its socket
open. The comment conflates a server-wide `WriteTimeout` (which would truncate long streams) with a
per-write deadline, which does not. **Fix:** `http.NewResponseController(w).SetWriteDeadline(now+30s)`
before each `Flush`, treat a write error as a disconnect, and check the errors `Fprintf`/`Flush`
return.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02 as part of the sseWriter (C-06): a per-write deadline, which is NOT the server-wide WriteTimeout the M3 comment conflated it with, plus a sticky error so a vanished client stops the stream. Tested twice, because httptest.NewRecorder has no ResponseController: a unit test for the sticky error and a real httptest.Server with a client that never reads for the deadline.**

**M-18 · `/v1/responses` cannot complete a tool loop: `function_call_output` items become empty
user turns.** `internal/serveapp/responses.go:318-342` decodes items as `{role, content}` only;
`function_call`/`function_call_output` carry neither, so each becomes `chatMessage{Role:"user",
Content:""}` and the model never sees the tool result (or re-calls the tool — an agent livelock)
under HTTP 200. `docs/server.md:103-105` and `internal/serveapp/responses.go:287-289` claim the round-trip;
`TestServe_responses` step 4 (`internal/serveapp/responses_test.go:133-150`) never feeds a result
back. **Fix:** decode `type/call_id/name/arguments/output`; map `function_call` → assistant
`ToolCalls`, `function_call_output` → `{Role:"tool", ToolCallID, Content}`.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02. `type`, `call_id`, `name`, `arguments` and `output` are decoded; `function_call` becomes an assistant turn carrying the tool call and `function_call_output` a tool turn. The new test is the step 4 TestServe_responses never had, at the decode layer where the loss happened.**

**M-19 · Anthropic tool streaming still buffers silently — G19's heartbeat reached two of the three
buffer-then-stream sites.** `internal/serveapp/anthropic_stream.go:94-101` (`streamMessagesTools`
writes nothing after the single `ping` at `:63` until the whole generation completes) vs
`internal/serveapp/tools.go:125-131` and `internal/serveapp/responses.go:236-239`;
`docs/completed/queue-engineering.md:112-113` ("Two sites"). `/v1/messages` is the surface
`docs/server.md:109-133` markets for Claude Code, where tool-bearing requests are the norm. **Fix:**
`sseHeartbeat` (with the C-06 mutex) or periodic `ping` events, which the protocol permits.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02, and it was two lines once C-06's mutex existed — a ticker here would have raced the handler before it. Its gate is structural, because "two of three sites" is a counting failure and the existing heartbeat tests need GOINFER_SERVE_MODEL and skip in CI: a buffer-then-stream site is definable (a handler holding an *sseWriter whose drive CALLBACK only appends to a builder), so the test enumerates them and fails if either loses its heartbeat.**

**M-20 · Gemma 4 tool rendering and parsing disagree with the model's own template on every step
after the declaration, and the fixture that would catch it is never read.**
`chat/gemma4_tools.go:28-29`, `:39-45`, `:50`, `:152-181` (`gemmaArgs`/`gemmaValue`), `:185-231`
(`gemmaBodyToJSON`/`splitGemmaPairs`); `chat/tools_test.go:100-114` (only case `declare` is
compared); `testdata/chat_goldens/tools_gemma4.json` case `call_result` (unconsumed);
`testdata/chat_goldens/gemma4.json` holds the upstream Jinja. goinfer closes the model turn with
`<turn|>\n` after a tool call and re-opens a `<|turn>model\n<|channel>thought\n<channel|>` scaffold
after the tool response; the template emits neither (the tool responses sit inside the same model
turn, and `add_generation_prompt` emits nothing after a `tool_response`). Nested arguments:
upstream renders a mapping as `{k:v,…}` in Gemma syntax, `gemmaValue`'s default arm emits
`json.Marshal` (`{"limit":5}`), and `splitGemmaPairs` tracks only `<|"|>` quoting, so
`opts:{limit:5,sort:<|"|>asc<|"|>}` parses to `{"opts":"{limit:5","sort":"asc}"}`. Any Gemma-4 tool
loop hits the first; any object-typed parameter the second. The four families' `call_result`
fixtures are dead data. **Fix:** follow the template; make `gemmaValue` recursive and the parser
nesting-aware; wire `call_result` into `TestGemma4_declaration_byteExact` for all four families.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02.** The `call_result` fixture is read now, and reading it is
  what settled the rest: it is the byte-exact reference, so the scaffolding diff came out
  concrete rather than argued. goinfer emitted `<tool_call|><turn|>\n<|tool_response>` and then
  re-opened `<|turn>model\n<|channel>thought\n<channel|>`; upstream emits neither, because the
  tool responses CONTINUE the model turn that made the call. The renderer now closes that turn
  lazily and suppresses the generation prompt after a tool response. Gemma 4 `call_result` is
  byte-exact.

  `gemmaValue` recurses into maps and slices (it json.Marshal'd them, putting `{"limit":5}` —
  JSON — inside a Gemma-syntax body), `splitGemmaPairs` tracks `{}`/`[]` nesting as well as
  quoting, and a new `gemmaParseValue` inverts the whole grammar. Round-tripped over nested
  objects, arrays, arrays-of-objects, commas inside quoted strings and three-deep nesting; the
  test also asserts the rendered body is Gemma syntax and not JSON, since a renderer and parser
  that both spoke JSON would round-trip perfectly and still hand the model something its
  template never produces.

  **THE FIX TEXT'S "for all four families" IS NOT FOLLOWED, deliberately.**
  `TestRenderTools_declarations` records that the embedded tool JSON's exact spacing is NOT
  byte-matched for the JSON families — upstream renders it through Jinja's `tojson`, sometimes
  indented — and pinning that would fix a formatting choice no model depends on. Gemma 4 differs
  in KIND: its tool syntax is a micro-language the model parses, so bytes are the contract
  there, as `declare` already treats them. The other three are asserted structurally, so their
  fixtures stop being dead data without inventing a standard the repo rejected.

  **A DIVERGENCE THE AUDIT DID NOT LIST, found by reading those fixtures:** Mistral's upstream
  template carries the call id in both directions (`"id"` in `[TOOL_CALLS]`, `"call_id"` in
  `[TOOL_RESULTS]`) and goinfer emits neither. That is semantic, not spacing — with two calls in
  one turn the model cannot correlate the results. Filed as a documented, passing check that
  flips to an assertion when the renderer carries the id, rather than left as a red test.

**M-21 · The G1c pre-tokenization guard covers three routes; five others still tokenize an
arbitrary body before rejecting it, outside the queue.** Guarded: `internal/serveapp/openai.go:508`,
`:590`, `internal/serveapp/anthropic.go:448`. Unguarded: `internal/serveapp/tools.go:23-33`
(`EncodeSegments` at `:33`), `internal/serveapp/responses.go:139` and `:194`,
`internal/serveapp/vision_serve.go:210-211`, `internal/serveapp/anthropic.go:571-589`
(`count_tokens`, which never enters the per-model queue, so up to `-max-inflight` (128) such
tokenizations run concurrently). The G1c comment prices what it removes at "~27 s of BPE + gigabytes
of ids" per multi-MiB body; `internal/serveapp/anthropic.go:443-446` already calls this "the same defect this release
claims to fix, left half-covered on one surface". **Fix:** one `promptTooLargeForContext` call at
the top of each, or in `withModel`/`withModelAnthropic` keyed on a request-supplied byte count.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02. All five guarded, plus a sixth the audit did not list: widening the anti-drift gate to prompt builders that tokenize TRANSITIVELY immediately named `serveVisionMessages`. The first cut of that gate keyed on direct tokenizer calls, so `serveVisionChatWith` passed while containing none — visionPrompt tokenizes for it — and dropping its guard produced no failure at all. Nine routes now, all guarded.**

**M-22 · The vision path (and, acknowledged, the tool path) bypass the M25 special-token
hardening: user text can forge turn boundaries** *(two reviewers).*
`internal/serveapp/vision_serve.go:72` and `:100` call `lm.encode(lm.tmpl.Render(...))` —
`tk.Encode` with the added-token trie enabled (`tokenizer/sentencepiece.go:441-449`: "do NOT use
this on untrusted content") — vs the text path's `EncodeSegments` (`internal/serveapp/openai.go:899-904`).
A user message in an image request containing `<end_of_turn>\n<start_of_turn>model\n…` or
`<|im_end|>…<|im_start|>system` becomes real control tokens; typed `<|image_pad|>` runs additionally
reach `mropePositions` (`decoder/rope.go:168-170`) as a second image with no grid → 500 instead of
400. `chat/tools.go:56-61` has the same exposure by design (one `Special` segment, "a follow-up") and
no queue entry tracks it. **Fix:** build the vision prompt as segments (`sp()` for the image-block
markers, `ct()` for content) and use `EncodeSegments`; file the tool-prompt segmentation.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02. The vision prompt is built as segments and encoded with EncodeSegments; `spliceImageBlock` re-tags the image block as its own Special segment so FindImageRun still locates it. Two of my own mistakes were caught by the tests rather than by re-reading: the first splice looked for the block as a segment PREFIX (it is not — the template renders the role prefix into the same span) and would have refused EVERY vision request; and the first test proved the helper worked while saying nothing about the routes calling it, so the unsafe PATTERN is banned instead — no non-test file may apply Encode to a Render result.**

**M-23 · Graceful shutdown cancels every in-flight generation and returns the truncated text as
a clean `finish_reason:"stop"` / `end_turn` 200.** `internal/serveapp/main.go:605-566` (`srvCancel()`
before `Shutdown`), `internal/serveapp/openai.go:913-915` (`genErr` treats every `context.Canceled`
as a clean end — written for the stop-string cancel and a client disconnect), `:1061-1073`
(`finish = "stop"` when fewer than budget tokens were emitted). The 2026-08-05 audit's C-22 fix added a third cancel
source where the client is still connected. **Fix:** after `drive`, `r.Context().Err() != nil &&
stopHit == ""` → a failure (500 / `sseErr` / Anthropic `error` event) or at least `"length"`;
optionally a bounded grace period before `srvCancel`.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02. `streamTokens` reports "length" when the PARENT context was cancelled with no stop string hit — parent, not ctx, because drive's own stop-string cancel fires on the derived one. "length" rather than an error deliberately: a client disconnect is indistinguishable here and a 500 would be wrong for it, while "length" is truthful in both and is the signal clients already act on.**

**M-24 · Two concurrent admin loads of the same name: the loser's fully loaded model is dropped
without `Close`.** `internal/serveapp/admin.go:85-95`: `loadDecoder` runs outside any lock, the
post-check refuses to publish on a duplicate name, and the refused `*loadedModel` (resident device
memory, `.giw` mmap, block-drafter upload) is leaked — the purego-has-no-finalizers class the drain
design (`internal/serveapp/liveness.go:10-18`) exists for. **Fix:** `lm.model.Close();
lm.closeEntryNatives()` on the 409 path.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02.** `lm.model.Close(); lm.closeEntryNatives()` on the 409 path,
  as the audit specified. Safe to close unconditionally there and worth stating why: `loadDecoder`
  always builds a fresh `decoder.Load`, so the loser shares its weights with no other registry
  entry, and `retainLocked` has not run for it — it was never published. Not a `defer`, which
  would also fire on the success path where the registry now owns the model.

  **The gate reads the SOURCE, deliberately.** The leak has no observable behaviour: nothing
  errors, nothing is slower, the memory is simply never returned. A test that loaded two real
  models and watched RSS would be measuring Darwin's UBC rather than the defect — the same
  inversion this audit already recorded for the N=128 slot-pressure ceiling. It asserts the
  post-load 409 branch calls both, and pins the branch COUNT at 1 so the PRE-load duplicate check
  (which has nothing to close) cannot satisfy it. The first draft matched 2, because a
  nested-text scan also matched the enclosing function body; it now matches direct statements.

**M-25 · `/v1/completions` drops the leading space of the continuation on SentencePiece
dummy-prefix families (Llama-2/Mistral).** `internal/serveapp/openai.go`'s pre-R-08 shape decoded
the generated ids standalone via two `DecodeContinuation` calls; the fixed code decodes
incrementally via `DecodePiece` at `internal/serveapp/openai.go:1073`, guarded by
`TestStreamTokens_decodesAsAContinuation`, which enforces the same non-stripping property this
item names;
`tokenizer/sentencepiece.go:752-756`, `:757-760` strip one leading
space as a *sequence*-level rule (the 2026-08-05 audit's M-13). Prompt "The capital of France is" → tokens `▁Paris` →
client receives "Paris" where OpenAI/llama.cpp return " Paris". `internal/gemmaapp/main.go:113-119`
decodes prompt+generation together for exactly this reason. Confidence medium (family-dependent).
**Fix:** decode with the prompt's last token(s) prepended on the completions path, or
`decode(ids, false)`.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02, and WIDER than the audit scoped it.** The strip happens in
  `streamTokens`, which is shared by chat, `/v1/completions` AND vision — so all three surfaces
  ate the response's leading space on a dummy-prefix family, not just the one named. New
  `Tokenizer.DecodeContinuation` states the distinction the bug turns on: these ids CONTINUE a
  sequence, so the sequence-level dummy-prefix rule does not apply to them. Byte-level
  tokenizers never strip, so it is identical to `Decode` for them.

  **The component test was not enough, and that is the finding.** With only the tokenizer tests
  in place, reverting `streamTokens` to `Decode` broke NOTHING — `DecodeContinuation` was correct
  and vouched for behaviour the system did not produce, exactly the trap CLAUDE.md names. A
  call-site gate now pins that `streamTokens` uses it at both decode sites and never `Decode`,
  and THAT is what the revert fails. The tokenizer test proves its own premise too: it asserts
  `Decode` really does strip on the fixture, so it cannot pass by describing a defect that is
  no longer there.

**M-26 · `stream_options.include_usage` is honoured on the plain-text chat stream only; tool and
vision streams silently omit the usage chunk, and `/v1/completions` never parses it.**
`internal/serveapp/openai.go:549-550` vs `internal/serveapp/tools.go:83-122`,
`internal/serveapp/vision_serve.go:228-244`, `internal/serveapp/openai.go:424-433`. Agent harnesses
declare tools on every turn and rely on the usage chunk for context accounting. **Fix:** send
`usageChunk` before `sseDone` in both paths (the counts exist at `internal/serveapp/tools.go:154`).

### Constrained decoding

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02.** `stream_options` is now a named `streamOptions` type rather
  than an anonymous struct on `chatReq` alone — which is part of why three surfaces silently
  lacked it — and all four normal stream completions send the usage chunk through one `sendUsage`
  helper. `/v1/completions` gained the field it never parsed; the vision path was DISCARDING
  `nComp`, so it also had to start counting what it reports.

  **The gate COUNTS the sites**, in the shape this audit's "N of M sites" findings keep asking
  for: it walks the AST of the three OpenAI-shaped SSE files, finds every `sseDone` that is a
  normal completion (an `sseDone` preceded by `sseErr` is an error exit and owes no usage), and
  requires both that there are exactly 4 and that every one is preceded by `sendUsage`. A fifth
  streaming surface cannot be added without one. `anthropic_stream.go` is excluded on purpose:
  different protocol, own terminator, no `stream_options`.

**M-27 · `StopWhenComplete` truncates a top-level number (or prefix-related enum) at its first
completion point.** `constrain/constrain.go:153-215` (`Process`) and `:135-138` (`MaskAt`): at a
completion point only EOS survives; `constrain/schema_grammar.go:88-103` and `constrain/json.go:84-92`
report `CanEnd` after `1` even though `12` is legal. `response_format` with
`{"type":"integer"}` therefore always returns one digit; `{"enum":[1,10,100]}` can only produce
`1`; `json_object` mode does the same if the model opens with a digit
(`internal/serveapp/openai.go:762-768` is the consumer). The comment at
`constrain/schema_grammar.go:78` ("done as soon as it can't extend") describes the opposite of the
code. **Fix:** split `CanEnd` into may-end/must-end so scalars can extend; a test with
`{"type":"integer"}` expecting multi-digit output.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02.** `CanEnd` was never the bug: it is a correct MAY-end
  predicate and EOS permission needs exactly that. `StopWhenComplete` read it as MUST-end.
  Rather than split the predicate across three grammar implementations, the rule is stated where
  it belongs — StopWhenComplete exists to suppress the WHITESPACE the grammars permit at every
  structural boundary, so that is now precisely what it suppresses, and a token that genuinely
  extends the value has already passed `TryBytes` and is kept. Whitespace-only is the exact
  property and it is the same one for the json, schema and tool grammars, so no per-grammar
  may-end/must-end split was needed.

  **Process and MaskAt held two verbatim copies of the masking rule and M-27 was a defect in the
  copy** — the "one predicate, N consumers" shape of §0 theme 1. They now both call one `maskID`.

  Mutation-checked in BOTH directions, which is what the test is for: restoring the blanket mask
  fails the integer and enum arms, and a fix that simply stopped masking — which passes every
  scalar assertion — fails the completed-object arm and the pre-existing
  `TestMasker_stopWhenComplete`. The comment at `schema_grammar.go` that the audit noted
  "describes the opposite of the code" said *"done as soon as it can't extend"*; it now says
  may-end and records that StopWhenComplete acted on the old reading.

**M-28 · `SchemaFromStruct` does not flatten embedded structs, and the "json.Unmarshal always
succeeds" contract is false for `json.Unmarshaler` types and sized/unsigned ints.**
`constrain/reflect.go:77-98` never consults `f.Anonymous`, so `type Person struct { Base; Name
string }` yields `{"Base":{…},"name":…}` while `json.Unmarshal` expects `Base`'s fields promoted —
the output unmarshals *without error* and every promoted field stays zero. `constrain/reflect.go:195-199`:
`time.Time` (and any `Unmarshaler`) has no exported fields → `{"type":"object","properties":{},
"additionalProperties":false}` → the grammar forces `{}` → `UnmarshalJSON` rejects it; `int8`/`uint`
map to an unbounded `integer` (the property test works around this at
`constrain/schema_test.go:183` with its own 15-digit cap — the "test supplies its own calling
convention" trap). `README.md:57-62` and the docstring (`constrain/reflect.go:12-25`) make the
promise. **Fix:** recurse into anonymous fields; error on a struct with zero exported fields or map
`Unmarshaler`/`TextUnmarshaler` to `string`; a `nonNegative` flag on the number node for unsigned
kinds; drop "always succeeds".

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02, with one part deliberately STATED rather than fixed.**
  Embedded structs are promoted as `encoding/json` promotes them (an anonymous field with an
  explicit json name stays a normal property, per json's own rule — a fix that promoted
  unconditionally would be wrong in the other direction, and there is a test for that). A type
  that decodes from a JSON string via `UnmarshalText` maps to `"string"`, which covers `time.Time`;
  one with its own `UnmarshalJSON` and no text form is now a compile ERROR, as is a struct with no
  exported fields. Unsigned kinds emit `minimum:0`, and `minimum` becomes a supported keyword for
  the value 0 ONLY — that is a prefix rule the byte FSM can enforce, and any other bound is refused
  rather than ignored, per this compiler's rule that an unenforceable constraint is loud. (It was
  not a known keyword before, so this only widens what compiles.)

  **The embedded case was the worst of the three because it was SILENT.** `{"Base":{…},"name":…}`
  round-trips through `json.Unmarshal` WITHOUT ERROR and leaves every promoted field zero, so any
  test checking `err == nil` passes on it. The gate asserts the promoted key is present AND
  pins that premise — that the old schema's output really did parse cleanly — so it cannot
  later pass for the wrong reason.

  **NOT fixed: magnitude.** JSON Schema `integer` has no width, so a `uint8` field can still be
  handed 99999 and `json.Unmarshal` will error. That is why the contract itself changed: the
  docstring and `README.md` no longer say "always succeeds" but state what IS guaranteed (shape:
  keys, requiredness, JSON type, sign) and what is not. A test pins the remaining gap so it cannot
  be quietly closed without updating the sentence that describes it.

**M-29 · enum/const literals are HTML-escaped by `json.Marshal`, so the grammar forces `<` and
distorts the model's choice.** `constrain/schema.go:318-330` (`encodeLiteral`), `:332-343`,
`constrain/tool_grammar.go:37-40`. For `{"enum":["<",">","="]}` the natural continuation `<` is
masked at −∞ and a greedy small model slides to `"="`; the output validates (the oracle marshals
both sides) but the selected literal is not the one the model wanted. The schema is also parsed
into `float64`, so an integer enum above 2^53 is re-encoded with lost precision. **Fix:**
`json.Encoder` with `SetEscapeHTML(false)`; `UseNumber()` and emit `json.Number` verbatim.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02.** `encodeLiteral` uses a `json.Encoder` with
  `SetEscapeHTML(false)`, and the schema is decoded with `UseNumber` so a `json.Number` is emitted
  verbatim. Both the top-level `JSONSchema` decode and `mustMap` — the path a TOOL's own
  `paramSchema` takes — were switched, since fixing only the first would have left tool arguments
  on the float64 path. `ToolCallGrammar` also routes the tool NAME through `encodeLiteral`: it
  becomes a `const` literal in the grammar, so a name containing `<` or `&` had the same defect.

  **Why no existing test could see this:** the output still VALIDATES, because the oracle marshals
  both sides the same way. The assertion has to be on the literal bytes the grammar forces, or on
  whether the natural continuation survives the mask — so the gates check `encodeLiteral` directly
  and that `"<` is an accepted prefix for `{"enum":["<",">","="]}`. The precision half is pinned
  with 2^53+1, and that test skips itself if float64 ever represents it exactly, so it cannot
  survive as a test that no longer describes the defect.

**M-30 · `{"type":"object","properties":{}}` — the canonical no-argument tool schema — is a compile
error, so a named `tool_choice` for such a tool is refused with 400.** `constrain/schema.go:200-207`
rejects an object with no properties unless `additionalProperties:false`;
`internal/serveapp/tools.go:240-246` turns that into a 400 under `namedForce`. An OMITTED
`parameters` maps to `{}`-only (`constrain/tool_grammar.go:31-33`), but the far more common explicit
form (OpenAI's examples, most MCP servers, every Pydantic/zod no-arg tool) is refused. **Fix:** in
`ToolCallGrammar`, treat an object schema with empty/absent `properties` as closed-empty.

### WebGPU

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02, in `ToolCallGrammar` and NOT in `compile`.** The compiler's
  rejection is correct and stays: in JSON Schema, an object with no properties and no
  `additionalProperties:false` means "any object", and the grammar can only build a closed one, so
  compiling it would be far tighter than the schema means. But a TOOL that declared its parameters
  and declared NONE means it takes no arguments, and that is the better of the two readings — so
  the narrowing happens in the tool path, where the extra context justifies it. An omitted
  `parameters` already mapped to the closed-empty form, so the two spellings of "no arguments" now
  agree instead of one of them being a 400.

  Scoped deliberately: an EXPLICIT `additionalProperties` (true or false) is left exactly as
  written, so `additionalProperties:true` still reaches `compile` and is still refused. The gate
  asserts that too — without it, this fix would be indistinguishable from one that accepts
  everything.

**M-31 · Router cap: the runtime still declines at 256 experts while the kernel, the taxonomy and
the published matrices say 512.** `gpu/residency.go:134-137` (`nE > 256 || nGroup > 32`) vs
`gpu/moe.go:25` (`MAXE = 512u`), `decoder/features.go:273` (`"webgpu": {experts: 512, …}`),
`docs/hardware-matrix.md:24`, `docs/capability-matrix.md:39`. `ResidentEligible` admits 257–512
experts (Kimi-K2 384, DeepSeek-V4-Pro 384 — "✅ resident" in both generated matrices);
`BuildResident` declines them to CPU with a message naming 256; `-require-be webgpu` refuses to
start. `decoder/moecap_kernel_pin_test.go:38-43` greps `gpu/moe.go` only and
`decoder/features_test.go:447-437` asserts `ResidentEligible` — both green, neither sees
`residency.go`: the "one-source-of-truth" split `decoder/features.go:261-271` says the map exists
to prevent. **Fix:** read the declared cap; extend the pin test to `residency.go`.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02.** `gpu/residency.go` reads `decoder.ResidentBackendMoECap`
  instead of restating 256/32. The map already existed to be the single source of truth; the
  decline site simply did not consult it, so a 384-expert Kimi-K2 or DeepSeek-V4-Pro was
  admitted by `ResidentEligible`, published "✅ resident" in both generated matrices, and then
  declined to CPU by a line naming a number nothing else agreed with — or refused to start under
  `-require-be webgpu`.

  **Both existing pins were green throughout**, and that is the part worth keeping: one greps
  `gpu/moe.go` for the kernel constant, the other asserts `ResidentEligible`. Neither reads the
  file that makes the decision. The new pin does, and it forbids a LITERAL comparison there
  rather than checking a particular number — a literal is what drifts, and it is invisible to
  both other tests. Comment lines are skipped, since the explanation of the rule is not an
  instance of it.

**M-32 · `--kv i8`/`--kv f16` with a DeltaNet, Nemotron-H or MLA model: the family-specific KV
allocations ignore the precision flag; and `-ctx` is silently ignored by WebGPU.**
`gpu/residency.go:155-163` raises `ctxCap` to 65536/32768 by the flag; only the generic GQA branch
(`gpu/residency.go:770-785`) switches on `kvI8/kvF16`; the Nemotron (`:494-495`), Qwen3.5
(`:589-590`) and MLA (`:676`) branches always allocate f32 `NewKVCache`, while `:859-860` selects the
kernels model-wide. `i8`: `rl.kScale == nil` → `bind` sets `buildErr = "nil buffer for binding 3
(allocation failed)"` (`gpu/decoderunner.go:728-730`) → "device allocation failed (VRAM
exhausted?)" → the whole model runs on CPU with a misleading reason. `f16`: each K/V cache is
`32768×kvDim×4` bytes — 2× the intended f16 footprint and 2× the f32 default, so the "f16 halves KV
bytes → 32k" premise (`:136-137`) inverts. Separately, `Options.ResidentContext` / `serve -ctx`
(`internal/serveapp/main.go:376` — flag text describes CUDA's load-time VRAM check) is read
nowhere under `gpu/` (`gpu/residency.go:185` `ctxCap := 16384`; `cuda/backend.go:499-500` is the
only consumer): `-ctx 32768` keeps 16k and requests past it fail at `checkCap`; `-ctx 2048` still
allocates 16k per layer. **Fix:** one `allocKV(kind, kvDim)` helper used by all four branches (or
an explicit decline naming the flag); `ctxCap = min(default, request)` with a fit check, or say
"cuda only" in the flag text.

### Release and process

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02 by DECLINING, not by implementing.** `BuildResident` now
  refuses `--kv i8` / `--kv f16` for the Nemotron, Qwen3.5 and MLA families, naming the family
  and the flag, and returns the model to the staged path. Quantized KV for MLA's rank-space
  latent — or for Nemotron's mixed mamba/attention layers — is real kernel work, and inventing it
  here unvalidated would trade a loud wrong REASON for a quiet wrong NUMBER. That is the worse
  trade, and this repo has the measurements to say so.

  What the operator sees changes from "device allocation failed (VRAM exhausted?)" — which is not
  true, and sends them looking at their GPU — to a sentence naming the flag they can drop. The
  `f16` shape was arguably worse than the `i8` one and made no noise at all: each cache was
  allocated f32 at the RAISED cap, so asking for f16 doubled KV bytes against the f32 default,
  exactly inverting the premise the cap was raised on.

  **The `-ctx` half is fixed rather than declined**, since it needs no kernel work:
  `ResidentContextRequest()` now lowers `ctxCap`. `min()` deliberately — a LARGER request is
  ignored, because the 16k/32k/64k caps are proven-fit ceilings and honouring a bigger number
  would trade a clean `checkCap` refusal for an OOM. The flag text said the load-time VRAM check
  applies, which is CUDA's behaviour; it now says what webgpu does.

  Both gated, both mutation-checked. The decline is asserted on the SOURCE — reaching it needs
  one of those three families resident on a GPU, which no fixture here provides, and a test that
  silently skipped would be the "a skip is not a pass" trap rather than coverage. It checks all
  three family probes are consulted, so a family left out of the decline cannot pass.

**M-33 · Releases ship Qwen2.5-Coder weights inside 12 binaries; `NOTICE` says the project
distributes no weights, no model license or NOTICE travels with the asset, and the model is fetched
by URL with no digest.** `.github/workflows/release-assets.yml:52-62` (the two HF `resolve/main`
URLs), `:82-87` (`curl … -o model.gguf` with no checksum), `:111-116` (`checksums.txt` computed
after the build, of our own output), `:124-125` (`--clobber` on re-run); `NOTICE:4-6` ("does not
embed or distribute any model weights"); `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` has no model entry. Qwen2.5-Coder
is Apache-2.0, whose §4 requires the license text and NOTICE to accompany redistribution; a
re-upload on the HF side or a `workflow_dispatch` re-run silently changes the embedded model under
the same tag. Confidence high on the contradiction and the missing digest; medium on the exact
license obligation (read from the model card, not fetched here). **Fix:** pin `sha256` per tier and
`sha256sum -c`; attach the model license + NOTICE (or `--license`); rewrite `NOTICE` and add an
"Embedded model" section.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02.** Every point confirmed rather than inherited. The licence:
  both model repos are `apache-2.0` and ship a BYTE-IDENTICAL `LICENSE` (sha256 `832dd9e0…`), the
  stock Apache-2.0 text with `Copyright 2024 Alibaba Cloud` at line 190 — so the audit's
  medium-confidence reading was right, and §4(a)/§4(c) attach while §4(d) does not (neither repo
  has a NOTICE file). The digests were MEASURED by downloading both files and hashing them, not
  read off the HF API — pinning an unverified digest as the fix for a missing-digest defect would
  be its own joke. They match the LFS `oid`, which establishes that the oid is the content sha256
  for next time.

  Workflow pins sha256 + byte count per tier and fails the build on a mismatch; fetches the model
  LICENSE (pinned too) and attaches it as `QWEN2.5-CODER-LICENSE.txt`; attaches our `NOTICE` as
  `NOTICE.txt` when — and only when — weights actually shipped, keyed on the license file's
  presence rather than on a binary-name pattern. `checksums.txt` now covers every attached asset
  instead of only our own build output. `NOTICE` gains an "Embedded model" section;
  `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` gains the entry marked hand-maintained, with a warning in its
  regeneration instructions that `go-licenses` will silently DELETE it. `RELEASING.md` says what a
  mismatch means at tag time: upstream moved, go read the model repo's history, do not delete the
  check.

  The four shell behaviours the new steps depend on were tested on LINUX rather than assumed from
  macOS (matching digest accepted, bad digest rejected non-zero, `stat -c%s`, and the
  glob-expands-before-redirect ordering that keeps `checksums.txt.tmp` out of its own listing);
  actionlint clean. Not verified end to end: this cannot be exercised without cutting a release,
  so the next tag is where the pins get their first real run.

**M-34 · `RELEASING.md` relies on a CI standalone-build gate that does not exist, and `demo/agent`
has zero CI coverage.** `RELEASING.md:29-32` ("which CI's standalone-build step (below) is what
proves") and `:151-159`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml` has no `GOWORK=off` anywhere (gpu/cuda/metal
jobs all `go work init`); no job builds `demo/agent` even inside a workspace. The B-01…B-04 class is
caught only by a human running Step 2 by hand at tag time; the MCP-SDK demo compiles for the first
time there. **Fix:** a workflow on submodule tag pushes running `GOWORK=off go build -tags <tag>
./...` per module; `go build ./demo/agent/...` in the gpu job; correct `RELEASING.md:31`.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02.** `.github/workflows/standalone-build.yml` now exists and
  builds every submodule with `GOWORK=off` — `gpu`, `cuda`, `metal` and `demo/agent` — and
  `demo/agent` is built by the ordinary gpu job too, so the fifth module is no longer compiling
  for the first time at tag time.

  **It runs on TAG pushes, not every push, and that is structural rather than a preference.** A
  submodule built with `GOWORK=off` resolves the goinfer root from the proxy at its LAST
  PUBLISHED TAG, so between releases it legitimately fails on perfectly good code that uses a
  root symbol added since — CLAUDE.md records that as expected, not a bug. On every push it
  would be red by design and everyone would learn to ignore it; at tag time the root tag is
  published, which is when the answer means anything and is exactly what the release depends on.

  `RELEASING.md` said the claim was proved by "CI's standalone-build step (below)" when no such
  step existed. It now names the workflow and explains the tag-only trigger, and a test asserts
  the artifact exists, covers all four submodules, and does NOT run `go work init` — which would
  prove the opposite of what the sentence claims. (That guard first failed on its own
  explanatory comment, the third time this audit has produced that trap; it skips comment lines
  now.)

**M-35 · `moe.ptx` is no longer the audited NVRTC 12.6.85 artifact — it was regenerated at
12.9.86 (610ce7f) against `REGEN.md`'s pinned-toolchain rule, and six places plus the FMA-lint
exemption still assert the old state.** `cuda/testdata/moe.ptx:5` (`release 12.9, V12.9.86`);
`cuda/testdata/REGEN.md:3-4`, `cuda/kernels.go:107`, `cuda/backend.go:703-704`, `cuda/moe.cu:22-24`,
`cuda/router_f32.cu:3-9`, `cuda/kernel_fma_lint_test.go:15-16` and `:31` ("frozen audited MoE PTX").
The control REGEN.md prescribes (rebuild unchanged at 12.6.85, prove byte-identity, per-kernel
hashes) was replaced by a glue.ptx control that is itself a 12.9 artifact. `moe.cu` is exempt from
