`TestKernelFMALint` precisely because it was frozen, and carries bare MACs (`cuda/moe.cu:125`,
`:159`, `:202`, `:258`) whose contraction a different NVRTC may choose differently; there is no
bit-identity sibling for the MoE GEMVs, so this is not a numerics bug — but "audited" is now a label
with no artifact behind it, and every comment steering the next editor around the frozen file is
steering around something that no longer exists. **Fix:** pin-regen per REGEN.md and record it, or
drop the exemption: convert `moe.cu`'s MACs to explicit intrinsics, add it to `lintedKernels`, update
the six comments.

---
## §4 — Minor

One paragraph each; fix when the surrounding code is next touched unless marked otherwise.

**Numerics and correctness, narrow**

  **DISPOSITION — PARTIALLY FIXED 2026-09-02: the labels are honest, the artifact is not
  regenerated, and the split is deliberate.**

  Corrected in place: `REGEN.md` opens with the actual state, `cuda/kernels.go`'s "audited
  12.6.85" claim, and the FMA-lint exemption reason — which said "frozen audited MoE PTX" while
  protecting a file the audited toolchain did not build. The audit's core complaint is that
  "audited" had become a label with no artifact behind it and every comment steering the next
  editor around the frozen file was steering around something that no longer existed. That part
  is closed.

  **NOT done: the regeneration itself.** Both real fixes are off-limits from this machine — (a)
  pin-regen at 12.6.85 needs the pinned CUDA toolchain, i.e. the Linux box, and (b) dropping the
  exemption means converting `moe.cu`'s MACs to explicit intrinsics, which changes kernel source
  that cannot be compiled or measured here. Guessing at either is the unvalidated change this
  audit declined for M-32, and for the same reason.

  What is added instead is the check that would have caught the drift:
  `TestMoEPTX_versionMatchesItsDocumentation` reads the NVRTC banner out of `moe.ptx` and holds
  the documentation to it, taking no side on which version should ship. Its logic was
  mutation-checked on this machine even though the package is linux-only and the test itself can
  only be compiled here — reverting each of the three doc claims individually flips it red.

- **N-01** · Both chunked scans compute decay ratios as quotients of f32 cumulative products,
  which underflow to `0/0` at real-checkpoint decay rates; the equivalence tests run only in a
  benign regime. `decoder/deltanet_chunked.go:137-141`, `:162`, `:186`, `:202`;
  `decoder/mamba2_chunked.go:91-97`, `:119`, `:134`; `decoder/deltanet_chunked_test.go:62` picks
  `A_log ∈ [-2, 0]`, `seq ≤ 40`. Real checkpoints initialise `A_log = log(U(1,16))` and
  `dt ∈ [0.001, 0.1]`, so per-step decay can be ~0.2 and `0.2^64 ≈ 1e-45` is below f32 min-normal:
  `c[m] = 0`, `c[i]/c[m] = NaN`. Neither scan is wired today (`decoder/deltanet_chunked.go:10-12`); the
  moment either becomes "the reference kernel for the perf rewrite" this is Critical. Carry
  log-decay (`exp(logc[i] − logc[m])`) or renormalise per chunk; extend the tests to
  `A_log = log(U(1,16))`, `chunk ≥ 64`.
  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02. Both scans carry the decay in LOG space. Measured: the f32 product flushes to zero at step 64 of 96 at decay 0.2 — this item's own arithmetic — and a product-form scanChunk produces NaN at position 64. Worth recording that widening the equivalence tests' A_log to log(U(1,16)), as suggested here, does NOT reproduce it: the per-step decay is exp(A*dt) and dt comes from the random weights, so a reverted scan still passes both regimes. The gate that catches it drives scanChunk with the decay array directly.**
- **N-02** · `drawChunked`/`drawFull` fall through to the LAST index on a rounding miss, which can be
  a −inf-masked token (`decoder/sampler_chunked.go:159`, `:163`, `decoder/sampler.go:441`; contrast
  `decoder/spec_sample.go:117-122`, which walks back to the last token with mass). Probability per
  draw ~1e-16; a contract nick.
  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02. The fall-through is one named function both draws call, lastWithMass(p, lo, hi), so the decision is testable without an rng seed that happens to produce the miss. The chunked caller passes its chunk bounds, so an all-zero chunk returns its own last index rather than reaching into a neighbouring one.**
- **N-03** · Combined `top_k` + `top_p` semantics differ from HF/llama.cpp (nucleus over the FULL
  distribution, then intersected with top-k — `decoder/sampler.go:158-159`, `:482-489`, `:553-572`)
  and no API note warns OpenAI-API clients. One sentence in `docs/server.md`.

  **DISPOSITION — DOCUMENTED 2026-09-02, semantics unchanged.** Confirmed in the code: when top-p
  is active the normalizer `Z` is computed over the FULL distribution (`chunkedZ` over every
  logit) and the nucleus cut is then applied within the top-k candidate set — where HF and
  llama.cpp apply `top_k` first and take the nucleus over the RENORMALIZED survivors, a strictly
  wider set for the same `top_p`.

  Not changed, because neither reading is wrong: OpenAI's API never defined the two together, and
  this repo's sampler has a pinned tie-break contract and seeded-draw goldens that a semantic
  change would move. What was missing is the warning, so `docs/server.md` now states the
  difference and that each filter ALONE matches — which is the part a client tuning against HF
  needs to know.

- **N-04** · Two different f32→f16 converters in `gpu/`; the one used for every int4 group scale and
  the f16 KV flushes subnormals, unlike the canonical `decoder.f32ToF16bits`.
  `gpu/gemv_w4a8.go:121-135` (`exp <= 0 → sign`, used by `packF16Pairs` → all W4A8 uploads and
  `NewKVCacheF16`); `gpu/mamba_f16.go:34-62` (RNE + subnormals, used by `packF16` only);
  `decoder/int4f16scales.go:41-68` (round-half-up + gradual underflow). An int4 group with scale
  < 2^-14 reads as all-zero on WebGPU only; `GOINFER_INT4_F16_SCALES` (`decoder/int4f16scales.go:10-17`)
  claims to reproduce WebGPU's unpack and does not for those groups. C-15 fixed `cuda/`; `gpu/` was
  not in that disposition (the 2026-08-05 audit's C-15). One canonical converter.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02.** `gpu/` now has ONE converter, byte-for-byte identical to
  `decoder.f32ToF16bits`; `f32to16` is an alias so the W4A8 upload path cannot drift from it
  again. Both old versions were wrong in different ways: `gemv_w4a8.go`'s flushed the entire
  subnormal range (`exp <= 0 → sign`) — and it is the load-bearing one, so an int4 group whose
  scale is below 2^-14 read as all-zero on WebGPU and nowhere else — while `mamba_f16.go`'s
  rounded to nearest EVEN where every other backend rounds half up.

  C-15 fixed exactly this in `cuda/` and `gpu/` was not in that disposition, so the gate is the
  same shape as `cuda/f16_convert_test.go`: a local copy of the canonical algorithm, bit-identity
  across every representable exponent, and — separately — an assertion that a representable
  subnormal does not come back zero, so the identity checks cannot pass against a canonical copy
  that is also wrong.

- **N-05** · GPTQ loader applies the v1 "+1" zero-point unconditionally and never reads
  `checkpoint_format` (`decoder/gptq.go:41-108`, `:140`); `sym`, `desc_act`, `bits`, `groupSize` are
  parsed and never read. A GPTQModel `gptq_v2` export dequantizes every weight one scale step low —
  finite, plausible, wrong. Parse the field; refuse anything but v1 or drop the `+1` for v2.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02 by REFUSING v2, not implementing it.** `checkpoint_format` is
  parsed now, and a `gptq` checkpoint declaring anything but `gptq`/absent is refused with a
  message saying what would otherwise happen. There is no v2 checkpoint here to validate a v2
  dequant against, and the failure being prevented is the silent kind — every weight one scale
  step low, no error, no NaN. Guessing at the arithmetic would replace a loud refusal with a
  uniformly wrong model. AWQ shares this parser and has no such field; the gate checks it is
  unaffected. The unread `sym`/`desc_act` fields are left as-is — they are parsed and stored, and
  reading them is a separate question from this one.

- **N-06** · `fp8Reconstruct` checks only the element count, not the declared shape, despite its
  comment (`decoder/fp8.go:124-129`); a `[in,out]`-shaped tensor with the same product loads
  transposed. Compare `wT.Shape` to `[out, in]`.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02.** `fp8Reconstruct` compares `wT.Shape` against `[out, in]`.
  The comment above it already claimed the shape was checked against the architecture; the code
  compared the element COUNT, and `out*in == in*out`, so a transposed tensor passed and loaded
  with every weight in the wrong place. Comment right, code wrong — the direction that survives
  review, because the reader checks the comment.

- **N-07** · `.giw` reader allocates layer×expert structs before comparing counts to the arch it
  already resolved (`decoder/serialize.go:360-359`, `:1225-1231`, vs `:404-406`): ~40× amplification
  on an exported entry point. Check against the arch before `make`.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02.** Both counts are compared with the resolved arch BEFORE the
  `make()`. `maxSerializedLayers` is ~40x a real model and `LoadSerializedWeights` is exported,
  so the old ordering let a hostile count amplify that far before `validateShapes` — which does
  catch it — ever ran.

  **The first version of this test passed without the fix**, and that is worth recording: a
  hand-edited blob fails the CRC before reaching any of this, so the test proved only that CRC
  works. It now reseals the CRC after editing and asserts the specific arch layer-count error —
  the only check that runs before the allocation, so receiving that message is what proves the
  ordering. With the fix reverted it reports `truncated body` instead, and fails.

- **N-08** · CUDA: GPU-boundary errors dropped in new code — DeltaNet `Reset` (`cuda/resident.go:1399`,
  `_ = r.do(…resetState…)`, called from `:1183`, `:1203`, `:1243`: a failed re-zero decodes from
  the previous sequence's recurrent state, C-01's shape with a silent failure mode), drafter uploads
  (`cuda/drafter.go:112-150` record into `r.setupErr` after build, which nothing reads; success with
  zeroed drafter weights), `capVec` (`cuda/resident.go:1664-1614`, feeds `HiddenCapture()` zeros).
  Partially B9, whose cuda count is stale (N-27).

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-03, all three sites.** `Reset` records its error and
  `Forward`/`ForwardN` surface it at the `pos == 0` re-zero: `Reset()` returns nothing (the
  cross-backend interface), and a failed re-zero is C-01's shape with a silent failure mode — the
  next sequence decodes from the previous one's recurrent state, and the state buffers being
  allocated zeroed means the FIRST generation is correct either way, so nothing single-sequence
  can see it. Same record-and-surface shape as gpu/residency.go (N-15).

  The drafter's uploads go through `r.upW`/`r.up32`, which record into `r.setupErr` — a field the
  BUILD path had already consumed by then, so every drafter upload failure landed somewhere
  nothing reads again and the drafter was returned successfully with zeroed weights. It now
  snapshots and compares. Worth naming the failure mode: a zeroed drafter proposes garbage,
  lossless verify rejects it, and the cost is acceptance rather than correctness — invisible to
  every correctness gate.

  `capVec` dropped both its sync and download errors while feeding `HiddenCapture()`, so a
  failure handed the caller a slice of ZEROS indistinguishable from a real capture — the input a
  speculative drafter fuses from. It records and leaves the row nil, which a caller can notice.

- **N-09** · CUDA `expertCache.admit` commits the slot mapping BEFORE the DMA
  (`cuda/resident.go:1040-1036`, `admit` at `:856-882`); a failed `UploadBatch` leaves expert `e`
  marked resident over the evicted expert's bytes, and the next token that routes `e` runs the wrong
  expert with no error. Invalidate the admits made in the call on error, or admit after success.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-03.** `admit` commits the slot mapping before the DMA, which is
  what lets a whole layer's copies batch into one `UploadBatch` — that stays. What is added is a
  rollback: every admit in a call is provisional, and on an upload failure the slots are returned
  to EMPTY so the next `admit` misses and re-uploads.

  Empty rather than restored-to-the-evicted-expert, deliberately: a failed batch may have
  completed some of its copies, so the slot's contents are unknown. Claiming nothing costs one
  re-upload; claiming the old expert would be the same bug with a different victim.

  Rolled back on the `slotIdx` upload failure too, where the bytes DID land and the cache's claim
  is true — the device's index table is then in an unknown state, and one redundant upload on an
  error path beats reasoning about a half-applied mapping.

- **N-10** · The attention sink is threaded into the decode launches but not the batched-prefill one
  (`cuda/prefill.go:726` hard-codes `ArgNull()`), and the glue `attention` fallback
  (`cuda/resident.go:2453-2429`, taken when `!r.prefillReady`) has no sink parameter at all, while
  `decoder/features.go:433-437` says the sink reaches both. Unreachable for batched prefill today
  (MoE declines to sequential); the fallback IS reachable if `prefill_batched.ptx` fails to JIT
  (`cuda/backend.go:719-748` continues) — a gpt-oss model then decodes without its sinks, undeclined.
  Pass `r.sinkArg(l)`; refuse `FeatAttnSink` archs when `!r.prefillReady`.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-03, differently in each half, because the two failures are not the
  same.** The batched prefill now passes `r.sinkArg(l)` instead of a hard-coded `ArgNull()`. That
  path is unreachable today only because every gpt-oss model is MoE and MoE declines batched
  prefill — a property of a DIFFERENT check, which this call site should not depend on.

  The glue `attention` fallback cannot be fixed the same way: it has NO sink parameter in its
  signature at all, so it can only be avoided. gpt-oss is therefore REFUSED when
  `prefill_batched.ptx` did not load, with a message saying why. That is what makes
  `decoder/features.go`'s claim — that the sink reaches both paths — true, by declining the case
  where it cannot. The refusal rides the existing setup-error path, so it declines to the
  staged/CPU path rather than crashing.

- **N-11** · The CUDA drafter's attention uses the TARGET's `attnScale` (`cuda/drafter.go:506`)
  rather than `1/sqrt(geo.HeadDim)` (`decoder/dflash.go:651`), against the file's own "THE NORM
  CONSTANTS ARE THE DRAFTER'S" rule (`:362-365`). Equal today only because DFlash drafters share
  head_dim 128 with Qwen3 targets at the default scale; lossless verify makes a mismatch perf-only
  and invisible to every correctness gate.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-03.** The drafter's attention uses its own
  `1/sqrt(geo.HeadDim)`, matching `decoder/dflash.go`'s CPU reference, instead of the target's
  `r.attnScale`. The file states the rule three dozen lines above the violation — "THE NORM
  CONSTANTS ARE THE DRAFTER'S, NOT THE TARGET'S, everywhere" — and this launch was the exception
  to it.

  Equal today only because DFlash drafters share head_dim 128 with Qwen3 targets at the default
  scale. Worth keeping the failure mode explicit: lossless verify makes any mismatch PERF-only —
  the drafter proposes worse tokens, verify rejects them, acceptance falls — so no correctness
  gate can see it, which is precisely why the rule was written as a rule.

- **N-12** · WebGPU `BuildResident` admits Nemotron regardless of `DecodeRunnerEligible`
  (`gpu/residency.go:112-115`) and the block-kind switch (`:456-522`) has no `default`, so a
  `nemoMoE` layer is appended with nil weights and `gpu/decoderunner.go:495-496` nil-derefs — the
  crash `decoder/residency.go:234-233` documents declining at "the one predicate every backend's
  admission funnels through", which this bypass skips. Reachable via the exported
  `ResidencyBackend.BuildResident`. Add the `default:` and gate the bypass on the same shape checks.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02.** The `NemotronBlockKind` switch has a `default` that fails
  the build with the kind and layer named. `BuildResident`'s nemotron bypass skips
  `DecodeRunnerEligible` — which `decoder/residency.go` calls "the one predicate every backend's
  admission funnels through" — so this switch is the last place the shape is checked, and an
  unhandled kind was appending a layer with nil weights for `decoderunner` to nil-deref on the
  first token. Reachable through the exported `ResidencyBackend.BuildResident`.

- **N-13** · `GOINFER_SSM_W8A16` with int4 projections dispatches the int8-weight kernel over int4
  nibbles and f16 group scales — silent garbage (`gpu/decoderunner.go:310`, `:467-477`; `gpu/gemv_w8a16.go:21-23`
  vs `gpu/gemv_w4a8.go:25-28`). Decline when any projection is `*ResidentW4A8`.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02 by DECLINING.** `GOINFER_SSM_W8A16` is cleared (with a line
  saying why) when any projection is a `*ResidentW4A8`. The W8A16 kernel reads one byte per
  weight with f32 row scales; W4A8 packs two nibbles per byte with f16 GROUP scales, so
  dispatching one over the other is not approximate — it is reading a different data structure.
  The flag is an opt-in experiment, and running it against int4 weights answers nothing.

- **N-14** · `ensureAttnWide` guards on the FIRST of three pipelines (`gpu/attention.go:961-822`;
  contrast `:496-504`) — the R-30 class in the file R-30 fixed; a failure on the second/third leaves
  a nil pipeline bound on the next kvF16/kvI8 plan. Guard on the last; skip when `m.mla != nil`
  (MLA never dispatches it).

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02.** `ensureAttnWide` guards on the LAST of the three variants
  it assigns. It compiles f32, f16-KV and int8-KV pipelines and returned early when the FIRST
  existed, so a failure on the second or third left those nil while the next call reported
  success — and a kvF16/kvI8 plan then bound a nil pipeline. The R-30 class, in the file R-30
  fixed. The three are all-or-nothing (any error returns before assigning the rest), so the last
  being non-nil implies all three.

- **N-15** · `DecodeRunner.ReadMambaCap` is exported and panics (`gpu/decoderunner.go:1214-1230`);
  `residentDecoder.Reset` drops every `WriteBuffer` error (`gpu/residency.go:1069-1090`, the C-01
  re-zero). Return errors.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02, both halves.** `ReadMambaCap` returns an error instead of
  panicking: it is EXPORTED, and "test-only" is a comment rather than a compiler constraint, so
  the panic was on a path any caller could reach.

  `residentDecoder.Reset` cannot return an error — `Reset()` is the cross-backend
  `ResidentForward` interface and returns nothing, so changing it ripples through decoder, cuda
  and metal. It records the first `WriteBuffer` failure instead, and `Forward`/`ForwardN` surface
  it at the `pos == 0` re-zero. That matters because the dropped errors were the C-01 re-zero: a
  failed one leaves the PREVIOUS sequence's recurrent state resident and the next generation
  continues from it silently. Recording without surfacing would have been the same silent failure
  with an extra field, so the gate checks both.

- **N-16** · Anthropic JSON decode errors echo Go struct/field/type names
  (`internal/serveapp/anthropic.go:127`) — the leak the 2026-08-05 audit's M-06 and the post-audit R-11 removed from `decodeJSON`
  (`internal/serveapp/helpers.go:131-148`). Share the error shaping.
  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02. Extracted to jsonDecodeMessage and shared by both decoders. Extracting it dropped the byte limit from the 413 message, which TestOversizeBody_BackstopBounded pins — restored, since a bare "too large" leaves the operator guessing which cap fired.**
- **N-17** · `previous_response_id` is a process-wide store keyed by one sequential hex counter
  shared with every id kind (`internal/serveapp/responses.go:94-100`, `:60-77`,
  `internal/serveapp/helpers.go:504`); with `-addr 0.0.0.0` and one shared key any client can
  continue any other client's stored conversation by incrementing the counter; no TTL. `crypto/rand`
  ids; document that the store is shared.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02.** `reqID` is 16 bytes of `crypto/rand` hex. The old counter
  was seeded from `UnixNano`, which hid the problem without fixing it: each id is still exactly
  one more than the id before it, so a client holding its own `resp_<hex>` can walk ±1 onto other
  clients' ids — and `previous_response_id` continues a stored conversation from an id, so with
  `-addr 0.0.0.0` and one shared key that reads back someone else's turns.

  The gate parses consecutive ids as big integers and requires that none differ by exactly 1;
  reverting to a counter reports **511 of 511** pairs. `crypto/rand` failing panics rather than
  falling back — a predictable fallback would restore the defect invisibly, which is worse than
  a crash on a platform where it cannot happen.

  NOT done: the TTL. The store is process-lifetime with no expiry, which is a capacity question
  rather than the disclosure one, and it wants a policy decision about how long a
  `previous_response_id` should stay resumable.

- **N-18** · Named `tool_choice` whose function is not in `tools` silently decodes unconstrained
  (`internal/serveapp/tools.go:301-322`, `:204-207`; the 2026-08-05 audit's M-05 made "named but unconstrainable" a 400,
  "named but nonexistent" falls through); `tool_choice:{"type":"any"}` with ≥2 tools is not enforced
  (`internal/serveapp/anthropic.go:357-371`) although `docs/server.md:114-115` says `any` rides the
  constrained path — same for OpenAI `"required"` (`internal/serveapp/tools.go:286-322`). Error on the first; a union
  grammar or a doc correction for the second.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02 (first half), CORRECTED IN THE DOC (second half).**

  A named `tool_choice` matching no tool is now a 400 naming the tools that ARE available. It
  used to fall through to completely unconstrained generation, having asked for one specific
  function — the louder of the two failures the 2026-08-05 audit left open, because it means a
  typo or a stale tool list and the prose answer looks like the model simply chose not to call
  anything. Fixed inside `constrainForcedTool` so all three surfaces (chat, responses, Anthropic)
  get it from one place, rather than at the three call sites.

  An existing test asserted the OLD behaviour ("nil forced tool should not error"); it now
  asserts the fix, with the unnamed case kept separately so the two cannot be conflated.

  `any` / `required` with two or more tools genuinely does not constrain — the audit is right —
  and `docs/server.md` claimed `any` "rides the same constrained decoding, so a malformed tool
  call is impossible". Constraining it needs a UNION grammar over the tool set, which
  `constrain` does not have; that is a feature, not a fix, so the doc now states exactly what is
  and is not constrained rather than promising something the code cannot do.

- **N-19** · Admin-load `quant` is not treated as an explicit quant for the `.giw` mismatch check
  (`internal/serveapp/admin.go:64-71` sets `c.quant` but `c.quantSet` is inherited from the CLI;
  `internal/serveapp/main.go:158-164`): with no CLI `--quant`, a request asking for int8 loads the
  bundle's baked int4 silently; with one, a request asking for nothing is rejected.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02.** The admin request is the authority for its own load: naming
  a quant sets `quantSet`, and naming none clears it so the CLI value remains a DEFAULT rather
  than becoming an explicit choice to conflict with. Both directions were broken and they fail
  oppositely, so the gate is table-driven across all four combinations — a fix that only moved
  `quant` would pass half of them.

- **N-20** · `demoteLoop` and the unload drain both mutate the session LRU, only one under `lm.mu`
  (`internal/serveapp/liveness.go:159-161` vs `internal/serveapp/main.go:483-469`;
  `internal/serveapp/sessions.go:20-24` says not goroutine-safe). Narrow window (`-kv-idle-demote` +
  `-session-dir` + an unload on a tick); take `lm.mu` in `startDrain`.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02.** `startDrain` takes `lm.mu` around the session save.
  `sessions.go` documents the LRU as not goroutine-safe, `demoteLoop` mutates it on its own
  ticker, and the drain's `ml.rw` waits out in-flight REQUESTS — which the idle-demote goroutine
  is not. `admin.go` already takes `lm.mu` around the load-side restore for exactly this reason,
  so the two sides now agree.

  Asserted on the source: reproducing it needs `-kv-idle-demote` and `-session-dir` and an unload
  landing inside a tick, and a test that raced for it would be flaky in the direction that
  reports success.

- **N-21** · KV snapshots and cold blobs are written world-readable (`internal/serveapp/sessions.go:236`,
  `:242`, `:359`, `:378`); a `.giw-kv` blob carries the conversation. `0o700`/`0o600`.
  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02. 0o600 files in a 0o700 directory; the directory matters as much as the files, since a readable one lists the session ids.**
- **N-22** · `/v1/messages` accepts an empty `messages` array (`internal/serveapp/anthropic.go:420-430`;
  G5 made the OpenAI route 400). Mirror G5.
  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02. Mirrors G5 on the Anthropic route.**
- **N-23** · Forced-tool path rebuilds the ~152k-entry token→bytes table per request, pre-queue
  (`internal/serveapp/tools.go:248` calls `constrain.TokenBytes` where `internal/serveapp/openai.go:768` uses
  `lm.cachedTokenBytes()`) — the 2026-08-05 audit's N-14 disposition says it is built once per model. The demos rebuild it
  per turn too (`internal/chatapp/main.go:502`, `demo/agent/agent/agent.go:537`,
  `internal/gemmaapp/main.go:175`).
  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02. lm.cachedTokenBytes(), which openai.go already used; one call site kept the uncached form.**
- **N-24** · Byte-level decode pushes added-token content through the byte table
  (`tokenizer/bytelevel.go:208-227`, `tokenizer/sentencepiece.go:466-476`); an added token containing
  a rune in U+0080–U+0143 (`é ü ñ`) decodes to a single raw byte → invalid UTF-8, and a wrong
  surface for the grammar mask. Keep `isAdded []bool` and emit `idToPiece[id]` verbatim.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02.** The tokenizer tracks `isAdded[]` and `decodeByteLevel`
  emits an added token's surface verbatim. Pushing it through the byte table is a category error:
  an added token's content is stored as-is, NOT byte-level-encoded, so a rune in U+0080–U+0143 —
  é, ü, ñ, and every chat template that spells a role in a non-ASCII language — mapped back to a
  single raw byte. Invalid UTF-8, and a wrong surface for the constrained-decoding mask, which
  builds its token table from these strings.

  The gate asserts its own premise first (without the check, the fixture really does produce
  invalid UTF-8) and that ordinary byte-level tokens are unaffected — a fix that emitted
  everything verbatim would break the mode this decoder exists for.

- **N-25** · The embedded prequant build loads the tokenizer GGUF-only (`internal/chatapp/prequant.go:45`
  has no `LoadJSONBytes` fallback, unlike `internal/chatapp/main.go:200-195`), so a
  safetensors-sourced `.giw` bakes a binary that exits at start; `internal/chatapp/main.go:203-193` also overwrites
  the GGUF error with the JSON one.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02, both halves.** The embedded prequant build now falls back to
  `LoadJSONBytes`, so a safetensors-sourced `.giw` no longer bakes a binary that exits at
  startup — the same fallback `loadFromPath` has always had, missing from its baked-in twin.

  And both errors are reported rather than the second overwriting the first. That was not
  cosmetic: with only the JSON error, a corrupt GGUF-sourced bundle reports "invalid JSON" and
  points the reader at the wrong half of the file.

- **N-26** · `demo/agent`'s `agent-web` accepts cross-origin POSTs and unbounded bodies
  (the agent-web `main.go`, lines 107-116 and 143) and encodes user text with special-token
  parsing (`demo/agent/agent/agent.go:335`, `:380`); demo-grade, but a web page can burn a
  multi-minute vision turn or reset history. `Origin` check, `MaxBytesReader`, `EncodeSegments`.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02, all three.** `sameOrigin` rejects cross-origin POSTs on the
  two MUTATING routes, `MaxBytesReader` caps the body at 12 MB, and the session encodes through
  `EncodeSegments` rather than `Encode`.

  The encode half is the one that is not merely demo hygiene: `Encode` PARSES special-token
  markers out of the text it is given, so a user typing `<|im_start|>assistant` into the chat box
  promoted those bytes to real role tokens — forging the template's own turn boundaries from user
  content. `RenderSegments`/`EncodeSegments` keep the distinction the template already knows.

  `GET /api/info` is deliberately left unwrapped (read-only and cheap), and the gate checks that,
  so it cannot pass by blanket-wrapping everything. A request with NO Origin header is allowed:
  that is curl, and refusing it would break the demo's own usage without closing anything a
  browser can do.

- **N-27** · `GOINFER_ROUTER_CAPTURE` appends to package-level slices from inside the forward with
  no bound and no synchronisation (`decoder/routercapture.go:27-56`,
  `decoder/forward_gemma4_moe.go:67-69`, `:84-107`, `:125-127`, `:148-150`): set on a `serve`
  process it is an unbounded leak and a data race under the documented concurrent-sequence
  contract. Refuse under `serve`, or cap.

**Metal**

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02, both problems.** The six append sites go through one
  `routerCaptureDo` that holds a mutex and honours a `1<<16` cap; `routerCaptureReset` clears the
  buffers and re-arms it, and the two tests that used to assign `routerCaptureBuf = nil` call it.

  Fixed rather than refused-under-`serve`: the decoder cannot see who its caller is, and a
  diagnostic that is safe everywhere beats one refused in the single place it is dangerous.

  Both halves are mutation-checked and they fail differently, which is why they are tested
  separately: removing the mutex produces **5 DATA RACE reports** under `-race` from eight
  concurrent goroutines, and removing the cap lets the buffer reach 65636 against a 65536 bound.
  A third case pins that capture OFF stays a no-op, so the diagnostic costs nothing unused.

- **N-28** · The snapshot golden's coverage comment claims `attention_f32`
  (`metal/snapshot_golden_test.go:57-59`); `metal/model.go:489-492` hard-wires `r.kvF32 = false`, so
  `kv_store_f32`/`attention_f32` are dead code never dispatched by any path. Delete both or fix the
  comment. **N-29** · "EXPECTED TO FAIL until re-baked" is stale in `metal/snapshot_golden_test.go:40-49`
  and `RELEASING.md:259-266` — the golden was re-baked 2026-08-06 (160dc3f) and updated since; a red
  today is a real drift and the note turns the suite's only absolute gate into noise.
  **N-30** · G22 (the `fault 0x10` autorelease-pool flake) is listed "In flight — QUEUED"
  (`docs/queue-engineering.md:29-62`) but shipped 2026-08-26 (f2fb1e5, `metal/go.mod:7` pins
  `aikit/gpu v0.30.1`; production paths pin their thread). Archive it. **N-31** · Pinned reduction
  widths are spelled as literals at the MoE/Gemma-4 dispatch sites (`metal/moe.go:610`, `:724`,
  `metal/gemma4_moe.go:387-399`, `:555-557`; `metal/deltanet.go:139`, `:142`) instead of
  `tgReduceNorm` (`metal/model.go:49-52`). **N-32** · The M-11 threadgroup-memory budget omits the
  DeltaNet out-proj staging width (`metal/model.go:330-332` vs `metal/deltanet.go:149`,
  `dp.valueDim*2`). **N-33** · Contradictory comments on the non-%8 vocab hazard
  (`metal/cmdbuf_status_test.go:94-98` vs `metal/model.go:776-783`; the code is the latter).

**Docs, comments and registry drift**

  **DISPOSITION — N-28, N-29, N-32 and N-33 FIXED 2026-09-02; N-30 and N-31 NOT DONE.**

  **N-28**: the coverage comment claimed `attention_f32`, which `model.go` builds only inside
  `if r.kvF32` after hard-wiring `r.kvF32 = false` — dead code no path reaches. Comment corrected
  rather than kernels deleted: the claim is the defect (the "doc comment claiming coverage" class
  — the reader matches it to a plausible kernel name and stops), and the dead branch is
  deliberate scaffolding for a kvF32 mode.

  **N-29**: the re-bake the "EXPECTED TO FAIL" note demanded HAS happened —
  `TestMetalSnapshotGolden` reports 6 checkpoints byte-identical on this machine, verified before
  touching the note. A standing expected-to-fail note turns the suite's only absolute gate into
  noise. (Note the audit's own description is out of date here: the note now refers to the
  G-02 re-bake, not the 2026-08-06 one it names.)

  **N-32** is a real bug, not a comment: `maxThreadgroupStageBytes` omitted DeltaNet's out-proj
  staging width, which `deltanet.go` dispatches as `dp.valueDim*2`. On a DeltaNet model whose
  value dim exceeds every other staged width, the M-11 budget under-counted and the check passed
  on a configuration that then exceeds the device limit at dispatch. **My first attempt at the
  test rows silently no-opped** (a regex had already rewritten the anchor line), so the fix sat
  ungated until a mutation check found it — the two rows that exercise the new term are the only
  ones that do.

  **N-33**: the test comment said C-10's guard "declines any non-%8 vocab to the CPU"; `model.go`
  says vocab is NOT checked there and `ForwardArgmax` ROUTES such a vocab around the hazardous
  kernel instead. The code is right; the comment described a guard that was considered and not
  built.

  **NOT done — N-30** (archiving G22 in `docs/queue-engineering.md`) and **N-31** (replacing the
  literal reduction widths at six dispatch sites with `tgReduceNorm`). Both are safe, but N-31
  touches six live Metal dispatch sites for a readability gain, and doing that in the same change
  as a real budget fix would make the budget fix harder to review.

  **DISPOSITION — PARTIALLY FIXED 2026-09-02: seven corrected, the rest left, and the split is on
  EVIDENCE not effort.** Each of the seven was verified against the code before touching the
  comment, and three were load-bearing rather than cosmetic:

  - `cuda/backend.go` said gpt-oss "carries [an o_proj bias] with no q/k/v bias at all" — it
    carries them. `gptoss_safetensors.go` loads q/k/v bias as REQUIRED (a missing one errors) and

  Also corrected: `decoder/gguf.go`'s five-name architecture list (the registry holds 34, and the
  list read as a limit — it now points at the map instead of restating it),
  `internal/serveapp/sessions.go`'s "not yet persistable" for rings (persistable since

  can fall back to the GGUF" (neither caller does — both return the error), and `cuda/doc.go`'s

  **NOT corrected**, and named so the disposition is not read as covering them: the `mtp.go`

- **N-35** · The 512 floor keys on the suffix length `K`, not on attention work
  (`decoder/forwardn.go:312-296`), while the comment justifying session divergence (`:42-43`, "1.32x
  on a warm 2048+128 suffix") cites a case the floor excludes. Either floor on `K·nKeys` (with the
  divergence measured at that shape) or say short suffixes are effectively excluded.

  **DISPOSITION — DOCUMENTED, DELIBERATELY NOT CHANGED 2026-09-02.** The floor does key on `K`,
  and the comment justifying session divergence does cite a warm 2048+128 suffix — `K=128`, which
  the floor excludes. Both halves of the audit's observation are correct.

  Re-keying to `K·nKeys` is the tempting fix and is why it was not taken: the divergence was
  MEASURED at the shapes the current floor admits, so widening the floor would extend an accepted
  output change to short suffixes whose divergence nobody has measured. That is the audit's own
  first option ("with the divergence measured at that shape") and it needs the measurement first.
  The code now says which case the floor excludes and what changing it would require, so the
  1.32x figure can no longer be read as describing a path this build takes.

- **N-36** · `docs/server.md` drift: the route list omits `GET /health` (`internal/serveapp/main.go:516`;
  auth-gated, which a probe needs to know); "reuses the KV cache for the longest token prefix" — the
  LRU's `bestExtend` requires the session's whole token list to be a prefix
  (`internal/serveapp/sessions.go:165-174`); nothing says GPU-resident models take the stateless path
  and get no prefix reuse (`internal/serveapp/openai.go:932-938`) while the dsh section recommends
  `-backend cuda` for prefill-dominated agent turns; vision is described as Gemma 3 only
  (`internal/serveapp/main.go:773-746` wires Qwen2.5-VL); the sampling-performance guidance
  (`docs/server.md:81-87`, "temperature-only … 3× behind top_k=20") predates P2b and is inverted
  by the repo's own numbers (`CHANGELOG.md:754`, `decoder/sampler_selection_test.go:296-305`).

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02, the route half.** `GET /health` is now in the route list AND
  marked auth-gated, which is the part a liveness probe actually needs — an unauthenticated probe
  against a keyed server gets a 401 and reads it as "down".

  The other four drifts in this item (the `bestExtend` whole-prefix requirement, GPU-resident
  models taking the stateless path, vision described as Gemma 3 only, and the pre-P2b sampling
  guidance) are NOT fixed: each needs the behaviour re-read or re-measured rather than the
  sentence rewritten, and the sampling one specifically wants a current number rather than a
  correction to a stale one. Left open rather than half-answered.

- **N-37** · Chat-template byte-exactness is pinned narrower than `chat/chat.go:1-5` claims: no
  `no_system` golden for any family, and `chat/templates.go:156-175` emits no system turn for ChatML
  when `system == ""` where Qwen 2.5's template inserts its default system prompt
  (`testdata/chat_goldens/chatml.json`); harmony multi-turn history renders without the `final`
  channel; the four `call_result` fixtures are read by nothing (M-20).

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02 by NARROWING THE CLAIM and pinning the divergence, not by
  changing the renderer.**

  Rendering `chatml.json`'s OWN committed `chat_template` with jinja2 shows the divergence
  exactly: with no system turn, Qwen 2.5's template inserts "You are Qwen, created by Alibaba
  Cloud…" and this renderer emits no system turn at all. That golden case is now in the fixture,
  derived from the template rather than hand-written.

  **The renderer is deliberately NOT changed.** `ChatML()` is the GENERIC ChatML renderer, shared
  with families that are not Qwen and carry no such default; hard-coding Qwen's sentence would be
  wrong for them. `TestChatML_noSystem_documentedDivergence` therefore asserts BOTH sides — and
  asserts the difference is EXACTLY the default system block, so some other drift cannot hide
  behind the known one. `chat/chat.go`'s unqualified "byte-exact" claim now states precisely what
  is covered: sys_user/sys_multi everywhere, no-system except ChatML's documented case, and tool
  rendering byte-exact for Gemma 4 only.

  The other three families' `no_system` goldens were NOT added: their templates need a
  `bos_token` the fixtures do not carry, so deriving them would mean supplying part of the
  reference myself — a golden that is half upstream and half mine is worse than none.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02.** `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` said the root `go.mod` "requires the
  other three at versions known to work with it, so `go get …/goinfer` brings all four and a
  `-tags cuda` build resolves without further action". Since the M-19 split it requires none —

  what actually happens: the root alone is what a pure-Go build needs, and a backend module must
  be named explicitly.
