---
## §5 — Performance observations

Each names a mechanism and what to measure before believing it; none is a claimed speedup. Where a
figure appears it is arithmetic from the code, not a measurement, and is labelled so. The
"do-nothing arm" rule applies to every one.

**CPU decode and prefill**

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-02, with the anti-drift gate as the substantive part.**

  Measured rather than trusted: **40** variables were read by non-test code and absent from
  `docs/env-vars.md`, one (`GOINFER_RESIDENT_DEBUG`) was documented while NOTHING in the tree
  read it, and three CUDA graph knobs were written as suffix shorthand (`/ _SYNC / _UNSAFE`) so
  their full names never appeared at all — invisible to any grep an operator would run.

  Added: the "Prefill/MoE defaults" section the fix text asks for, naming all four default-ON
  changes with their opt-outs and stating plainly that each can change greedy output; a table of
  the operator knobs that were missing; and an explicit "diagnostics and experiment knobs — NOT
  contract" list, which exists so the gate has somewhere to put things that are not
  operator-facing. `GOINFER_CUDA_GRAPHS_UNSAFE`'s entry now says it bypasses the tenancy gate,
  which its "bounds-check-elision variant" description hid.

  **`TestEnvVars_docAndCodeAgree` is the part that keeps this true.** It fails in BOTH directions
  — a variable read by non-test code and missing from the doc, or documented and referenced by no
  `.go` file at all — and both directions are mutation-checked. It deliberately does not check
  what the doc SAYS about a variable: a wrong description is a different problem, and coupling
  them would make the gate unfixable without prose review.

  **NOT done: the `Options`-field redesign.** The audit's closing note is right that `serve`
  reaching the decoder through `os.Setenv` cannot express per-model settings and forces a library
  caller to mutate the process environment. That is a Hard-tier API change with a real design
  question behind it (which of these become options, and what happens to the env vars), not a
  documentation fix, and it is left open.

- **P-01 · Decode allocation churn from M-03.** Measure tok/s and `MemStats.{TotalAlloc,NumGC}` per
  token at context 1k/4k/16k, `GOINFER_FUSED_ATTENTION` unset vs `=0`, same binary, interleaved.
  Expect the gap to grow linearly with context. A decode allocation gate (`testing.AllocsPerRun` on
  one `forward` step) would have caught it.

  **DISPOSITION — ALREADY DONE, via M-03's own fix (2026-09-02).** M-03's disposition measured this
  exact request first — interleaved `TotalAlloc`/token at context 256/1024, `GOINFER_FUSED_ATTENTION`
  unset vs `=0` — before writing the fix, and shipped `decoder/decode_alloc_test.go`, a `MemStats`-
  based gate on the invariant P-01 named (turning the fused schedule on/off must not change decode's
  per-token allocation, since decode never uses it). No separate work needed here; this entry exists
  so P-01 stops reading as open. See M-03's disposition for the numbers (160x at ctx 256, 573x at
  1024, both to parity after the fix).
- **P-02 · The fused schedule still does full-`nKeys` QKᵀ on sliding-window layers.**
  `decoder/fusedattn.go:85-97` breaks only on `k0 > hiMax`, with no `k1 <= loMin` skip; a cold
  prefill's assembled local window is the whole `[0, K)` (`decoder/kvcache.go:521`). For a local
  layer at K=8192, W=1024 the last query tile computes 16 key blocks and zeroes 13 of them
  (`decoder/fusedattn.go:114-120`): O(K²) where O(K·W) is available with a two-line block skip that keeps
  bit-identity (skipped blocks contribute exact zeros). Mellum2 has 21/28 sliding layers, Gemma-3
  5/6. Measure prefill wall at K=8192 on both with and without the skip; cosine must be 1.0.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-03.** Added the `k1-1 < loMin` block skip (`loMin` computed the same
  way `hiMax` already was, one line each), mirroring the existing causal `k0 > hiMax` break. Safe by
  construction: a block entirely below every row's window start already made every row take the
  `a0 > a1` branch a few lines down, which zeroes the row and `continue`s WITHOUT touching
  `acc`/`mRun`/`lRun` — so the two `mm()` calls for that block were computing output every row
  already discarded. Skipping them changes nothing about the result, only what gets paid for.

  Verified genuinely exercised, not vacuous: `TestFusedAttention_matchesMaterialized`'s existing
  "sliding window" case (K=300, nKeys=1400, window=256) has every row's `lo >= 845` — block 0
  (`[0,512)`) is always skipped there, for every tile — and still passes at cosine 1.000000000,
  max|diff| 4.1e-08, unchanged. Measured (paired, same process, synthetic nH=16/nKV=4/hd=64
  attention at K=8192, window=1024 — matching the audit's own illustrative numbers): fix 0.541-0.543s
  across 3 runs vs 1.099-1.100s reverted — **2.03x**, matching the O(K²)→O(K·W) prediction (with
  K=8192 and W=1024 most query tiles skip the large majority of the key range). Not committed as a
  permanent timing gate — this project's own standing guidance is wary of wall-clock CI flakiness;
  the correctness gate (`TestFusedAttention_matchesMaterialized`, already exercising the skip) is
  what protects the regression, and this measurement is recorded here rather than in a new test file.
- **P-03 · P18 allocates ~50 MB per 512-row chunk per layer.** `decoder/mlp.go:511`, `:514-515`,
  `:522`, `:532-535`, `decoder/forwardn.go:556`: `perRank` (n·k·hidden ≈ 37.7 MB at Mellum2 shapes),
  `gathered`/`gate`/`up`/`outBuf`, `logits`, `idxs`/`wtss` and `emOut` are fresh per chunk per layer —
  ~22 GB over one K=8192 prefill, the order of the 20.9 GB the per-row path was criticised for.
  Hoist to one per-prefill scratch sized `moeExpertMajorChunk`. The per-row attribution arm showed
  0.99–1.02× for allocation alone, so expect small but nonzero.

  **DISPOSITION — NOT IMPLEMENTED, evidence-based (2026-09-03).** `decoder/mlp.go:583-588`'s
  `moePrefillScratch`/`GOINFER_MOE_PREFILL_SCRATCH` already exists FOR exactly this question, on
  the structurally-analogous per-row `moeMLP` path: it isolates "stopped allocating per row" from
  "batched the matmuls" (P18's expert-major win), because moeMLP with a non-nil scratch "computes
  exactly what it computes with nil — no arithmetic changes." That IS "the per-row attribution arm"
  this item's own text cites: 0.99–1.02Γ— for allocation alone. `moeMLPBatch`'s chunk buffers
  (`perRank`/`gathered`/`gate`/`up`/`outBuf`/`logits`/`idxs`/`wtss`/`byExpert`) are the same shape
  of short-lived, allocate-write-read-discard churn, on the same GC — there is no mechanism by
  which hoisting THEM would behave differently from hoisting the per-row buffers already measured.

  Not re-measuring fresh: `maxRows` (three of the largest buffers: `gathered`/`gate`/`up`/`outBuf`)
  varies per chunk with the routing pattern, so a correct hoist needs real grow-to-max scratch in a
  MoE hot path that gpt-oss/GLM-MoE/Mellum2/gemma4-MoE all route through — real implementation risk,
  and (per the P-02/P-04/P-05 experience just above) another parity deps_hash refresh — for a return
  the project's own prior, structurally-equivalent measurement already puts at noise level. Citing
  that evidence directly rather than re-deriving it is the efficient call here; re-open if a future
  profile shows GC pressure specifically from this path dominating a real prefill.
- **P-04 · The batched path's `Workspace` keeps aikit's default fan-out threshold**
  (`decoder/forwardn.go:355`, never `SetThreshold`; decode uses 300K at `decoder/scratch.go:86-87`),
  so small-K `forwardN` (spec verify, K≤8) runs its q/k/v/gate/up batches serial where decode's
  identical M=1 matmul fans out. Measure verify latency per round at K=4/8 with
  `DefaultDecodeParallelThreshold` vs current.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-03.** One line: `ws.SetThreshold(DefaultDecodeParallelThreshold)`
  right after the `Workspace` is declared, mirroring `scratch.go`'s decode path exactly. Safe for
  the SAME `ws` at prefill-scale K too — large-K matmuls clear both aikit's 16.78M default and the
  300K threshold comfortably, so this only changes behavior for the small-K case it was missing;
  `tune.go`'s own doc comment records `thr=0` (always-parallel) showing no over-parallelize penalty
  on decode-shaped matmuls, which is what K≤8 batches are.

  Correctness: `TestForwardN_matchesSequential` stays bit-identical at K=6 and K=128 (aikit's
  parallel-width independence contract, not incidental — the same guarantee that already licensed
  `DefaultDecodeParallelThreshold` for decode). Measured (paired via git stash, same process, real
  auto-discovered checkpoint, 20 rounds/cell): K=4, 27.7ms β†’ 25.0ms; K=8, 45.3ms β†’ 41.3ms — a
  consistent ~10% per round in the expected direction at both K. Smaller than the raw
  parallel-vs-serial matmul ratio alone would suggest, because the qkv/gate-up batch is one part of
  a full forward (attention, norms, the rest of the layer stack) — the ~10% is what that fraction is
  worth end-to-end, not the kernel-level speedup.
- **P-05 · Per-slot `vt` is allocated and unused whenever the fused schedule runs**
  (`decoder/scratch.go:345-346`; `gatherKVFused` writes `vBlk`), and `prefillAttnWorkers`
  (`decoder/scratch.go:232-242`) charges `2·nKeys·hd` but not `vBlk`/`sBlk`/`tmp`/`acc` — real
  per-slot use is ~25% above the 256 MiB budget at 8k. Allocate one of `vt`/`vBlk` per mode.

  **DISPOSITION — PARTIALLY FIXED 2026-09-03: the budget-accounting half only.** Fixed the
  measurable complaint — `prefillAttnScratchBudget` oversubscription — without touching what
  `newHeadWorkerPool` allocates. `prefillAttnWorkers` now adds `fusedScratch`'s bytes
  (`sBlk+2·t·hd+2·t+hd·nKeys`) to `perSlot` whenever `GOINFER_FUSED_ATTENTION` is enabled, so the
  worker count it derives bounds what actually gets allocated.

  The audit's suggested fix — allocate `vt` XOR `vBlk` per mode — is real and would additionally
  eliminate `vt`'s ~2 MiB/slot outright (not just budget for it), but needs `useAcc64` and
  `cache.treeMask` at `newHeadWorkerPool`'s call site to decide correctly: `vt` is unused when fusion
  is ACTIVE, but also unused whenever `useAcc64` is true (the acc64 arm reads `vals` directly,
  skipping `vt` regardless of fusion) — and `scores` (the LARGER piece, `tile·nKeys` vs `vt`'s
  `nKeys·hd`) is unused whenever fusion is active REGARDLESS of `useAcc64`. Getting that three-way
  condition wrong risks a nil-slice access in the prefill hot path every model goes through — real
  risk for a fix `newHeadWorkerPool`'s current signature has no way to express safely. Deferred
  rather than rushed; the accounting fix already closes the concrete, measured claim.

  Verified: `TestPrefillAttnWorkerBudget_countsFusedScratch` recomputes the real per-slot footprint
  independently (not by re-reading `prefillAttnWorkers`' own formula) at shapes chosen so the
  BUDGET binds below `maxAttnWorkers`(6) — smaller shapes let the P-core cap bind first and would
  pass even with the bug present. At hd=128, K=nKeys=65536: reverted, 3 workers × 104.13 MB =
  312.38 MB, over the 256 MB budget (mutation-checked — this is what the mutation reproduces);
  fixed, 2 workers × 104.13 MB = 208.25 MB, within it. The pre-existing
  `TestPrefillAttnPoolInvariance`/`TestPrefillAttnRowTileInvariance` bit-identity gates and the rest
  of the decoder suite stay green — this only changes the worker COUNT, never what a worker
  computes.
- **P-06 · RoPE cos/sin recomputed per position per layer in the batched loop**
  (`decoder/rope.go:31-34`, `decoder/forwardn.go:424-427`): ~2 s of a ~250 s 8k prefill on a 1.5B;
  a per-prefill `[K][half]` table removes it. Sub-1%; measure before bothering.

  **DISPOSITION — MEASURED, NOT WORTH IT (2026-09-03).** Direct microbenchmark of just the
  `cos(theta)`/`sin(theta)` cost `applyRoPE` pays (28 layers Γ— K=8192 positions Γ— half=64, the
  audit's own 1.5B shape): **198 ms total — 0.08% of a 250 s prefill**, well under even the audit's
  own conservative 0.8% (~2s/250s) estimate. A per-prefill `[K][half]` cos/sin table would need
  threading through every RoPE call site and its variants (partial rotary, YaRN `mscale`, GPT-J
  interleaved) for a saving that doesn't clear noise on a real run. Not implemented — this is the
  "measure before bothering" the item asked for, and the measurement says don't.
- **P-07 · `gatedDeltaNetStep` walks the `[hk,hv]` state column-wise (stride `hv`) in its inner
  loops** (`decoder/deltanet.go:226-243`): with `hk=hv=128` each `vd` iteration touches 128 floats
  512 B apart, twice, so the head's 64 KB state streams 128× per token as cache lines, and nothing
  vectorises across the strided access. Interchanging to `kd`-outer with `kv[hv]`/`delta[hv]`
  scratch keeps every per-output reduction in the same `kd`-ascending order (bit-identical) and
  makes the inner loop contiguous. `docs/task-zeno-compare.md` puts this step at ~19% of a decode
  token; the `GOINFER_DELTANET_TIMING` seam (`decoder/deltanet.go:21-60`) reports the number to
  compare. Measure on Qwen3.5-35B-A3B paired, then `TestGatedDeltaNet_parity` +
  `_chunkedMatchesSequential` for identity.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-03.** Interchanged to `kd`-outer/`vd`-inner exactly as suggested:
  the first reduction (`kv`) accumulates into a small scratch walking `S` row-major
  (`S[kd*hv:kd*hv+hv]`, contiguous); `delta` is computed from it and aliases the same backing
  array (each index read once then overwritten once β€” safe); the second pass updates `S` and
  accumulates `out` the same way. Both reductions still sum their `kd=0..hk-1` contributions in
  ascending order for a fixed `vd`, and `S`'s columns are disjoint across `vd`, so processing every
  `vd`'s read-phase before any write-phase (rather than interleaved per `vd`, as the original did)
  changes nothing about the arithmetic.

  Correctness proven at TWO levels: `TestGatedDeltaNet_parity`/`_chunkedMatchesSequential` (the
  audit's own asks) stay green with IDENTICAL printed precision before/after (`maxAbs=2.09e-07
  cosine=1.00000000`, mutation-checked via git stash). More rigorously, a standalone comparison of
  the old and new inner-loop bodies on identical random inputs at hk/hv shapes including the real
  128Γ—128 gives EXACT float32 `==` equality (not just tolerance) across every output and state
  element β€” written and run, not committed as a permanent test since the existing parity gates
  already exercise the production path going forward and a frozen side-by-side of two hand-copied
  snippets would drift from it silently.

  Performance: the 35B checkpoint is present on this Mac but was NOT loaded for this measurement β€”
  17 GB RAM against a real Qwen3.5-35B-A3B decode risks the kind of resource pressure this session
  already hit once loading a real DeepSeek checkpoint concurrently (G-09's webgpu-suite finding).
  Instead, measured the inner loop directly in isolation at the audit's own cited real dimensions
  (hk=hv=128, 200k iterations): **1.53Γ— (34.2ΞΌs β†’ 22.4ΞΌs per call)**. Applying the audit's own
  ~19% figure (`docs/task-zeno-compare.md`) through Amdahl's law gives β‰ˆ7% end-to-end decode β€” real,
  not the noise-level P-01/P-03/P-06 outcomes. Not the full real-model paired measurement the item
  asked for; a genuine gap, left open rather than claimed.
- **P-08 · Mamba-2 in/out projections stay f32 on the CPU path** (`decoder/mamba2.go:44`, `:51`,
  `:84`, `:158` — `[]float32` through `matvec`), the same 4× byte-stream the DeltaNet fix of
  2026-08-19 removed (`decoder/deltanet.go:77-84` records "22.1 GB per token across 48 DeltaNet
  layers, against ~9.5 GB for the whole int4 FFN"). Nemotron-H is the CPU family this hits hardest.
  `ssmQ8CPU`/`ssmQ8WeightsOnly` (`decoder/mamba2.go:76-83`, `:167-174`) already instrument the
  precision question. Measure granite/nemotron CPU decode f32 vs W8A8 mamba projections, paired,
  with the real-model oracle cosine as the accuracy arm.

  **DISPOSITION β€” NOT IMPLEMENTED, deferred on real risk (2026-09-03).** This is the same class of
  change as 6d4fc79 (qwen35's DeltaNet/qattn f32β†’WeightMat conversion, 2026-08-19) β€” and that
  change is the exact one this session spent a multi-commit bisection diagnosing after it silently
  broke `TestQwen35GGUF_vsSafetensors` for two weeks (see this doc's own account earlier). Doing
  the same for Mamba-2 means the same shape of risk: loader changes (GGUF + safetensors) routing
  `inProj`/`outProj` through `quantizeWM`, a forward-pass change (`matvec` β†’ a WeightMat-aware
  call), a `serialize.go` .giw writer/reader update, AND retroactively moving `mamba2Weights`'
  contribution in `ResidentWeightBytes`/`ResidentWeightBytesPaged` from `f32SliceBytes` to
  `wmBytes` (this session's own M-01/M-02 work).

  `ssmQ8CPU`/`ssmQ8WeightsOnly` already give a REAL accuracy confirmation seam β€” int8
  weights+activations or int8-weights/f32-activations, matching the resident W8A8 path exactly β€”
  but exercising it meaningfully needs the granite/nemotron REAL-model oracle tests
  (`decoder/granite_real_test.go`, `decoder/nemotron_real_test.go`), which need real checkpoints
  not present on this machine (only `testdata/granite-tiny`/`nemotron-tiny`, shape-only synthetic
  fixtures too small to say anything about a real quantization-noise question, the same "minimal
  repro can hide the defect" trap this project's own CLAUDE.md names). Implementing and shipping a
  change of this shape on synthetic-fixture confidence alone, when the DIRECTLY ANALOGOUS change
  already fooled a real-checkpoint gate for two weeks, is not a risk worth taking without the
  infrastructure to actually validate it. Genuinely open, not evidence-based like P-03/P-06 β€”
  revisit with the CUDA/Linux box or real granite/nemotron checkpoints in reach.
- **P-09 · `gemma4Attend` re-gathers and transposes every KV head's full history per layer per
  token** (`decoder/forward_gemma4.go:301-310`): O(nKeys·hd) copies × nKV × layers on top of the
  attention itself — a 2–3× traffic multiplier at long context. Store V transposed at append time.
  Only worth it if Gemma-4 CPU decode is a target.

  **DISPOSITION β€” NOT IMPLEMENTED, contingent (2026-09-03).** Confirmed the mechanism:
  `gemma4Attend` (`decoder/forward_gemma4.go`) gathers+transposes V from `KVCache` into its own
  `g4attnScratch` on every call, family-specific (not the shared `attendBatchedHeads` path). The
  fix means `KVCache.Append` storing V pre-transposed β€” a layout every gemma4 code path touching
  the cache (prefill, decode, and any resident bridge reading the same cache) would need to agree
  on, real structural surface for a family-specific CPU path. The item's own text already poses the
  precondition as a question ("only worth it IF Gemma-4 CPU decode is a target") that is a product
  priority call, not something resolvable by measurement alone. Not implemented pending that call.

**Loaders and paging**

- **P-10 · Every `.giw` load CRCs the entire bundle before touching a layer**
  (`decoder/serialize.go:313-313`, `decoder/model.go:221-222`; `internal/prequant/prequant.go:244-252`
  does it again right after writing). For a >RAM `--stream-weights` bundle the whole file is read
  from disk at every start (NVMe ~10 s; the 5400 rpm archive minutes) and the pages it pulls are
  exactly the ones paging then evicts. Per-layer CRCs in the layer tail (or a Merkle of them in the
  header) keep the guarantee and make verification lazy. Measure cold-page-cache TTFT with the CRC vs
  header-only.

  **DISPOSITION β€” NOT IMPLEMENTED, out of proportionate scope here (2026-09-03).** Confirmed the
  mechanism at `decoder/serialize.go:313-313`: `crc32.ChecksumIEEE(body)` over the entire payload
  minus the trailing 4 bytes, which for a paged/mmap `--stream-weights` bundle means reading every
  byte into memory at load just to checksum it, defeating the point of paging. The fix is a real
  ON-DISK FORMAT change β€” a new `.giw` version (this repo's history shows v4/v6/v8 tail additions
  were each their own deliberate undertaking), writer changes to compute and place per-layer CRCs
  (or a header Merkle), reader changes to verify lazily per-layer as read rather than eagerly at
  open, and backward-compat handling for every already-written bundle at the current version. Not
  a local-loop fix comparable to P-02/P-04/P-05/P-07.

  Also could not properly measure the claim here: it specifically needs a COLD page cache (this
  Mac has no reliable way to drop it without `sudo purge`, and CLAUDE.md's own storage rules
  forbid ever benchmarking off the slow archive disk the item's "5400 rpm archive minutes" figure
  references β€” the two constraints point at the same trap from opposite directions). Genuinely
  open; revisit as its own scoped format-version task, not folded into this pass.
- **P-11 · `loadFusedExperts` materialises whole 3-D f32 expert tensors per layer, × GOMAXPROCS**
  (`decoder/weights.go:1081-1113`, callers `:870` and `:1842`; contrast `streamExperts` at
  `:1188-1212`, one expert at a time): Qwen3.6-35B-A3B ≈ 3 GB transient per in-flight layer → ~25 GB
  at 8 workers on top of the resident int4. Measure peak RSS of `Load(int4)` at GOMAXPROCS=1 vs 8,
  before/after per-expert `SubF32`. The gpt-oss safetensors loader is the opposite — single-threaded
  (`decoder/gptoss_safetensors.go:97`; every other loader uses `parallelLayers`).

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-03.** Rewrote `loadFusedExperts` to mirror the pattern
  `loadGemma4MoE`/`streamExperts` already use for gemma4's fused experts: `st.TensorF32(name)`
  (eager, materializes the whole 3-D tensor) replaced with `st.Tensor(name)` (lazy handle) +
  per-expert `guT.SubF32(e*guStride, guStride)` / `dnT.SubF32(e*downStride, downStride)`, so only
  one expert's f32 is ever live per read instead of the whole `[nExpert, 2·inter, hidden]` +
  `[nExpert, hidden, inter]` pair. Signature unchanged, so both call sites (`:870` qwen3_5_moe,
  `:1842` Mixtral's `block_sparse_moe`) needed no edit. Added explicit `Elements()` shape
  validation (matching `streamExperts`' own style) since the eager `TensorF32` path used to get
  that check for free from its own length assertion.

  Correctness: **neither existing forward-parity test actually exercises this function.**
  `TestQwen35_forwardParity`'s and `TestMixtral_forwardParity`'s tiny fixtures
  (`testdata/qwen35-tiny`, `testdata/mixtral-tiny`) both store *unfused* per-expert tensors
  (`experts.N.gate_proj.weight` / `block_sparse_moe.experts.N.w1.weight`), so `fusedExperts` at
  `:584` is false for both and the pre-existing unfused-loader branch runs instead — confirmed by
  mutating the new `SubF32` offset math (`e*guStride+7`, then `e*guStride+99999`) and rerunning
  both tests: identical PASS, identical printed cosine, in both cases. This is exactly the
  calling-convention trap this repo's CLAUDE.md warns about: the two tests I ran first "because
  they touch the callers" were vouching for code they never reached. Wrote a third, targeted test —
  `TestLoadFusedExperts_perExpertSlicing` (`decoder/loadfusedexperts_test.go`) — that builds a
  synthetic fused `gate_up_proj`/`down_proj` safetensors pair via the existing `writeSafetensors`
  helper, tags every (expert, position) with a unique value, and asserts each expert's `Gate`/`Up`/
  `Down` reads back exactly the slice it should. Mutation-checked against the same two offset bugs:
  both are now caught (`SubF32 range [103,151) out of bounds`), and pass cleanly on the real fix.
  Added `TestLoadFusedExperts_shapeMismatch` for the new `Elements()` guard. `go test ./decoder/...
  -v` green throughout (Mixtral's own `cosine=NaN` is pre-existing — same value on unmodified
  `weights.go` — not introduced here).

  Peak-RSS reduction at real Qwen3.6-35B-A3B scale, the measurement the audit asked for, was NOT
  attempted: loading that checkpoint risks the same resource pressure flagged for P-07/P-08 on this
  17 GB Mac (G-09's webgpu-suite OOM). The mechanism is the same one `streamExperts`' own doc
  comment already banks a peak-transient reduction for at gemma4 scale; not independently
  re-measured here. A genuine gap, left open rather than claimed.
- **P-12 · Kind-4 paged bundles fault the canonical copy outside the pager's budget on any M>1
  path** (`decoder/moepaging.go:61-79` registers row4 OR canonical, never both;
  `decoder/serialize.go:1265-1277` aliases both): P18 expert-major prefill (default ON) reads the
  canonical bytes, which are demand-faulted and never accounted or released by `SpanCache`. Correct
  output, wrong budget; `advisedBytes()` cannot see it. Measure RSS during a 4k-token prefill on a
  kind-4 vs kind-3 bundle at the same `WeightCacheBytes`. (`layerPager`'s window is also one layer
  over budget by construction — `decoder/layerpaging.go:107-114`, `:119-137`: resident set is
  `window+ahead`.)

  **DISPOSITION — main claim NOT REACHABLE; secondary claim FIXED (2026-09-03).** The main claim's
  premise doesn't hold: `moeMLPBatch` (P18's M>1 batched path) refuses outright whenever a pager is
  live — `if moe == nil || len(lw.Experts) == 0 || moeSelOverride != nil || moeSelTrace != nil ||
  pager != nil { return false, nil }` (`decoder/mlp.go:493`) — and the call site
  (`decoder/forwardn.go:559`) passes `m.pager` straight through, so a live expert pager forces every
  row in the chunk onto the per-row `moeMLP` fallback (`break // refused: leave every row to the
  per-row path below`). The per-row path is always M==1 (confirmed by the pager's own registration
  comment: "the M==1 decode kernel reads ONLY row4... this arch's forward is always M==1, decode and
  prefill alike"), which is exactly what the pager registered under `MappedSpanRow4`. So a kind-4
  paged bundle and P18's batched canonical-reading path are mutually exclusive by construction — the
  described interaction cannot occur. Same shape as the earlier "spec × expert-pager" finding: verify
  two features can actually run together before trusting a bug story about their interaction.

  The parenthetical secondary claim is real and independent of the above: confirmed by tracing
  `layerPager.enterLayer` — it prefetches `l+ahead` before evicting `l-window`, so steady-state
  residency is `window+ahead` layers, not `window`. `finishLayers`' own doc comment claimed residency
  "stays bounded by `window`" — false against the mechanism it describes. Reproduced directly:
  `TestLayerPager_residentBytesWithinBudget` drives `enterLayer` across a real 24-layer dense .giw
  (`prequantGGUF`'s qwen2.5-coder-0.5b fixture) at a 128 MiB budget and measured peak resident
  **133.2 MB against a 128 MB `window`-derived subset — the `window=9` case overshoots to 147.9 MB**,
  i.e. one `maxLayer` (14.79 MB here) over budget regardless of window size, matching the trace.
  Fixed with the one-line change the trace implies: `window := max(int(budget/maxLayer)-ahead,
  ahead+2)`, so the budget-derived component already accounts for the `ahead` layer that stays
  resident concurrently; the `ahead+2` correctness floor (never evict a layer just prefetched) is
  untouched. Re-measured post-fix at the same 128 MiB budget: window drops to 8, peak resident 133.2
  MB — within budget. Mutation-checked (`git stash` the fix, same test, same budget: FAILS,
  147.9 MB > 134.2 MB budget). `TestLayerPaging_bitExact` still passes byte-identical (window shrinks
  by exactly one, still ≥ the `ahead+2` floor for correctness) — the fix only tightens the budget
  accounting, not the streaming mechanism itself. `gofmt`/`go vet`/`staticcheck` clean.

**Sampling and speculation**

- **P-13 · `computeLogprobs` full-sorts the vocabulary per token whenever `top_logprobs > 0`**
  (`decoder/sampler.go:376-399`; also disables the on-device argmax path, `:167`, `:191`): O(V log V)
  with two V-sized allocations per token, ~10–20 ms of host work at V=152k against a ~3 ms CUDA
  step; `sort.Slice` is unstable so tied top-logprobs report in arbitrary order. `topKByLogit`
  (`:539`) exists. Measure tok/s with `logprobs:true, top_logprobs:5` vs false.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-03.** Replaced the full-vocab `[]indexedProb` build +
  `sort.Slice` with `topKByLogit(logits, topN)` (O(V·log N)) followed by a final sort of just the
  N-element result. Ranking by logit agrees with ranking by prob since softmax is strictly monotone
  in the logit at a fixed positive temperature, so the selected index SET is identical to the full
  sort's; only the tiny final ordering step remains. Made the final sort's tie-break deterministic
  (smaller id wins, matching `topKByLogit`'s own convention) rather than leaving it to `sort.Slice`'s
  unspecified behavior on ties, which the audit item itself flagged as a pre-existing wart —
  genuinely fixed, not just relocated: `TestComputeLogprobs_matchesFullSort`'s explicit tie case
  (`logits = {0,5,1,5,2}`) caught my own first pass leaving the tie-break non-deterministic in the
  final sort even though the index SET was already correct, and would fail again on either kind of
  regression.

  Correctness: `TestComputeLogprobs_matchesFullSort` checks 50 random-logit trials × 5 `topN` values
  (including `topN >= vocab`) against `referenceTopLogprobs`, an independent full-sort oracle, plus
  the deliberate tie case above. Mutation-checked twice — corrupting the `topKByLogit` count
  (off-by-one) and flipping the final sort's comparator — both caught (index panic, wrong order).
  `TestSampler_logprobs` (pre-existing, end-to-end through `Sampler.SampleWithInfo`) still passes.

  Performance: isolated benchmark at the audit's own cited V=152k, `topN=5` (written and run, not
  committed — the existing correctness test already exercises the production path going forward):
  **24.96 ms → 1.61 ms, 15.5×**, confirming the audit's "~10-20 ms" estimate for the old path
  directly. Not re-measured as end-to-end tok/s against a live CUDA step (the audit's own ask) —
  this function runs on the host regardless of backend, so the isolated number is the load-bearing
  one; a genuine gap for the tok/s framing specifically, left open rather than claimed.
  `gofmt`/`go vet`/`staticcheck` clean.
- **P-14 · Sampled n-gram speculation allocates two full-vocab float64 vectors per verify position**
  (`decoder/spec_sample.go:37`, `:42`; `:61` clones logits per position; called from
  `decoder/spec_ngram.go:405`, `:421`, `:312`): 11–22 MB of garbage per round at V=152k. A per-sampler
  scratch suffices. Measure GC count and tok/s at T=0.7 `--spec ngram` on CUDA vs T=0, with off.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-03.** Added two per-`*Sampler` scratch fields (`distBuf`,
  `specLogitsBuf`) mirroring the pre-existing `vocabBuf` pattern and its own justification: verified
  by reading every consumer (`specStep`, `drawResidual`, `drawDist`, `traceFromDist`) that the
  returned distribution vector is only ever read synchronously within one verify-loop iteration
  before the next call — `SpecTrace` has no `[]float64` field, so tracing doesn't retain it either.
  `distVectorFrom`'s two `make([]float64, len(logits))` sites (the greedy point-mass and the
  top-filtered scatter) now pull from `distBufN`; `distVectorHist`'s `slices.Clone(logits)` now pulls
  a `[]float32` from `specLogitsBufN`. Left `softmaxStable`'s own internal allocation (the
  no-filter branch) untouched — it's shared infra used well beyond speculative verify, and threading
  an output buffer through it is a materially bigger, riskier change than this item's scope.

  Mutation-checking this surfaced a real gap before it shipped: the existing speculative suite
  (`TestNgramSpeculativeGreedyParity`, `TestNgramSampledHarness`, etc.) all still passed with either
  of the two `clear()` calls deleted, because every fixture's logit set keeps the same support shape
  call to call — never actually exercising whether stale mass from a shrunk/moved support leaks
  through the reused buffer. Wrote `TestDistVectorFrom_scratchClearedAcrossPositions`, which drives
  two consecutive calls with deliberately DISJOINT argmax/kept sets and asserts every index outside
  the current call's support reads exactly zero; it fails with a clear diagnostic on either `clear()`
  removed, and passes clean on the real fix. Full speculative/sampler suite green.

  Performance: isolated allocation benchmark (`distVector` at V=152k, `TopK=40`, written and run, not
  committed) — **1,224,023 B/op → 5,842 B/op (209×), 3 allocs/op → 2** (the two remaining allocs are
  `topFilterLogits`' bounded-by-`topK` candidate slices, not full-vocab). Confirms the audit's
  "11–22 MB of garbage per round" was real and is now essentially eliminated on the steady-state
  path. Not re-measured as GC count / tok/s at T=0.7 `--spec ngram` on CUDA specifically (the audit's
  own ask) — this is host-side allocation pressure independent of backend, so the isolated number is
  the load-bearing one; the CUDA tok/s framing specifically is a genuine gap, left open.
  `gofmt`/`go vet`/`staticcheck` clean.
- **P-15 · `forEachChunk` spawns GOMAXPROCS goroutines and a channel per call, twice per sampled
  token** (`decoder/sampler_chunked.go:110-136`, from `:52` and `:86`); ~1% of a 3 ms step. Penalties
  rebuild a `map[int]int` over the whole history every token (`decoder/sampler.go:340-346`,
  `:268-276`) — O(n²) over a generation with `presence_penalty` on an 8k prompt. Every batched
  verify allocates its full layer scratch (`decoder/forwardn.go:222-211`, `:298`, `:1059-1064`; EAGLE
  up to three times per round).

  **DISPOSITION — three separate sub-claims: NOT WORTH IT, FIXED, NOT IMPLEMENTED (2026-09-03).**

  **(1) `forEachChunk` goroutine/channel overhead — measured, not worth it.** Isolated the
  coordination cost alone (a no-op `forEachChunk` at V=152k vs. a sequential loop over the same 64
  chunks): **~6-9 µs vs ~25 ns** — real but tiny, and it runs twice per sampled token
  (`parallelMax` + `expChunked`). Against a full `Sample()` call at the same V (~380 µs, measured for
  P-14), that is under ~5%; against the audit's own cited "3 ms step" it is well under 1%, matching
  the audit's own estimate directly rather than just trusting it. `forEachChunk` already has a
  small-vocab sequential bailout (`workers <= 1 || n < numChunks*64`), so the remaining cost is
  already gated to the case where the parallel win is real. Not changed.

  **(2) Penalty map rebuild — FIXED.** Confirmed the mechanism: `penaltyWindow()` returns the WHOLE
  history whenever `RepeatLastN ≤ 0` (the default), and `applyPenaltiesOver` rebuilt a fresh
  `map[int]int` by rescanning that window from scratch on every call — O(n) per token against a
  history that grows by one token per step, O(n²) over a generation, exactly as the audit describes.
  Added `histCounts map[int]int`, maintained incrementally through a single chokepoint
  (`recordHistory`, `decoder/sampler.go:147-153`) that both history-mutation sites now go through
  (`Observe`, and `SampleWithInfo`'s own post-draw record — previously two independent `append`
  call sites, a real desync risk caught by mutation-checking below). `applyPenalties`
  (`decoder/sampler.go:328-337`) now feeds `histCounts` directly to a new
  `applyPenaltiesFromCounts` helper when `RepeatLastN ≤ 0`, skipping the rescan entirely; the
  windowed case (`RepeatLastN > 0`, a small bounded window, not the O(n²) shape) keeps the original
  rebuild via `applyPenaltiesOver`, now just a thin wrapper over the same shared helper.

  Correctness: `TestApplyPenalties_incrementalMatchesRebuild` drives a mixed sequence of `Observe`
  calls (the prompt-seed shape) and a real `Sample` call (the per-token auto-record shape — the
  second history-mutation site) with all three penalty kinds active, and after every step compares
  the fast path's logit output against `applyPenaltiesOver` called directly on a fresh rescan of
  `s.history`, bit for bit. Mutation-checked by reintroducing a raw `s.history = append(...)` at the
  `SampleWithInfo` record site (bypassing `recordHistory`): caught immediately, diverging on the very
  next step (`fast[2]=1.6076922` vs `slow[2]=1.4576924`) — this is the exact desync class the single
  chokepoint exists to prevent, and the test would have caught it whether or not I'd noticed it by
  reading first. Full sampler/speculative suite green. Also corrected a pre-existing stale citation
  found in passing: `:159-163` pointed at `ArgmaxEquivalent` (unrelated), not `penaltyWindow`'s
  "whole history when `RepeatLastN ≤ 0`" comment it was presumably meant to support — retargeted to
  `:268-276`, the actual `penaltyWindow` definition. `gofmt`/`go vet`/`staticcheck` clean.

  **(3) Batched-verify full layer scratch — NOT IMPLEMENTED, out of proportionate scope here.**
  `runLayersFromEmbedN` allocates nine separate `K`-sized scratch slices (`norm`, `q`, `k`, `v`,
  `ctx`, `att`, `gate`, `up`, `mlpOut`) per call. Back-of-envelope at a typical block-spec draft depth
  (K=8, hidden=4096, inter=11008, GQA `kvDim`≈1024): roughly ~1.4 MB per call — real, and called from
  three sites with materially different lifetimes: `blockspec_cpu.go`'s EAGLE/block-spec verify
  (`PrefillLastNArgmax`, per-round) and two multimodal prefill paths (Gemma3-VL, Qwen2.5-VL,
  once-per-prompt with `K` = the whole prompt, not a draft depth). A correct fix needs a scratch
  struct scoped to `*KVCache` (the natural single-owner lifetime, since `StreamWeights` supports
  concurrent streams and a `*Model`-scoped buffer would race across them), grown on demand across
  the WIDELY varying `K` these three callers pass, verified safe across all three call sites and
  mutation-checked across nine buffers — a substantially larger, riskier undertaking than either of
  the two fixes above, closer in shape to P-05's worker-pool scratch than to P-14's two-buffer case.
  Deferred as its own scoped follow-up rather than folded into this pass; a genuine gap, left open
  rather than claimed.
- **P-16 · `GenerateNgramSpeculativeAdaptive` at `Theta ≥ 1`** (`decoder/spec_adaptive.go:82-84`,
  `Depth()` constant 0) still pays `slices.Clone(hist)` + the n-gram scan
  (`decoder/spec_ngram.go:358-355`) + a 1-row `ForwardN` with full-logits readback instead of the
  greedy `ForwardArgmax` path per token; the Metal A/B (2062a1b) measured 1.01× of off at one
  prompt and the residual grows with context. Decline (fall back to plain `Generate`) when
  `Theta ≥ 1`.

  **DISPOSITION — FIXED 2026-09-03.** Both entry points —
  `Model.GenerateNgramSpeculativeAdaptive` (`decoder/spec_adaptive.go:124-147`) and
  `Session.GenerateNgramSpeculativeAdaptive` (`decoder/session.go:176-196`) — now check
  `ad.Theta >= 1` right after resolving it and, when true, validate the request exactly as
  `genNgram`/`genSpec` would (`validateNgramSpec`, so an invalid drafter or `SamplingParams` still
  errors identically) and then dispatch straight to plain `Generate`/`Session.Generate` instead of
  `genNgram`/`genSpec`. `Depth()`'s own comment already explains why this is safe: at `Theta >= 1`
  it returns 0 for every `proposedLen`/`alpha` combination, so no round would ever draft anything —
  every call was paying for a history clone, an n-gram scan, and a full-logits `ForwardN` to verify
  zero draft tokens. `Session.Generate` reuses the warm KV prefix exactly as `genSpec` does, so the
  decline loses no session behavior.

  Correctness: `TestNgramAdaptiveThetaAtLeastOne_declinesToGenerate` (Model) and
  `TestSessionNgramAdaptiveThetaAtLeastOne_declinesToGenerate` (Session) each check TWO things —
  output is identical to plain `Generate` at `Theta=1`, and a `countingDrafter` wrapper proves
  `Draft` is never called (the n-gram scan genuinely never runs), plus that a `nil` drafter at
  `Theta=1` still errors rather than silently succeeding through the decline path. Mutation-checked
  on both: reverting either fix makes its test fail with the drafter called 15 times instead of 0.
  The pre-existing `TestNgramAdaptiveGreedyParity`/`TestSessionNgramSpecParity` (which exercise the
  normal `Theta < 1` path, unaffected by this change) still pass. Full decoder suite green.
  `gofmt`/`go vet`/`staticcheck` clean.

**Serving**

- **P-17 · `streamTokens` re-decoded the whole generated sequence and rescanned it for stop
  strings on every token** (`internal/serveapp/openai.go`, pre-R-08 shape; same pattern still
  present in `internal/chatapp/main.go:349-352`, `internal/gemmaapp/main.go:131-146`,
  `demo/agent/agent/agent.go:445-462`): O(n²) in output length, ~1–2 ms/token near the end of a
  64k-token reply on a resident GPU. Decode incrementally (`DecodePiece` exists since the 2026-08-05 audit's M-13) and scan
  the new tail plus `max(len(stop))−1` bytes — fixed on the `openai.go` path 2026-09-03, see the
  update below. The decoder-as-embedder runs one forward per token
  (`decoder/embed.go:101-106`, the "sequential prefill" the flags call ~9× slower) and tokenized every
  input twice at what was then `internal/serveapp/embeddings.go:122` and `:251-258` — both since
  fixed and restructured; see the 2026-09-03 update below for where the mechanism lives now.

  **DISPOSITION — decoder-as-embedder sub-claim FIXED (2026-09-03); `streamTokens`'s re-decode
  FIXED on the serving hot path (2026-09-03, update below); the demo-CLI copies remain
  deliberately deferred.**

  **Fixed: the decoder-as-embedder ran a sequential per-token forward.** `HiddenLast`
  (`decoder/embed.go:36-45`) called `runLayers` once per token in a loop, never using the batched
  prefill path (`runLayersFromEmbedN`) plain generation's own prompt phase already uses. Split it
  into `hiddenLastBatched` (`:83-92`, one `runLayersFromEmbedN` call over the whole sequence, taking
  the last row — already past the final norm per that function's own contract, so this must NOT
  normalize again) and `hiddenLastSequential` (`:97-113`, the original per-token loop, kept as the
  fallback for `K==1` and the families `canBatchN` excludes — `NonGatedMLP`/`LearnedPosEmbed`/
  own-`runLayers`). `HiddenLast` picks between them via the same `canBatchN` gate every other
  batched-prefill caller already uses.

  Correctness: `TestHiddenLast_batchedMatchesSequential` runs both paths on the same real Qwen3-0.6B
  checkpoint (Qwen3-Embedding-0.6B's base architecture) at four sequence lengths and compares them
  directly — **cosine 1.0000000000, max|diff| ≈ 0 at every length tested (2, 3, 5, 12 tokens)**.
  Mutation-checked twice: pulling the wrong row (first instead of last) dropped cosine to
  0.15-0.27 — caught; re-adding the exact double-normalization bug this codebase has already named
  elsewhere (`blockspec_cpu.go`'s own comment on the same class of mistake) dropped cosine to
  ~0.91-0.92 — also caught, confirming the "don't normalize twice" contract is actually load-bearing
  and not just asserted. All four pre-existing `TestHiddenLast_*` tests (shape/determinism/pooling,
  guards, context-window refusal) still pass unchanged.

  Performance: isolated benchmark at K=64 on the same real checkpoint (written and run, not
  committed): **sequential 5.52s → batched ~0.38-0.45s, ~12-14×** — beating the audit's own ~9×
  estimate. `gofmt`/`go vet`/`staticcheck` clean.

  **Update (2026-09-03, from `docs/task-recompute-audit.md` R-08): `streamTokens`'s O(n²)
  re-decode is now FIXED, on the serving hot path.** The mechanism was as described —
  `lm.tk.DecodeContinuation(ids)` (`internal/serveapp/openai.go`) re-decoded the WHOLE generated
  slice from scratch every token — but the concern this disposition raised about a drop-in
  `DecodePiece` swap turned out to be MORE cautious than the code actually required. Tracing
  `decode()`'s per-token loop (`tokenizer/sentencepiece.go`) directly: a byte-fallback token's raw
  byte is written out whenever a flush lands (a non-byte-fallback token, or end of input), and
  that flush is an UNVALIDATED raw-byte write — no UTF-8 interpretation happens in `decode()`
  itself (that's what `completeUTF8` is for, separately, at the SERVING layer). So splitting a
  decode call at ANY token boundary, including mid-byte-fallback-run, changes only WHEN a flush
  happens, never WHAT gets written — concatenation is associative everywhere, not just at
  non-byte-fallback boundaries. No `IsByteFallback`/restart-boundary tracker was needed; a plain
  `DecodePiece(id)` appended per token is byte-identical to `DecodeContinuation` of the whole
  sequence. Proven directly, not just argued (`tokenizer/TestDecodeContinuation_isIncrementallyAssociative`,
  a multi-byte-emoji byte-fallback run split at every possible point). The SECOND invariant this
  disposition named — windowing `completeUTF8`/`stopTailHold`/`firstStop` to the tail instead of
  the full text — was implemented and checked the same way: `text[:printed]` provably never
  contains a stop match (complete or in progress), via `stopTailHold`'s own hold-back invariant,
  and `TestStreamTokens_windowedScanMatchesFullRescan` runs 300 random token streams (split
  matches, near-misses, a 3-token-split match) through both the new and old algorithms, comparing
  byte-for-byte. Both new tests mutation-checked. The existing static M-25 guard
  (`TestStreamTokens_decodesAsAContinuation`) needed updating to check for `DecodePiece` instead
  of `DecodeContinuation` — re-verified by mutation. Full `internal/serveapp` suite green.
  **`chatapp`/`gemmaapp`/`agent.go` are NOT touched** — this disposition's own prioritization
  ("lower priority once the mechanism is proven correct in `openai.go`") still applies; their loops
  have no stop-string logic at all, and their whole-sequence `Decode(out)` call is semantically
  CORRECT there (no M-25 mixup — `out` is exactly the response's own ids), so the same fix would
  need its own design pass rather than porting over unchanged. See `task-recompute-audit.md`'s
  R-08 for the full account.

  **Update (2026-09-03, from `docs/task-recompute-audit.md` R-07): embeddings double-tokenize is
  now FIXED.** Confirmed the traced mechanism was exact:
  `decoderEmbedder.encodeLocked` (`internal/serveapp/decoder_embedder.go`) tokenizes to get `ids`,
  uses them for the forward, and discards them; `CountTokens` then re-tokenized the SAME text from
  scratch purely to report `usage.prompt_tokens`. Fixed with a new optional capability,
  `embedBatchCounter` (`EncodeBatchCounted`, mirroring the existing `embedTokenCounter` pattern),
  that the decoder-backed embedder implements by returning `len(ids)` from the same `encodeLocked`
  call already made for the vector — no second tokenize. `handleEmbeddings`
  (`internal/serveapp/embeddings.go`) prefers it when the embedder implements it, falling back to
  the original `EncodeBatch` + `countEmbedTokens` for encoders that can't — the aikit
  `encoder.Encoder` interface's `EncodeBatch` itself is external and can't be changed to return
  counts, but unlike this doc's own aikit-blocked items, the fix here needed no aikit change at
  all, since it lives entirely in goinfer's own `decoderEmbedder` wrapper. Gated by
  `TestDecoderEmbedder_encodeBatchCountedMatchesSeparateCalls` (heavy: real Qwen3-0.6B checkpoint;
  vectors bit-identical to `EncodeBatch`, counts identical to `CountTokens`) and
  `TestHandleEmbeddings_prefersEncodeBatchCounted` (portable: a counting fake proves the handler
  dispatches to the byproduct path and never calls the two-pass fallback). Both mutation-checked.
  Full `internal/serveapp` suite green (133 pass / 0 fail / 27 skip).
- **P-18 · The session LRU's whole-containment rule turns a stop-string hit into a full cold prefill
  plus an eviction** (`internal/serveapp/sessions.go:136-157`, `:155-164`;
  `internal/serveapp/openai.go:1069` (shifted by the 2026-09-03 R-08 rewrite; `ids = append(ids, id)`
  still runs before the stop check) commits the stop-string tokens the client never sees;
  `decoder/session.go:73-80` could reuse a partial prefix but is never asked). Measure TTFT of turn 3
  in a 3-turn chat with `"stop":["\n\n"]` vs without at 2k history. Llama-3's live `Today Date:`
  preamble (`chat/templates.go:197`, `:202`) also invalidates every warm session at midnight — worth
  documenting rather than changing.

  **DISPOSITION — CONFIRMED and MEASURED; the fix is L-15's scope by the audit's own design
  (2026-09-03).** Both mechanisms check out exactly as described. `bestExtend`
  (`internal/serveapp/sessions.go:165-174`) only reuses a session when its ENTIRE stored token list
  is a prefix of the new prompt (`n == commonPrefix(toks, prompt)`) — there is no partial-prefix
  path. `streamTokens` (`internal/serveapp/openai.go:1069`) appends every generated id to `ids`
  BEFORE checking for a stop-string hit, and only `text[printed:cut]` — up to the cut, not the
  whole thing — reaches the client via `onText`; the session's eventual `commit`/`reconcile` records
  the full `ids`, invisible tail included. So a session that just hit a stop string stores tokens the
  client's next prompt structurally cannot contain, `bestExtend` always misses on that session next
  turn, and `acquire` falls through to a fresh cold-prefill slot plus an eviction of the just-used
  session — confirmed directly with `TestBestExtend_stopStringTokensForceColdPrefill`
  (`internal/serveapp/sessions_test.go`): a session with two invisible post-stop tokens appended
  returns `bestExtend() == -1` against the exact next-turn prompt a real client would send.

  Measured the TTFT cost this forces, using the real production prefill path (`prefillLogits`,
  batched via `canBatchN`/`forwardLayersN` where it applies — not the sequential fallback) on a real
  checkpoint at the audit's own cited 2k-token scale: **cold prefill (n=2048) 11.07 s vs. a warm
  1-token continuation 74.6 ms — 148×**. This is CPU-backed and the small qwen2.5-coder-0.5b fixture
  (the only real checkpoint available without loading something larger on this box), so the absolute
  seconds don't transfer to a resident GPU deployment, but the qualitative shape — a stop-string hit
  turns a ~tens-of-ms continuation into a full prompt-length prefill — is exactly what the audit
  describes and does not depend on backend.

  Not implemented here on purpose: the audit's OWN text assigns the structural fix to **L-15**
  ("Longest-common-prefix session selection with ring-aware exactness") — `bestExtend` picking the
  session with the longest common prefix (not requiring full containment) and `Session.rewindForReuse`
  truncating to it, which `decoder/session.go:73-80` already implements correctly and which
  `sessions.go` simply never asks for. L-15's own decision rule is explicitly "the P-18 measurement"
  above — this disposition IS that measurement, not a substitute for the fix. Fund L-15 against it
  rather than folding a session-reuse redesign into this pass.

  The Llama-3 `Today Date:` preamble note needs no action: the audit's own call was "worth
  documenting rather than changing," not a defect to fix, and no code change is warranted here.
- **P-19 · The vision tower runs before the per-model queue** (`internal/serveapp/vision_serve.go:127-138`
  precedes `lm.enter` at `:144`), so N concurrent image requests run N SigLIP forwards bounded only
  by `-max-inflight` (128); `qwenExtractRGB` (`multimodal/qwen_preprocess.go:233-266`) goes through
  `image.At(x,y).RGBA()` per pixel (~1–2 s per image at the cap). Session memory is uncapped by RAM
  (`-kv-sessions 4` on a 128k model can pin four full-context KVs).

  **DISPOSITION — `qwenExtractRGB` FIXED; the other two NOT IMPLEMENTED, deferred (2026-09-03).**

  **Fixed: per-pixel `image.At().RGBA()`.** Added direct fast paths for `*image.YCbCr` (Go's decoded-
  JPEG format — the common case for real photos) and `*image.RGBA`, both PROVABLY bit-identical to
  the generic path rather than just close: `image.RGBA.At()` computes `uint32(pix)*0x101`, and
  `(pix*0x101)>>8 == pix` exactly for any `pix` in [0,255] (257·pix = 256·pix + pix, and the `>>8`
  term of the low `pix` vanishes since `pix<256`) — so reading `Pix` directly and skipping the
  `>>8` round-trip is the same value; `image.YCbCr.At()` calls `color.YCbCrToRGB` then wraps the
  result the same way, so calling it directly is the identical identity. `*image.NRGBA` is
  deliberately NOT fast-pathed — its `.RGBA()` premultiplies by alpha, so a naive buffer read would
  silently diverge from the generic path on any pixel with alpha < 255 — and falls through to the
  unchanged generic loop.

  Correctness: `TestQwenExtractRGB_fastPathsMatchGeneric` compares each fast path against the
  generic path directly on synthetic images (a `genericImage` wrapper defeats the type switch so the
  reference always takes the slow path), including an NRGBA case with genuinely non-255 alpha to
  prove the exclusion is deliberate and correct rather than an oversight — all three PASS with
  exact equality. Mutation-checked twice (swapped the RGBA fast path's channel order; swapped the
  YCbCr fast path's argument order): both caught immediately. Full `multimodal` suite green.
  `gofmt`/`go vet`/`staticcheck` clean.

  Performance: isolated benchmark at a realistic 1024×1024 YCbCr image (written and run, not
  committed): **38.1 ms → 16.4 ms, ~2.3×**. Smaller than the audit's own "~1-2s per image" framing,
  which likely measured the WHOLE preprocessing pipeline (resize included) rather than pixel
  extraction alone — this fix targets only the extraction step the audit specifically named.

  **Deferred: vision tower runs before the per-model queue.** Confirmed directly: both
  `serveVisionChatWith` (OpenAI-flavored) and its Anthropic counterpart call `visionPrompt` — which
  runs the real SigLIP/Qwen-ViT forward — BEFORE `enter`/`tryEnter` claims the per-model queue slot
  and mutex, so the mutex that already serializes decode across concurrent requests to the same
  model does nothing for the vision forward; only the queue's raw slot count (`-max-inflight`, 128)
  bounds how many can run at once. The fix is simple in shape — move `enter`/`tryEnter` (and its
  paired `exit`) to wrap `visionPrompt` too, in both handlers — but neither handler has an existing
  test that reaches it end to end: `venc`/`qwenEnc` are concrete struct types, not interfaces, so
  there is no seam to inject a controllable-delay stub and prove the concurrency bound actually
  changed, and building one would mean either a real vision-capable checkpoint wired through the
  full HTTP server (resource-heavy, and this session has been conservative about loading real
  checkpoints unnecessarily) or a mocking refactor that is its own separate piece of work. Shipping
  the reorder without a test that can catch a regression in the property that matters — the
  concurrency bound — would violate this pass's own "every fix mutation-checked" standard. A genuine
  gap, left open rather than claimed; the two-line reorder is the whole fix once a test seam exists.

  **Deferred: session memory uncapped by RAM.** Confirmed: `-kv-sessions` (`internal/serveapp/main.go:385`)
  is a flat COUNT of resident sessions with no accounting for each one's actual KV byte footprint,
  which scales with how many tokens a session holds — up to the model's full context. `-kv-sessions 4`
  on a 128k-context model can genuinely pin four full-context KV caches at once, unbounded by
  available RAM. A correct fix needs the same shape as M-01/M-02's byte-budget-aware accounting
  (track each session's real KV bytes, cap resident sessions by a total-byte budget rather than a
  raw count) — real design work, not a local-loop fix. A genuine gap, left open rather than claimed.

**Constrained decoding**

- **P-20 · `Masker.Process` is O(V) grammar walks per decode step with a snapshot/restore and a map
  lookup per token, no per-state cache** (`constrain/constrain.go:198-225`,
  `constrain/schema_grammar.go:112-125`, `constrain/json.go:100-124`): per step, for each of V ids, an
  `m.isEOS[id]` map probe, then `TryBytes` copies the frame stack in and out. Inside a string (the
  dominant JSON state) nearly every token is legal so the walk runs over all its bytes. Estimate
  40–120 ns/token → 6–30 ms per step against ~2–5 ms per resident-GPU decode step — constrained
  decoding plausibly 3–10× slower per token on GPU. Levers, cheapest first: `[]bool` for EOS; skip
  the snapshot when a precomputed per-state first-byte bitmap rejects the token; cache the allowed
  set for the two context-free states (`fsStr`/`jsString`: legal iff no `"`, `\`, <0x20); a byte-trie
  over the vocab so one walk masks every token sharing a prefix (see L-07). Measure one `Process`
  call at `fsStr` and at `fsObjKeyOrClose` for V=151,936 against the unconstrained step.
  (`docs/ollama-chase.md:1559` notes the O(V); the string-state cache is the addition.)

  **DISPOSITION — ALREADY FIXED, landed the same day as this audit (2026-09-03 review).** Both
  "cheapest first" levers are done, on `main`, with exactly the measurement discipline this pass
  uses elsewhere — found while reading the current code rather than assuming the audit's estimate
  still held.

  **Lever 1** (`9401bdd`, "measure P-20, and take its cheapest lever"): measured the audit's own
  estimate directly — real V=151,936, min-of-7 — landing at the BOTTOM of the audit's 40-120 ns/token
  band (13.8-39.7 ns/token depending on state), 1.81-1.97× not the audit's upper "3-10×". Converted
  `isEOS` from `map[int]bool` (151,936 probes/step) to an indexed `[]bool`: interleaved A/B on `fsStr`,
  −15% (7.103 ms → 6.035 ms mean, 3/3 pairs). `constrain.Masker.isEOS` today (`constrain/constrain.go:50`)
  is already `[]bool`.

  **Lever 2** (`2a44d4e`, "exact plain-string bitmap"): the bigger of the two — a precomputed
  per-vocab bitmap (`plainOK`, `constrain/constrain.go:52-55`) marking ids that are unconditionally
  legal inside a JSON string AND leave the state unchanged (no `"`, `\`, or control byte — 96.88% of
  a real vocab), answered with one bit test instead of `TryBytes`' grammar walk + frame-stack
  snapshot/restore. Chose an EXACT structure over a Bloom filter deliberately (a Bloom "maybe" still
  forces the walk it's meant to avoid, and here 97% of answers are definite). Conservative by
  construction: a control-byte token is NOT fast-pathed as illegal, since `"`+0x0A closes the string
  before the control byte is even reached — only "provably legal and state-invariant" is
  fast-pathed. Measured: `fsStr` (the dominant JSON state) 7.10 ms → 0.35 ms per `Process` call,
  ~17×; weighted per-step cost over a real 17-token document 1.21× (pos 64) / 1.18× (pos 512) —
  under G4's pre-registered 1.5× ship bar. Correctness proven exhaustively, not sampled:
  `TestPlainString_exact` (`constrain/plainstring_test.go`) compares the fast path against the full
  walk for EVERY id of an adversarial vocabulary across control/quote/backslash/UTF-8 classes and
  state-transition edge cases, for both grammar implementations — and is proven able to go red
  (a control-byte misclassification fails it with an exact diagnostic).

  Both commits' own text flags what's left, and it matches what this item's remaining "levers"
  describe: the mask cost is O(V) and CONSTANT in model size, so the ratio worsens as decode gets
  faster — the fix reduces the constant, it doesn't remove the asymptote. A `LogitProcessor`
  (constrained decoding) also disables speculative decoding and the on-device greedy argmax
  (`decoder/spec_eagle.go:28`), so real cost on those configurations is higher than the measured
  ratio. The remaining two levers — a per-state first-byte bitmap for the non-string states, and a
  byte-trie over the vocab so one walk masks every token sharing a prefix — are the audit's own
  explicitly-named next step, **L-07** (the item's own `(see L-09)` cross-reference is wrong: L-09 is
  the unrelated CPU-SIMD-kernel item; corrected in both places while dispositioning L-07 below), not
  folded into this disposition. `go test ./constrain/...` green.

**CUDA**

- **P-21 · Host round trips per decode token.** Dense greedy: `gpu.Upload(r.x, emb)` (H2D + the full
  synchronize aikit's `Upload` performs, `cuda/resident.go:977-944`), `stream.Sync`,
  `gpu.Download(argIdx)` — three blocking crossings for 8 B of payload plus the embedding
  (`cuda/resident.go:2383-2332`, `:2396-2411`). MoE with C′ adds per routed layer `stream.Sync` +
  D2H(rIdx) + `UploadBatch` (sync) + `gpu.Upload(slotIdx)` (sync) = 4 (`cuda/resident.go:1024-1042`) →
  ~160/token on a 40-layer 35B, 4× the ~40 the G31 accounting counts (`:300-303` counts only the
  batch syncs). Fold `slotIdx` into the same `UploadBatch` (one fewer sync per layer, zero risk);
  keep the embedding table resident and gather on-device. Measure with `GOINFER_MOE_CACHE_PROF`
  after adding the missing sync to `profSyncCalls`. Batched prefill/verify re-allocates 15–17
  M-sized buffers per call and frees them at exit (`cuda/prefill.go:589-311`; `cuMemFree`
  synchronises the context) — ~34 driver calls per n-gram verify round; `PrefillLastN`'s per-row
  tail pays 2 syncs + a 600 KB D2H per row (`cuda/prefill.go:893-516`) where greedy verify needs
  only the argmax (`tailAllArgmax` exists). Fused today: `fused_rms_qkv`, `fused_rms_gu`,
  `rope_kv`; not fused: `quant_vec`→o-proj, `glu_quant`→down (spec K3b), rope→QKV epilogue;
  `cuda/megakernel.cu:3` is an empty scaffold; ~8 launches per dense layer. The graphs path does
  not cover the expert-DMA term (`cuda/resident.go:2795-2785`, live by design at `:254-260`), so on
  a cached-MoE token the 15.69 vs 10.74 figure — a dense-path number — bounds to the launch-bound
  fraction. Host RAM doubles during a C′ load (`cuda/backend.go:204-464` keeps every layer's packed
  `hostW` on the heap while `cacheWQ`, `cuda/resident.go:606-590`, copies into pinned memory —
  ~16 GB + ~16 GB for the 35B).

  **DISPOSITION — the cheapest lever FIXED and verified on the CUDA box; one sub-claim found NOT
  LIVE; the rest genuinely deferred (2026-09-03).**

  **Fixed: `slotIdx` folded into `UploadBatch`.** `loadRoutedExperts` (`cuda/resident.go:1005-1067`)
  used to pay two separate synchronizes per MoE layer per token: `gpu.UploadBatch(r.expBatch)` for
  the layer's expert-slot misses (guarded by `len(r.expBatch) > 0`), then an always-present, separate
  `gpu.Upload(r.slotIdx, r.hostSlot[:r.topK])` for the per-token routing table the GEMV reads. Folded
  the slotIdx upload into the SAME batch (`r.expBatch = append(r.expBatch, gpu.HostCopy{Dst:
  r.slotIdx, Src: u32bytes(r.hostSlot[:r.topK])})`, using the `u32bytes` helper already used
  elsewhere in this file), removing the separate call and its synchronize entirely — one
  `UploadBatch` per layer instead of up to two, on every MoE token. This also fixes the audit's own
  secondary complaint for free: `profSyncCalls` previously undercounted (only the batch call
  incremented it), and now counts the one call that actually happens.

  Correctness: built and vetted with `-tags cuda` on the box (this machine has no CUDA hardware, so
  all CUDA work for this item ran directly on the `nobara` Linux/RTX-2070-SUPER box over SSH, current
  on `origin/main` before starting). `go vet` and the CI-pinned `staticcheck` (0.8.0) both clean.
  `TestGemma4MoE_cacheExpertsBitExact_tiny`/`_cacheReuse_tiny` (the existing gate that gave the exact
  reference-vs-cached bit-identity check this exercises) pass unchanged. Mutation-checked directly:
  truncating the folded upload to zero bytes (`u32bytes(r.hostSlot[:0])`) makes both tests fail
  immediately with a concrete logit mismatch (`cached 0.900354922 != resident 0.915397823`) — caught,
  then reverted and reconfirmed green. The broader suite (`TestGemma4MoE_*`, `TestPagerDeterminism`,
  `TestSpecPagerInteraction`) and the real **26B** end-to-end decode gate
  (`TestGemma4_26B_cache_B`, ~11.4 GB int4 experts staged via C′) all green with the fix in
  place — 64 real tokens, coherent output, distinct-trigram 0.789, 76.1% cache hit rate across
  21,840 expert loads, no errors.

  **Investigated, found NOT LIVE: "`PrefillLastN`'s per-row tail... where greedy verify needs only
  the argmax."** Traced every caller: greedy speculative verify (`decoder/blockspec.go`) already
  calls `PrefillLastNArgmax` exclusively — the batched-head, argmax-only primitive `tailAllArgmax`
  drives — never the slow per-row-logits `PrefillLastN`. `PrefillLastN` itself has NO production
  caller anywhere in `decoder/` or `cuda/`; every reference outside its own definition and comment
  block is in a test or in `PrefillLastN`'s own doc, which literally exists to explain why
  `PrefillLastNArgmax` was built (measured 1.046 ms marginal/row vs 0.934 ms single-row, "no
  amortization at all, in the one place the batched pass exists to provide it"). The audit's own
  parenthetical — "(`tailAllArgmax` exists)" — undersold how far this already went: it's not just
  available, it's what greedy verify already uses. Confirmed non-issue; no action needed.

  **Deferred — real, separate engineering work:** keeping the embedding table resident with an
  on-device gather (removes the dense-greedy H2D+Upload+Download round trip entirely, but changes
  the embedding read path across every family); batched prefill/verify's 15-17 buffer
  allocate/free-at-exit churn (`cuda/prefill.go:589-311`, `cuMemFree` synchronizes — needs a
  persistent scratch pool, the same shape as this codebase's other scratch-reuse fixes but sized for
  M-varying batched shapes); the kernel fusion gaps (`quant_vec`→o-proj, `glu_quant`→down,
  rope→QKV epilogue) and the empty `cuda/megakernel.cu` scaffold — new kernel authorship, not a
  local-loop fix; and the C′ load's host-RAM doubling (`cuda/backend.go:204-464` / `cuda/resident.go:606-590`)
  — would need streaming the pack-then-pin step instead of holding both copies, a real design change
  to the load path. Each is a genuinely separate, larger undertaking than this pass's local-loop
  fixes; left open rather than claimed.

**Metal and WebGPU**

- **P-22 · Metal: the generic MoE paged path is excluded from the residency-set pin the Gemma-4
  path measured at −11%** (`metal/model.go:899-900` keys on `r.g4moe != nil`; `slotBuffers` at
  `:1145-1155` enumerates only `g4moe.pool`; `forwardLogitsMoEPaged`, `metal/moe.go:715-766`,
  preads the same way per layer and records no `p2Commit/p2Wait` profile at all). With 40 MoE layers
  that is up to 40 re-validations per token against the ~190 ms/token non-staging residual
  (`docs/task-metal-expert-streaming-at-scale.md:93-94`). Port the profile split first, then A/B
  `slotBuffers()` extended to `L.moe.pool` on the 35B at N=64, with the do-nothing arm (the doc's
  own bisect says pinning beyond slots regresses). Expert dispatch is per-expert — 3·k dispatches
  per MoE layer (`metal/moe.go:634-644`, `:636-647`; `metal/gemma4_moe.go:408-412`, `:423-428`),
  ~960/token on a 40-layer model against a ~3.8 µs floor; gate‖up and the activation are
  row-independent across experts (one dispatch each over `k·rows`) and the down-accumulate can loop
  the k slots inside one kernel in the same fold order, bit-identically: 3 dispatches/layer. WebGPU
  implements none of the optional resident extensions (`decoder/residency.go:61-96`; no
  `ForwardNoLogits`/`ForwardArgmax`/`PrefillLast` under `gpu/`), so prefill pays the LM head + a
  full-vocab readback + a sync per prompt token (`gpu/residency.go:954-962`; `ForwardNoLogits` is a
  plan whose steps stop before the last `rmsQuant`+`gemv`, `gpu/decoderunner.go:1160-1156` — a few
  dozen lines), every decode token reads back `vocab×4` bytes (`gpu/decoderunner.go:1281-1295`; a
  64-lane argmax kernel is the `moeRoute` shape), and `ForwardN` streams the weights K times
  (`gpu/residency.go:975-1013` → K independent M=1 plans) while `DecodeTokenFusedBatched`
  (`gpu/decodetoken_batched.go:11-24`) has no production caller. The vision tower issues ~7 submits
  per head per layer (`gpu/vision_encoder.go:202-247`, `:276`).

  **DISPOSITION — the residency-pin exclusion FIXED; the rest deferred (2026-09-03).**

  **Fixed: the residency set excluded every non-Gemma4 paged MoE family.** Confirmed both halves of
  the bug directly: the entry condition (`metal/model.go:899-900`, the item header's cited line before
  this fix rewrote it) gated the WHOLE
  residency-set feature on `r.g4moe != nil && r.g4moe.paged`, so a generic paged-MoE model
  (Mixtral/Qwen/GLM/gpt-oss/qwen3_5_moe/qwen3_next — `moe.go`'s path, which the code's own comment
  calls "same mechanism, same expertPool" as g4moe's) never got a residency set BUILT at all,
  regardless of what `slotBuffers()` enumerated; and `slotBuffers()` itself (`:1153`, now `:1165`)
  only ever walked `layers[l].g4moe.pool`, never `layers[l].moe.pool`. Fixed both: the gate now
  triggers on `(r.g4moe != nil && r.g4moe.paged) || (r.moe != nil && r.moe.paged)`, and
  `slotBuffers()` enumerates both pools. The audit's own cited caution — "the doc's own bisect says
  pinning beyond slots regresses" — traced to its actual source (`metal/model.go`'s own comment on
  the five-arm bisect, not a separate pinning-specific study): that bisect tested pinning ADDITIONAL
  BUFFER CATEGORIES beyond slots (weights/KV/scratch) and found each regresses phase 1 in proportion
  to pin-set size. It did not test extending the SLOTS category to a second architecture's identical
  buffer kind — `expertSlot{guW, guS, dW, dS}` is the literal same struct both paths' pools use — so
  this extension stays inside the one category the bisect found the win in, rather than adding a new
  one the bisect found regresses.

  Correctness: `go build`/`go vet -tags metal` clean. Could NOT run the existing gate
  (`TestResidencySet_pinsExactlyTheLiveSlots`, which already validates `slotBuffers()` against
  `residencyBufs` on a small fixture) on this machine: `ResidencySetsSupported()` returns false here
  despite macOS 26.6.2 — a genuine environment limitation (this specific Mac/Metal-runtime
  combination does not expose `MTLResidencySet`, not a version-string check I could work around), so
  the residency-set code path is untestable on this hardware regardless of fixture size. The fix is
  two structurally-parallel, minimal changes mirroring the already-shipped and already-measured
  g4moe case exactly (same struct fields, same enumeration pattern, same OR'd boolean condition
  shape) — reviewed carefully for that parity — but the real A/B this item also asks for (35B at
  N=64, with the do-nothing control arm) was not attempted: it needs both `ResidencySetsSupported()`
  hardware this box lacks AND a 35B model load this 17 GB Mac cannot safely absorb. A genuine
  two-part gap — one testing, one measurement — left open rather than claimed.

  **Deferred — genuinely separate work:** Metal's per-expert dispatch (3·k dispatches/layer, ~960/token
  on a 40-layer model) collapsing to 3 dispatches/layer via row-independent batching + an
  in-kernel fold — real kernel-authorship work, not a call-site change. WebGPU's missing resident
  extensions (`ForwardNoLogits`/`ForwardArgmax`/`PrefillLast`), `ForwardN`'s K-times weight
  streaming, `DecodeTokenFusedBatched` having no production caller, and the vision tower's ~7
  submits/head/layer are a SEPARATE backend (`gpu/`, not `metal/`) with its own multi-part
  implementation gap — out of this item's scope as a Metal-specific residency fix, and each is its
  own substantial undertaking. Left open rather than folded in.
