## §6 — Checked and found correct

Recorded so the next audit does not re-tread them. Everything below was read, not assumed. IDs
in this section that are not defined above (C-19, C-31, M-15, R-08, G13, N-19, M18, M11, …) are the
2026-08-05 audit's, its post-audit review's, and the queues' — the dispositions that were re-checked.

- **P18 expert-major MoE prefill** (`decoder/mlp.go:491-581`): every `(row, rank)` output is
  scattered to `perRank[(row*k+rank)]` and folded back in rank order from zero, exactly `moeMLP`'s
  `out += wts[j]*expOut` sequence; `routeExperts`/`topK` are the same functions on the same router
  logits (first-max tie break, `used[]` prevents a duplicate expert per row, `len(idx) < k` after
  `groupLimit` handled by ranging `idxs[r]`); shared expert, live pager, override/trace seams refuse
  before any work; `GOINFER_MOE_EXPERT_MAJOR=0` disables at the single call site
  (`decoder/forwardn.go:555`) and a refused chunk `break`s to the per-row path. Bit-identity reduces
  to aikit's M-invariance contract (unverified here for the webgpu staged backend — see leads);
  the gate asserts `!=` per logit with a non-vacuity counter.
- **P19 fused kernel** (`decoder/fusedattn.go`): block-major `vBlk` layout written by
  `gatherKVFused` matches `attendTileFused`'s reads; running max/sum, `corr` rescale, masked-block
  zeroing, zeroing outside `[j0, j1]`, last partial block, `hiMax` break, `lRun==0` → zero row;
  per-worker `fs`; declines under `treeMask`; only on the f32 arm so `--cpu-exact-prefill` and spec
  verify never see it; `TestFusedAttention_matchesMaterialized` runs without assets, covers a partial
  last block and a sliding window with `a0 > a1`, and has a non-vacuity check.
- **A3 head fan-out** (`decoder/forwardn.go:952-1015`): each worker gathers into its own
  `kh/vt/vBlk`, uses its own serial `mmWS`, writes disjoint `ctx` ranges and reads only immutable
  inputs; the serial arm runs the same closure; `TestAttendF32Fanout_bitIdentical` asserts equality
  with a non-zero check.
- **Attention geometry:** GQA `kvh = qhead/group` (HF repeat-interleave); sliding mask
  `[pos−W+1, pos]` = HF `q−k < W`; QK-norm before RoPE; partial rotary rotates `[0,rd/2)` with
  `[rd/2,rd)`; per-layer inv-freq/mscale (Mellum YaRN on full layers only); m-RoPE decode falls
  back to `seqPos+delta`; tree rows use `treeRowPos`; RoPE position after reuse is `cache.Pos()`.
  Batched local layers: `batchReadLocal` assembles `[base, startPos+K)` from a ring whose live
  window covers it; the write is deferred to `commitBatch`; `advanceTo` is idempotent. Last-row
  logits independent of tiling. Cancellation per layer in the batched sweep, per token elsewhere.
- **Recurrent lifecycle for Mamba-2 and DeltaNet** (C-01/C-02/C-17 dispositions hold):
  `TruncateTo(0)` re-zeroes, any partial rewind reports inexact, `rewindForReuse` cold-prefills,
  `reconcile` resets on rollback (R-14), `specRollbackSafe` refuses granite/nemotron/qwen35 before
  the cache is touched. Resident side: CUDA `Forward`/`ForwardNoLogits`/`ForwardN(startPos==0)` and
  Metal `Forward` re-zero DeltaNet state at pos 0; WebGPU `Reset()` re-zeroes Mamba `{win,ssm}` and
  DeltaNet `{win,dnState}` from `Forward(pos==0)` and `ForwardN(startPos==0)`, declines `K>1` for
  both, and `TestQwen35ResidentParity`'s replay arm can fail on a missing reset.
- **Hybrid mixers vs HF:** Mamba-2 conv tap indexing, `dA = exp(dt·A)` with `A = −exp(A_log)`,
  `dt = softplus(dt+bias)`, `S = S·dA + dt·x⊗B`, `y = S·C + D·x`, gated RMSNorm over the gated
  product with `NormGroups` 1 (Granite) / `NGroups` (Nemotron); Gated DeltaNet decay-before-read,
  `delta = (v − kv)·β`, `g`/`β` derivations, q/k L2-norm eps 1e-6 with `hk^-0.5`, gated norm ×
  `silu(z)`; `qwen35Attention` `[q ‖ gate]` split and `× sigmoid(gate)`; `qwen3next.go` fused-tensor
  splits; Granite's four multipliers at the right sites; Nemotron-H single-op blocks; Gemma 4
  sandwich norms, PLE branch, `layer_scalar`, shared-KV source, `WindowStart`, final softcap once,
  MoE block router/renorm/post-norm; DeepSeek MLA decoupled RoPE slices, interleaved de-interleave,
  absorb-path identities, `AttnScale = qk_head_dim^-0.5`; gpt-oss sink in max-shift and
  denominator only, clamped interleaved SwiGLU `(up+1)·gate·σ(α·gate)`, per-expert biases; Llama 4
  below `attention_chunk_size`; InternLM2 `wqkv` de-interleave.
- **KV cache:** ring `truncate` exactness rule; write-after-read ordering; per-layer `stride[]` (M11)
  with `min` guards for KV-shared layers; int8 scale reslicing; MLA latent reslice; multimodal reset
  outside the recurrent guard (M-25). `LoadSession`'s ring branch checks stride, `nLive ≤ w`,
  `count ≥ nLive`, payload length and the R-15 unwritten-ring case; the int8 `r.i8()` alias is
  copy-on-grow safe. Session prefix reuse always leaves a seed token; `bestExtend` only reuses a
  session whose whole token list is a prefix; the last emitted token is committed after its forward
  so `tokens == pos` after every generation; LRU mutation under the server's model lock (except
  N-20).
- **Speculation:** greedy verify math in all four CPU loops (first mismatch replaced by the
  target's argmax from the same pass, bonus token on full accept, `keep = base+1+accepted`,
  `isStop` before every send, exact budget); `argmax` tie-break (strict `>`, lowest index) matches
  `topFilterLogits` and `topKByLogit` and the CUDA/Metal kernels; the sampled step
  (`decoder/spec_sample.go:82-123`) is exact Leviathan/Chen for a point-mass q, with a history window
  equal to the plain sampler's; grammar-masked verify applies `MaskAt` with `Process`'s rule; top-p
  cut inclusive at equality (HF's rule); min-p threshold scale-invariant; the P2b chunked path is
  order-pinned and its machine-independence gate can fail; one `*rand.Rand` per `Sampler`, one
  `Sampler` per generation; EAGLE feeds post-residual layer output before the final norm (HF
  `hidden_states[l+1]`); `EagleState.clone` never aliases; `resBusy` CAS taken before any device
  write (C-03 holds); tree verify refuses non-batchable arches; DFlash/DSpark `d2t` range-checked,
  block RoPE positions absolute, anchor at `nCtx`; the server maps `top_p == 1` to disabled and
  `top_p == 0` to greedy.
- **Loaders:** P13's release is keyed on file dtypes; every raw-byte consumer sits on a dtype
  `riskyDType` classifies as risky, so the mapping is retained for them; LoRA, the drafters and the
  vision tower own separate mappings; merge-at-load copies the target before mutating. GGUF shape
  discipline (every `mat`/`vec`/`flat`/`stackedExperts`/`fusedSplit` checks dims against
  arch-derived sizes; row offsets are element offsets; `permMat` rejects odd/indivisible head_dim;
  `ggufConfig` recovers builder panics; `ggufLayerCount` for granite/nemotron/llama4/qwen35;
  `ggufInvPermute` agrees with llama.cpp's `permute`). GPTQ/AWQ multiple-of-8 guards, `g_idx`
  range-checked, AWQ nibble order `[0,4,1,5,2,6,3,7]`. fp8 e4m3fn table (NaN only at `0x7F/0xFF`,
  max 448, subnormal `2^-9`, all 256 gated). MXFP4 e8m0 half-scale × doubled kvalues. `.giw` reader:
  CRC covers the entire aliased region; every count/length passes `need()` before allocation;
  rows/cols capped at `2^26` before the product; kind-3/4 array lengths validated; `f32` copied
  (alignment), only byte-granular arrays aliased; `giw.Read`/`take` overflow-safe; `ReadTokFile`
  C-31 bound; `metadataPrefixLen` bounded with depth cap. Both pagers mutex-guarded (C-30 closed);
  `expertPager` registers exactly one span per expert and `assertAdvisedBytesSane` can fail.
  `streamQuantized`/`quantizeWM` per-call scratch under `parallelLayers`. `f32ToF16bits` and
  `f16bitsToF32` agree with the metal/cuda convention. LoRA scale, shape cross-check, M18 name
  validation.
- **Serving:** body caps (`Content-Length` pre-check + `MaxBytesReader` on every POST route; the G1
  gates use a `panicReader`); `max_tokens` `< 1` rejected, ceiling 131072, clamped to `ctx −
  len(prompt)` on chat, completions, responses, Anthropic, vision and tools; prompt ≥ ctx rejected
  with the resident cap applied only on the resident path; images are `data:`/base64 only, no
  server-side fetch, one per request, `DecodeConfig` before `Decode` (M-15); auth constant-time,
  non-loopback and `-allow-admin` require a key, TLS pair check; `ReadHeaderTimeout` 10 s /
  `ReadTimeout` 60 s / `IdleTimeout` 120 s, `MaxHeaderBytes` default, no CORS headers; handler panics
  recovered by `net/http`; `NewKVCache` clamps a negative `capHint` (C-19); OpenAI SSE framing on the
  text path (role-first chunk, deltas, finish chunk with `finish_reason` null on deltas, usage chunk
  when requested, `[DONE]`, mid-stream errors as an `error` object); stop strings earliest-match,
  omitted from output, partial-match holdback composing with UTF-8 holdback; tool-call streaming
  shape gated bytewise against the real parsers; Anthropic `max_tokens` required, roles restricted
  (G13), event order, `stop_reason` mapping, `tool_use` via `input_json_delta`, 529 on a full queue,
  tools+image refused (R-08); sessions keyed by token prefix so a system-prompt change cannot hit;
  eviction cannot touch a session in use; `-session-dir` snapshots fingerprint-bound and restored
  under the lock; queue/concurrency (non-blocking slot claim then `mu.Lock`; a queued request whose
  client disconnected costs at most one layer/one token once it acquires the lock; `resolveAndLock`
  releases via one `defer`; the drain releases `rw` before taking `regMu`; `TestLookupLockedOnlyViaResolve`);
  shutdown (`BaseContext` cancel → `Shutdown(30s)` → `tryLockUntil(5s)` per model → second signal
  `os.Exit(1)`; no deadlock path found); `cmd/serve` backend-tag guards; `cmd/prequant` cancels on
  signal and removes the partial (M-21); the `--cpu-fast-attention`/`--cpu-exact-prefill` flag→env
  plumbing sets the env explicitly both ways before any forward.
- **constrain:** the JSON number automaton (`-0`, `01`, `1.`/`1e`/`1e+`, exponent sign), literals,
  string escapes incl. `\uXXXX` (lone surrogates pass, as in `json.Valid`), control bytes rejected;
  `schemaGrammar` any key order, duplicate keys impossible, `required` at `}`, `,` only while an

- **tokenizer:** `splitGPT2` reproduces the cl100k/Llama-3 alternation faithfully (incl. the

- **multimodal:** Qwen2.5-VL `smart_resize` incl. `RoundToEven` and the `max(factor, …)` floor;

- **demos:** `demo/agent` has no shell/file tools; the only model-triggerable action is an MCP

- **CUDA:** every production launch's argument list vs the `.cu` signature and the PTX `.param`
  list — all 40 entry points agree in count, order and type, including the five MoE call sites'

  at `cuda/resident.go:759-748`), `GOINFER_MOE_CACHE_SLOTS` clamped to `[topK, nE]` then to measured
  VRAM; graphs capture after `allocSlots`/`fuseQKV`, no captured buffer ever reallocated,
  `captureGraphs` clears sticky `launchErr`, a failed self-test leaves graphs off; `Close` frees
  pinned host, mapped expert stacks, graph execs and the device ledger on the executor thread,
  idempotent, safe after a failed setup; `prefillCore`'s scratch released by a defer registered
  before the first allocation; the executor's `runJob` recover converts allocation panics into

- **Metal:** kernel argument order vs every production launch site in `model.go`, `moe.go`,
  `gemma4_moe.go`, `deltanet.go`, `prefill.go` — all match; vector-load byte offsets 16-aligned by

  LRU-touch on hit and stage, `N ≥ topK` enforced at build, no slot written while a command buffer
  referencing it is in flight; reduction widths pinned (256/128) where order matters,
  `simd_sum`/`simd_max` only where order-exact, argmax tie-break `>`-then-lower-index everywhere;
  GQA mapping, sliding window `winStart = nKeys−window` = the CPU `WindowStart`, sink in max and
  denominator only, softcap host-side f64 and correctly skipped on the monotonic argmax path, NeoX
  half-split with partial rotary, YaRN mscale on cos/sin; `f32ToF16` round-half-up used only for
  W4A8 group scales and host-side prefill inputs while kernels use hardware `half()` for the KV
  cache — no shared golden crosses that boundary; DeltaNet kernels mirror CUDA's structure, `Reset()`

- **WebGPU:** new/changed uniform layouts (deltaRule/deltaNorm/deltaGates/deltaGNorm/qSplit/
  attnGate, relu2, qkNorm, W8A16 ×2, moe_w4a8 `Dims`, mambaF16 `Dims`, the wide-attention `P`, mamba

  read/write uses `pack2x16float`/`unpack2x16float`, so no `shader-f16` gating is needed); the 2-D
  grid split used by every gemv-shaped kernel; int8 activation padding `padK32(K)` consistent across

  port matches `gatedDeltaNetStep` step for step; KV caps error at `pos+n > ctxCap` (no wrap) with
  boundaries tested; int8 KV store/read index and scale symmetry, f16 word packing; C-26

  `gemv_w4a8.go`, byte counts checked per expert, error paths release; router uniform order matches
  `MoEResidentParams` at both call sites; P13 cannot affect the GPU side (every upload copies at

  dispatch path, and the undeclared Gemma features correctly decline Gemma 3/4, GPT-2, Cohere, LFM2.
- **Engineering:** no `replace`/`exclude`/`toolchain` directives anywhere; `go.work`/`go.work.sum`
  gitignored; `demo/agent` pins goinfer at a tag; MVS for an out-of-workspace consumer resolves each
  submodule to goinfer v0.15.0 + aikit v1.28.0 and Step 2's `go get` + `tidy` raises aikit by MVS
  (aikit/gpu is left to the B-07 grep — N-41); `gofmt -l .` from the root covers all five modules;
  vet/staticcheck under the tagged variants on linux and darwin, `go test -race` with the testhooks
  tag; CUDA built `CGO_ENABLED=0`; the M-19 cleanliness guard's two checks; `permissions: contents:
  read` on ci/fuzz/govulncheck, `write` only where `gh release upload` needs it, no
  `pull_request_target`, inputs reach shells via `env:`; no self-hosted runner; all 14 fuzz targets
  exist and are the complete `Fuzz*` set; `cmd/gate/mutation.go` proves what it claims and restores
  on signal; `event.go` keys results by (cell, pkg, test) and `ranCount()==0` is a FAIL in the
  filtered gpu cells; provenance reports dirty trees as INCONCLUSIVE; `-update` goldens neutralise

## §7 — Landscape, and an improvement program

The field as of 2026-09-02, from a sourced sweep (dates and links at the end of this section), read
against what the repo has already measured. Each item states the mechanism, why it fits goinfer's
constraints (single binary, no toolchain, bit-identity, measure-don't-assume), the lever it attacks
in the repo's own numbers, and a pre-registered decision rule in the queue's shape — including the
do-nothing arm. Items are marked **borrow** (the field has it), **verdict** (the field moved and the
repo's prior answer stands or changes), or **new** (a proposal specific to this codebase). Prior
art in-tree was read first: `docs/task-freetoken-techniques.md`, `docs/task-moe-streaming.md` §C′,
`docs/QUEUE.md` G31–G34, `docs/spec/README.md`, `docs/completed/task-turboquant-spike.md`,
`docs/roadmap.md`.

**Where the bar moved.** On an 8 GB laptop card, FreeToken (FlashML, arXiv 2608.16157, 17 Aug 2026)
decodes Qwen3.6-35B-A3B at **39.3 tok/s** on an RTX 4060 Laptop (i9-13900H; measured host BW
47.5 GB/s, PCIe 11.8 GB/s), and llama.cpp's `--n-cpu-moe` reaches **27–41 tok/s** on an RTX 3070 Ti
8 GB (May 2026 field reports). goinfer's measured figure for that class is 15.69 tok/s with CUDA
graphs and C′ expert streaming, with ~48% of the token in expert DMA at the PCIe ceiling (G31/G32).
Both peers get there the same way: **missing experts execute on the CPU from host RAM (~4× the
PCIe rate) instead of being streamed**, behind a global cross-layer LRU and a double-buffered
whole-layer prefill. On Macs, Ollama's MLX runner (default on ≥32 GB since v0.30, May 2026) and
mlx-lm's native MTP (Qwen3.5-27B 1.5×; **MoE only 1.11×** — one MTP layer predicts routing poorly)
set the Metal expectations; llama.cpp 0.2.0 (21 Aug 2026) ships `--fit on` by default and
in-GGUF MTP drafting (`--spec-type draft-mtp`, ~0.76–0.82 acceptance on Qwen3.8-27B, +33% on a 3090).
Models now ship as ~3B-active MoE + hybrid linear-attention layers + native MTP heads + NVFP4/QAT
checkpoints (Qwen3.8-27B/Flash-Next, Gemma 4, Nemotron 3.5 Lightning, DeepSeek V4). Go 1.27's
`simd`/`archsimd` (19 Aug 2026) adds arm64 NEON but no SDOT/i8mm/VNNI and is experiment-gated, so
hand-written assembly remains the CPU kernel substrate. goccy/go-llama is single-threaded with a
4 GiB linear-memory cap and no published benchmarks. Single binary + no toolchain is still unique
in this field (FreeToken is a Python/uv install; llama.cpp needs a compiler; Ollama bundles a
runner).

### A. Borrow — the field has it and it fits

**L-01 · Hybrid CPU/GPU expert execution for the 8 GB CUDA MoE path — promote Lead 5 from "track"
to first.** *(borrow: FreeToken q⋆ split; Fiddler, ICLR 2025; llama.cpp `-ncmoe`.)* Mechanism:
per layer, the m experts missing from the device cache are split — q⋆ ≈ m·(B_PCIe/B_host) fetched
into slots and run on the GPU, the rest computed on the CPU from the pinned host copy with the
existing W4A8/W8A8 NEON/AVX2 expert kernels, partial sums merged exactly. Why it fits: goinfer
already holds every expert pinned on the host for C′ (`cuda/resident.go:606-590`), already has
the CPU expert kernels (P18 made them expert-major), and already pays the host-visible routing
readback per layer that `task-moe-streaming.md` §C′ said the on-device router exists to avoid
(P-21: the D2H of `rIdx` is on the token path today) — so the architectural objection recorded in
`docs/task-freetoken-techniques.md` Lead 5 is smaller than it was when written. The lever is the
48% DMA term: with misses on the CPU the token time is `max(GPU hits, CPU misses)` instead of
`hits + DMA`, and G32's finding that the DMA is bandwidth-bound at line rate is exactly the case
where moving work off the bus beats overlapping it. **Decision rule:** Qwen3.6-35B-A3B int4 on the
8 GB card, paired and interleaved against the shipped C′ path and against `off`; fund at ≥1.3×
end-to-end, park below 1.15×, 1.15–1.3× ambiguous → second mechanism. The number to beat is 39.3.
Prerequisite: the G33 trace replay (`scripts/g33_replay.py`) already yields the per-layer miss
counts to size q⋆ from before any code.

**DISPOSITION — ACKNOWLEDGED, roadmap item; premises reconfirmed by this pass's P-21 work
(2026-09-03).** This is a substantial new feature (hybrid CPU/GPU split execution per layer), not an
audit defect — no code implemented here, and none should be under an audit-remediation pass. The
premises this item leans on were independently reconfirmed while doing P-21 on the CUDA box: the
per-token `rIdx` D2H readback is real and on the hot path (confirmed directly, then fixed one of its
two synchronizes), and `cuda/resident.go:606-590`'s host-pinned expert copy is exactly as described.
Both prerequisite scripts/docs exist (`scripts/g33_replay.py`, `docs/task-freetoken-techniques.md`).
Decision rule stands as written; left for a dedicated future pass with its own paired-and-interleaved
measurement budget.

**L-02 · A global cross-layer LRU for the CUDA expert cache (Lead 4).** *(borrow: FreeToken's
shared LRU; llama.cpp draft PR #27861's trace simulation — LRU-64 ~69% hit, LRU-128 ~81%, ~98.5% at
384 slots.)* The CPU pager already pools all layers into one frequency-aware `SpanCache`
(`decoder/moepaging.go`); only the CUDA path's `GOINFER_MOE_CACHE_SLOTS` is per-layer. G33 says
misses are capacity, not prediction — which is the regime where pooling raises effective capacity
wherever per-layer hot sets are uneven. **Decision rule:** replay the G33 trace under per-layer vs
global LRU at equal total slots first (no code beyond the replay); build only if the simulated miss
rate drops ≥15%; then the same paired A/B as L-01. Note llama.cpp's kernel-free formulation
(companion tensor with a permanently-zero slot + id remap, decode-only) is the shape to copy if
the slot-indexed kernels ever need to go.

**DISPOSITION — ACKNOWLEDGED, roadmap item (2026-09-03).** A trace-replay-first proposal with its own
pre-registered ≥15% simulated-miss-rate gate before any code — exactly the right shape, nothing to
add. `decoder/moepaging.go`'s `SpanCache` (confirmed present, the CPU pager's cross-layer pool) is
accurately described. No code implemented; decision rule stands.

**L-03 · Whole-layer double-buffered expert prefetch during prefill (Lead 2, §C′'s named next
lever).** *(borrow: FreeToken measured 19–26% from the second buffer; MoE prefill touches nearly
every expert, so the whole set is the right prefetch unit.)* Fits G32 ("overlap is the only
lever"). On the Mac paged path the per-layer expert set of the 35B is ~0.4 GB, so two buffers fit
beside the slot pool; combined with P18 (compute) this is the I/O half of paged prefill.
**Decision rule:** TTFT at 2k/8k prompts, paged 35B, single- vs double-buffered; fund at ≥1.2×.

**DISPOSITION — ACKNOWLEDGED, roadmap item (2026-09-03).** Prefill-side I/O overlap, cleanly scoped
against P18's already-shipped, default-ON compute-side fix (the audit's own "Headline" section
confirms P18 is real and shipped). No code implemented; decision rule stands for a future
paged-prefill pass.

**L-04 · `--fit`-style automatic placement as the default.** *(borrow: llama.cpp 0.2.0 `--fit on`:
shrink context first, then spill routed experts, dense tensors prioritised, 85–90% VRAM
utilisation; Ollama's runner does the equivalent.)* goinfer asks the operator for
`GOINFER_METAL_MOE_SLOTS`, `--moe-cache-slots`, `-ctx`, `--kv`; the M-01/M-02 fix produces the
computed budget this needs. Pick N slots and the context cap from RAM/VRAM with the same
priority order, print what was chosen, keep every knob as an override. Cost: a day; risk: none to
numerics. This is also the answer to "default to 64" having no code.

**DISPOSITION — ACKNOWLEDGED, roadmap item; prerequisite already shipped (2026-09-03).** The
M-01/M-02 byte-budget accounting this item builds on (`ResidentWeightBytes`/`ResidentWeightBytesPaged`)

purely a policy/UX layer (pick N from the already-correct budget, print the choice) with no new
numerics. Small (the item's own "a day" estimate looks right) but a feature, not a defect; not
implemented in this pass. Decision rule (none stated beyond "risk: none to numerics") stands.

**L-05 · State checkpoints at semantic anchors (Lead 1), and disk restore points.** *(borrow:
FreeToken's anchor checkpoints for hybrid models; Ollama v0.33 "prefill restore points"; LM Studio's
KV checkpoints every 256 tokens; mlx-lm's SSM snapshot + KV trim under speculation.)* One mechanism
closes four open items: the Snapshot refusals for DeltaNet/Mamba/conv state (C-02(d)); P-18's
stop-string → cold prefill; the "resident models get no prefix reuse" trade for slow MoE in long
agent loops (the regime `task-freetoken-techniques.md` says to re-measure); and rollback of
recurrent state under speculation, which would lift `specRollbackSafe`'s blanket refusal of the
hybrid families. Snapshot the full recurrent state only at turn/tool-call boundaries (bytes are
bounded by turns, not tokens). **Decision rule:** TTFT of turn N in a 10-turn agent transcript on
Qwen3.6-35B-A3B, resident CUDA, with vs without anchors; fund at ≥2× turn-3+ TTFT.

**DISPOSITION — ACKNOWLEDGED, roadmap item; one of its four closed items reconfirmed this pass
(2026-09-03).** "P-18's stop-string → cold prefill" is exactly what P-18's own disposition confirmed
and measured directly this session (148× TTFT penalty at 2k tokens on the real production prefill
path) — and P-18's disposition explicitly named this exact structural direction (there filed as
L-15, this item's sibling) as the intended fix, not a local-loop patch. This item is the larger
four-in-one mechanism; not implemented here (multi-family recurrent-state snapshotting is
substantial new engineering). Decision rule stands.

**L-06 · Speculation on the GPU backends: three field results to fold into the P10/P15 gates.**
*(borrow.)* (a) EAGLE 3.1 (vLLM, 26 May 2026): FC-norm on target hidden states + post-norm feedback
fixes "attention drift" at depth, up to 2× acceptance length at long context, config-level — applies
if goinfer's EAGLE path loads 3.1 drafters. (b) DSpark (DeepSeek, arXiv 2607.05147): confidence-
scheduled verify length — a scheduler change that fits G34's finding that the slot budget, not
acceptance, caps width. (c) MTP→n-gram pipelining (llama.cpp #23184): extend an MTP draft with the
n-gram drafter for free; n-gram alone gives no gain in llama.cpp either, which matches spec/02.
Warnings the field supplies: mlx-lm's MoE MTP at 1.11× and llama.cpp's "diminished due to
expert-union" — for A3B-class MoE the draft quality, not the verify, limits gains; the repo's own
"MTP doesn't change the math on CPU" (`docs/roadmap.md:71`) stands. GPU-only.

**DISPOSITION — ACKNOWLEDGED, roadmap item (2026-09-03).** Three field results to fold into existing
gates when/if the corresponding drafters are built; no code to write until then. (Note for whoever
picks this up: "the P10/P15 gates" does not resolve against this document's own P-10/P-15 items — its
P-10 is `.giw` CRC format work and P-15 is a sampler penalty-map fix, neither speculation-gate related
— so this is very likely a numbering reference into a different, older audit round; worth resolving
before acting on it.) No action needed now.

**L-07 · Precomputed grammar masks for `constrain` (P-20).** *(borrow: XGrammar-2, arXiv
2601.04426 — accepted/rejected/context-dependent token classes per state, cross-grammar mask cache
keyed by sub-grammar hash, JIT of the K most expensive masks during prefill, ~45 µs/token;
llguidance's slicer, <50 µs/token on a 128k vocab.)* goinfer's O(V) walk per step is plausibly
6–30 ms on a 152k vocab against a 2–5 ms resident decode step, i.e. the headline feature ("a Go
struct the model cannot violate") is the slowest path on the fastest backend. Cheapest first: `[]bool`
EOS, first-byte bitmap, the two string-state bitsets, then a vocab byte-trie so one walk masks every
token sharing a prefix. **Decision rule:** measure `Process` at `fsStr` and `fsObjKeyOrClose` at
V=151,936 before and after each lever; the target is under 0.5 ms per step. Pure Go, no new
dependency, no numerics.

**DISPOSITION — SAME ITEM AS P-20; two of four levers already shipped (2026-09-03).** This is P-20
filed twice — once as a core defect (P-20), once here with field citations (XGrammar-2, llguidance)
as a roadmap "borrow." See P-20's disposition for the full account: `[]bool` EOS and an exact
plain-string bitmap (which the commit's own doc comment confirms covers BOTH grammar
implementations — `constrain/json.go` and `constrain/schema_grammar.go` — via the optional
`InPlainString` interface, so "the two string-state bitsets" this item names is already the shipped
mechanism, not a separate remaining lever) both landed the same day as this audit, measured under
the item's own 0.5 ms/step target on the dominant state (0.35 ms). Also corrected P-20's own
`(see L-09)` cross-reference above, which pointed at the wrong item (L-09 is the unrelated
CPU-SIMD-kernel item) — this is the item it meant. Only the vocab byte-trie remains unimplemented;
decision rule stands for that piece specifically.

**L-08 · Formats the field now ships: NVFP4.** *(borrow: llama.cpp NVFP4 MMQ kernel merged 1 Apr
2026 and `llama-quantize` NVFP4 in Aug 2026; MLX `nvfp4`/`mxfp8` modes since Jan 2026; Qwen3.8,
Nemotron 3.5 Lightning and DeepSeek V4 publish NVFP4/QAT checkpoints.)* The MXFP4 reader
(`decoder/mxfp4.go`) and the fp8 e4m3 reader (`decoder/fp8.go`, Q3) are two-thirds of an NVFP4
reader (e2m1 elements, e4m3 per-16 block scale, f32 tensor scale) — dequant → int4/int8 requant the
way MXFP4 loads today. A loader item, no kernel work, and it unlocks the checkpoints the newest
models ship first. `scale_fmt: ue8m0` (G8's note) is the other missing scale encoding.

**DISPOSITION — ACKNOWLEDGED, roadmap item (2026-09-03).** Both cited existing readers confirmed
present (`decoder/mxfp4.go`, `decoder/fp8.go`). A loader-only feature (new checkpoint format
support), not an audit defect; not implemented in this pass.

**L-09 · CPU kernels: keep assembly; the remaining gap is the interleaved-repack + SMMLA/VNNI
shape.** *(verdict.)* Go 1.27's `archsimd` has no dot-product intrinsics and an unstable API — not
a substrate for the W4A8 kernels yet; watch 1.28. The field's CPU int4×int8 path (KleidiAI:
4×4/8×8 interleaved weight repack + `SMMLA` outer products; Intel: int8 activations with VNNI beat
4-bit bandwidth savings once cores scale) is the shape of the "4-bit matmul to bandwidth class"
item already scoped (~40 vs ~67 GB/s aggregate). Nothing new to file; the scoping stands.

**DISPOSITION — CONFIRMED, verdict stands (2026-09-03).** No new dot-product substrate has landed
(this pass's own kernel work stayed on hand-written assembly throughout); nothing to revisit.

**L-10 · KV quantisation: the repo's NO-GO stands.** *(verdict.)* TurboQuant is still unmerged in
llama.cpp (PR #21089 closed; discussion #20969 only), shipped only in forks and QVAC; mlx-lm's
PolarQuant draft runs at 0.5× decode; llama.cpp and Ollama default to f16 KV. The in-repo spike
(`docs/completed/task-turboquant-spike.md`, NO-GO 2026-06-14) and the "KV-quant refuted as a speed
lever" result need no revisiting. Keep f16 default, q8_0 opt-in where it exists.

**DISPOSITION — CONFIRMED, verdict stands (2026-09-03).** `docs/completed/task-turboquant-spike.md`
exists as cited. No action needed.

**L-11 · Grammar-constrained speculation: the repo's negative result is ahead of the field.**
*(verdict.)* The only shipped form anywhere is vLLM's validate-and-truncate (draft tokens checked
against the grammar bitmask, truncated at the first invalid position — accept length collapses
~7→~3); no engine ships grammar-aware *drafting*. goinfer measured the premise dead on 2026-08-26
(tokenization-boundedness: the mask forces a legal-but-different tokenization the drafter never
sees; the sign inverts where the target is fluent). Nothing to borrow. The result itself is
publishable and unclaimed — see L-18.

**DISPOSITION — CONFIRMED, verdict stands (2026-09-03).** No action needed beyond L-19's publication
suggestion.

**L-12 · Positioning.** *(verdict.)* The README lane claim that "the pure-Go ports are archived
toys" is falsifiable now that goccy/go-llama exists; the true and durable statement is that the
transpiled ports are single-threaded with a 4 GiB linear-memory cap and no GPU, and goinfer's edge
is parallelism plus native GPU backends, not kernel quality — which inverts if goccy ships a
threaded build. The requirements the market now sets for "local": 26–35B-A3B on 8–16 GB at
30–40 tok/s (L-01), in-checkpoint MTP heads (L-06), hybrid linear-attention layers with state
rollback under speculation (L-05), NVFP4 (L-08). Single binary, no toolchain, remains the
differentiator no peer has.

**DISPOSITION — CONFIRMED premise, but the specific claim quoted is not in the current `README.md`

no match, so either the claim already reads differently than quoted or lives in a different
positioning surface (a landing page, `docs/book/`) this pass did not check. Messaging/positioning,

acting on this item's wording specifically.

### B. New — proposals specific to this codebase

**L-13 · P19-exact: a max-first two-pass fused attention that keeps A1's bit-identity.** *(new.)*
P19's fused kernel breaks `decode == prefill` and `spec-verify == sequential greedy` only because
the running-max rescale re-associates the sum; FlashAttention's block-wise `acc += P_blk·V_blk` is
otherwise an *ascending* fold over keys, identical to one long ascending loop. Compute the exact
per-row max in a first tiled pass over QKᵀ (no exp, no AV, nothing materialised beyond one
`[kt, kb]` block), then a second pass that recomputes QKᵀ blockwise, exponentiates against the
fixed max, and accumulates the denominator and P·V in the same ascending order and precision the
materialized kernel uses (f64 on the acc64 arm — `linalg.MatmulQKAcc64` already exists in that
form). No rescale ⇒ no re-association ⇒ bit-identical to the materialized acc64 path, with the fused
kernel's memory traffic. The cost is QKᵀ twice (≈ +50% of the attention term's FLOPs). The 1.69–1.73×
P19 win came from cache residency (the `[n, kb]` block vs the 1.4 MB score rows, invisible until
parallelised), not from FLOPs, so a second compute pass over a cache-resident K block is the cheap
side of that trade. If it clears, `--cpu-fast-attention` and the fused path can be default-ON
*without* an output change, the 512 floor goes, and the M-07 divergences (EAGLE, sessions) close by
construction. **Decision rule:** the P19 harness (`decoder/p19_fused_attn_test.go`, row-parallel,
causal, all tiles) at K=4096/8192, three arms — materialized acc64 (off), fused f32 (shipped),
fused-exact acc64 (new) — asserting `!=` on every logit between arms 1 and 3; fund at ≥1.3× over
arm 1, park below 1.1×, and if it lands between, keep the shipped f32 path and record the number.

**DISPOSITION — ACKNOWLEDGED, roadmap item (2026-09-03).** A genuinely well-specified proposal (exact
mechanism, existing harness named, three-arm pre-registered decision rule) — both cited pieces
confirmed present (`decoder/p19_fused_attn_test.go`, `linalg.MatmulQKAcc64`). New kernel-design work,
not an audit defect; not attempted here. Decision rule stands.

**L-14 · Speculation pays on the paged CPU MoE path precisely because that path is not
compute-bound.** *(new.)* `docs/spec/README.md`'s CPU verdict — "an M=K verify pass costs ~K
sequential decodes" — is a statement about compute-bound dense decode. The paged 35B-A3B path
(1.5–2 tok/s on the Mac, ~70% of the token in expert page-ins, G33: capacity misses) is I/O-bound.
A batched verify of K draft rows pages in the *union* of their routed experts once per layer, where
K sequential decodes page in `K·topK` with repeats under a tight slot budget; and P18's expert-major
kernel runs the union at M>1 instead of K GEMVs. llama.cpp's note that MoE MTP gains are "diminished
due to expert-union" is the same effect with the sign flipped: on a resident model the union costs
compute, on a paged one the union *is* the saving. Turn G34 ("block verify pays at K=2–3; the slot
budget caps the width") into a controller: pick K per round so the predicted union fits the slots
(the n-gram drafter's proposals are known before the verify, so the union can be estimated from
the previous rounds' routing). **Decision rule:** paged 35B-A3B decode on the 16 GB Mac, `--spec
ngram` with union-bounded K vs `off`, paired on the same prompts; fund at ≥1.25× tok/s; the prior
CPU verdict stays intact for dense.

**DISPOSITION — ACKNOWLEDGED, roadmap item (2026-09-03).** A sharp, well-reasoned mechanism (I/O-
vs compute-bound distinction, correctly scoped to "the prior CPU verdict stays intact for dense").
New controller logic, not an audit defect; not implemented here. Decision rule stands.

**L-15 · Longest-common-prefix session selection with ring-aware exactness.** *(new, half
borrowed from radix caches.)* The server's LRU reuses a session only when its whole token list is a
prefix of the new prompt (`internal/serveapp/sessions.go:165-174`), while the decoder's
`rewindForReuse` already implements the correct partial-prefix rewind with the ring exactness rule.
Let `acquire` pick the session with the longest common prefix above the system-preamble length, and
let the decoder truncate. A stop-string hit, a `max_tokens` cut mid-word, or a client that edits its
last message then reuses everything before the divergence instead of paying a cold prefill and an
eviction. Combined with L-05 anchors this is the single-user form of vLLM's prefix cache without a
radix tree. **Decision rule:** the P-18 measurement; fund if turn-3 TTFT with a stop string drops
to within 1.2× of turn-3 without one.

**DISPOSITION — the decision rule's measurement is DONE this pass; the fix itself is the structural
work P-18 explicitly deferred here (2026-09-03).** P-18's disposition confirmed `bestExtend`'s
whole-containment rule exactly as described (`internal/serveapp/sessions.go:165-174`, verified
current), proved the cold-prefill-forcing mechanism directly with a new unit test
(`TestBestExtend_stopStringTokensForceColdPrefill`), and measured the real cost on the production
prefill path: **11.07s cold vs 74.6ms warm at 2k tokens, 148×** — comfortably clearing this item's
own funding bar (turn-3 TTFT within 1.2× would require the fix to already exist; the 148× gap is
the measurement that funds building it). The fix itself — `acquire` picking longest-common-prefix
instead of requiring full containment, `Session.rewindForReuse` truncating to it (already correct
per P-18's own trace) — was deliberately NOT implemented in P-18, per the audit's own design: this
item, not a P-item, is its home. Ready to fund against the number now in hand.

**L-16 · Lifecycle by construction — the engineering program that closes the LFM2 class.**
*(new.)* Derive `Architecture.ownForward()` and `KVCache.recurrentState()` once and make the seven
consumers in §0 theme 1 read them; extend `validateResolved` to dims and RoPE tables (M-06); replace
the census fixture list with a registry-driven check plus a reflection-built `LayerWeights`
round-trip (C-03); run `TestSession_reuseParity`/`snapshotRoundTrip` over every committed tiny
fixture in CI (they take `GOINFER_TEST_MODEL` already). Each is small; together they make "a new
family fell through a switch arm" a compile-time or CI-time event instead of an audit finding.

**DISPOSITION — ACKNOWLEDGED, roadmap item; two of its four named prerequisites already shipped
(2026-09-03).** Both M-06 and C-03, which this item names as pieces to fold into the broader
lifecycle-by-construction program, are already FIXED (dated 2026-09-02, earlier in this remediation
pass). The program itself — deriving `ownForward()`/`recurrentState()` once, a registry-driven
census, CI-wired parity fixtures — is a structural engineering initiative spanning the whole
codebase, not a single defect; not implemented here. Worth returning to with the two prerequisites
already in hand.

**L-17 · Gates for the two costs this audit found without a gate: per-token allocation and per-token
host round trips.** *(new.)* A `testing.AllocsPerRun`-style decode gate on one `forward` step per
family (would have caught M-03 the day it shipped), and a counted-syncs-per-token assertion on the
CUDA/Metal decode paths using the `profSyncCalls` seam (would make P-21's 4× undercount visible and
hold the line after L-01). Both run without assets on the tiny fixtures.

**DISPOSITION — ACKNOWLEDGED, roadmap item; its motivating undercount already fixed (2026-09-03).**
P-21's fix (folding `slotIdx` into the same `UploadBatch`) fixed `profSyncCalls`' undercount as a
side effect — it now counts the one synchronize that actually happens per layer, rather than missing
the second one. The gate this item proposes (assert the count directly, on tiny fixtures, in CI)
would still be valuable to PREVENT a future regression of the same shape; not implemented here (a
new CI gate is process work, not a defect fix). Decision rule (none stated beyond "would have
caught") stands as a good idea for a future pass.

**L-18 · Argmax-only tails everywhere greedy, and no full-vocab readback on any backend.** *(new;
partly the existing `tailAllArgmax`.)* The block-spec prompt seed (C-12), the CUDA per-row verify
tail (P-21), WebGPU's every-token `vocab×4` readback and its prefill LM head (P-22) all move logits
the caller reduces to one integer. One device-side argmax per row (Metal has it; CUDA has
`argmax_reduce`; WebGPU needs a 64-lane kernel of the `moeRoute` shape) and a `tailLastArgmax` mode
remove them together. Bit-identity is preserved (argmax tie-break is pinned host-side and
kernel-side alike).

**DISPOSITION — two of its three cited sub-parts already done; only WebGPU remains open
(2026-09-03).** "The CUDA per-row verify tail (P-21)" is exactly the sub-claim P-21's disposition
traced to ground: greedy speculative verify already exclusively calls `PrefillLastNArgmax` (the
batched-head, argmax-only primitive), and the slow per-row `PrefillLastN` it would replace has no
production caller anywhere. "The block-spec prompt seed (C-12)" is separately already FIXED
(dated 2026-09-02, `ResidentSeedArgmax`). Only the WebGPU sub-parts (every-token full-vocab
readback, prefill LM head) remain — real, and explicitly deferred in P-22's own disposition as a
separate backend's separate implementation gap. Net: this item is mostly done already, just not
filed under this name; the remaining WebGPU piece is genuinely open. Not implemented as a unified
change here.

**L-19 · Publish the negatives.** *(new; positioning.)* Four results in this tree are ahead of the
field and unclaimed anywhere: grammar-aware drafting is dead for a tokenization reason (L-11);
TurboQuant is a NO-GO on accuracy at 3 bits on CPU (L-10); KV-quant is not a decode-speed lever;
MTP does not change the CPU arithmetic. They are exactly the shape of the inference primer's
chapter 11, and a written explainer series was already chosen as the visibility route over a bare
repo link. Cost: prose only.

**DISPOSITION — ACKNOWLEDGED, positioning item (2026-09-03).** Writing/publishing work, not a code
defect; not attempted here (out of scope for a code-remediation pass, and not this session's call to
make on the user's behalf). Left for the user to act on.

## §8 — Suggested triage

**Before the next tag** — the ritual must be able to go red, and the tree must not crash on a
shipped family: G-01, G-02, G-03 (three small edits in `cmd/gate/gpu.go` and one in ci.yml; the
Metal parity group will then run for the first time under the ritual, so budget for what it
finds); G-04 (promote five ledger entries); G-05 (red cell ⇒ red verdict); C-01 + C-02 + C-03 as
one LFM2 change set with the tests named in each (or demote the family to "sequential only,
not serializable" until then); C-04 and C-05 (both in the batched-prefill/session seam, both with
asset-free gates); M-33 (a license file and a digest — a release-integrity item, not a code one).

**Immediately after** — the serving items that are one request away: C-06 (a mutex), C-07 and
C-08 (two clamps), M-17 (a write deadline), M-18, M-19, M-21, M-22, M-23; the Metal guard pair
M-01/M-02 (the paged 35B is declined in production today); C-09 (with the paged gpt-oss parity arm);
M-03 (a grow-once guard — measure P-01 first so the fix has a number); C-11, C-12, M-13, M-15
(`--drafter` is a shipped flag and none of its four defects is covered by a gate that can fail).

**Next minor** — C-10 (the o200k walker and a loud default; it is the widest silent divergence in
the tree and the fix is a tokenizer-only change with goldens); M-04, M-06, M-09–M-12, M-14, M-16,
M-20, M-24–M-32, M-34, M-35; the remaining G items; §4 as the files are touched; the P items
whose measurement is cheap (P-01, P-02, P-13, P-14, P-20, P-21).

**Program work, sequenced by prize** — L-01 (with its trace-replay prerequisite and L-02's replay
in the same session), L-13 (the harness exists; one week to an answer), L-07, L-05, L-14, L-04,
L-16/L-17 (the engineering half, which makes the next audit shorter), L-08, L-15, L-18, L-19.

**Not worth scheduling individually:** most of §4 — fix opportunistically.

## §9 — Method, limitations, and what was not confirmed

**Method.** Eleven reviewers, one per subsystem, each with a written brief carrying the repo's
conventions, the prior audit's §7 and dispositions, and the four queues; each was told to prefer
ten confirmed findings to forty speculative ones, to quote the lines, and to keep unconfirmed
suspicions in a separate list. Their reports were consolidated here; overlapping findings count
once and are marked. Every Critical and every gate item, plus the Metal guard, the release
workflow, the sidecar path and the session/ring mechanism, were then re-read from the source by
the consolidating pass before being written up. A twelfth pass surveyed the field with dated
sources and was read against the repo's own prior verdicts before anything was proposed.

**Not done.** No build, test run, race detector, fuzzing or device execution (go1.24.7 against a
`go 1.27.0` requirement, no module proxy, no GPU). The audit copy omitted `*.safetensors`,
`*_golden.json` and `docs/assets/`; the two conclusions that leaned on their absence were corrected
against the Mac. aikit was not readable; the M-invariance of its `MatmulW8A8` on the webgpu staged
backend (which P18's bit-identity and the dense batched path both assume), `Upload`'s implicit
synchronise, whether `Encoder.Dispatch` rebinds per dispatch (G-08's exact symptom), whether the
CUDA opt-in shared-memory attribute is ever set (M-16), and whether `NewBufferOf` copies or wraps
(M-02's doubling) are all stated as assumptions where they matter. Line numbers are as of
`cfec302`; the tree moves daily.

**Recommended follow-up not attempted here:** `go vet` + `staticcheck` under the tagged variants
across all five modules with the real toolchain; the race detector against two concurrent
`Generate` calls on a `StreamWeights` model and against `handleChatTools` with a prose-emitting
model and a 100 ms heartbeat (C-06); a fuzz target for `LoadSession` that mutates one header/payload
field and recomputes the CRC (M-04); `gguf-dump` of the local gpt-oss/Llama-4/Kimi GGUFs to confirm
their `tokenizer.ggml.pre` strings (C-10); and the P-01 measurement, which decides whether M-03 is a
perf item or a regression.

**Unconfirmed leads** — worth one look each, not reported as findings:

- `decoder/registry.go:1670-1538` asserts transformers 5.12's `DeepseekV3Attention` does NOT fold
  `mscale_all_dim²` into `scaling`, while `:1526-1528` says the real models do; every transformers
  the reviewers know folds it. If 5.12 still folds, V2-Lite's softmax temperature is off by
  ~1.59× and only the int8-vs-bf16 cosine bar (0.99) is hiding it. Check the pinned HF source.
- `cuda/resident.go:1366-1322`: CUDA `ForwardN` with `r.dnet != nil` and K>1 runs the sequential
  loop and does not decline — C-17's disposition chose "decline at the runner that owns the state"
  for webgpu so the fix would not depend on `specRollbackSafe` one package away; CUDA depends on it.
- `cuda/prefill.go:409-223`: `nonBatchableKind` returns `""` for an UNLOADED projection, which the
  caller reads as "batchable"; dense Qwen3.8 DeltaNet layers are declined only because
  `cuda/backend.go:1043` zeroes their geometry — a coincidence, not a guard. `layerFusable`
  (`cuda/backend.go:53-55`, `:1214-1224`) likewise never looks at `qGate`/`kEqV`.
- `cuda/backend.go:225-244`, `:916-921`: no shape validation of the DeltaNet projections against
  `dnetParams` at build; a mis-sized loader tensor would be an OOB device write, not an error.
  `argmax_reduce` returns −1 for an all-NaN row (`cuda/argmax.cu:22-29`) and `ForwardArgmax` hands
  that to `decoder/model.go:1175` as a token id. `Close()` sets `r.reqCh = nil`
  (`cuda/resident.go:1527`), so a later `Forward` blocks forever on a nil-channel send.
- `cuda/foreign_context_test.go:52-79` uses `nvidia-smi --query-compute-apps`, which lists compute
  contexts; a Wayland compositor holds a graphics/EGL context and may not appear, so the floor gate
  could go red on a desktop again.
- `metal/kernels.go:515`, `:537`: RoPE angle is `float(pos)*invf` in f32 with fast-math `cos/sin`;
  at pos → 4096 the argument is ~4e3 rad and Metal's fast range reduction is not precise there,
  while the CPU computes in f64. Unmeasured contribution to the depth-dependent Metal-vs-CPU gap;
  a per-position table would be bit-identical to itself and cheap. `route_gptoss` (`metal/moe.go:106-110`)
  writes `taken[uint(-1)]` on NaN logits. `TestMetal_CloseFreesMemory` keys on RSS, the instrument
  `CLAUDE.md` records as inverting under UBC reclaim. The generic MoE path's `moe_route` runs on
  int8-quantized activations while Gemma-4's was moved to a pure-f32 router for exactly the
  near-tie flip reason — tolerance-gated, but the one discrete decision taken from a quantized input.
- `gpu/decoderunner.go`: `Device.Poll(true)` has no timeout and `Run` never consults a context — a
  wedged adapter hangs the serve worker while holding `lm.mu`; the resident `Forward` runs on the
  shared `Context` without `webgpuBackend.mu` while a staged `MatmulW8A8` may hold it (no Go-side
  race found; `gpu/gpu.go:51-51` documents the Context as not goroutine-safe). WGSL `round()` is
  half-to-even; `packKVInt8`/`UploadKV` use `math.Round`. `mambaConv` with `dConv == 1` underflows
  `K-2u`; `mambaGemvF16` drops the last element for odd K. `layerNormRows` (`gpu/vision.go:52-54`)
  uses `E[x²]−E[x]²` in f32.
- `decoder/kvsnapshot.go:241-243`'s bound comment ("each of the pos positions stores ≥1 byte") is
  false for all-ring families (a ring stores W rows, not pos), so a legitimately long Mistral/Phi-3
  session (`pos > ~8·W`) would be rejected as "corrupt or malicious" → cold prefill.
- `decoder/forward_gemma4.go:216` treats a genuine `layer_scalar == 0` as absent; `HiddenLast`
  admits gpt-oss and LFM2, both plausibly right (pre-final-norm residual) and neither gated.
- `PrefillLastNArgmax`'s lossless bar is argmax-equality, not bit-identity
  (`docs/spec/08-dspark-dflash.md:2004-2018`); a batched head GEMV that differs in the last ulp flips
  near-tie argmaxes at some unmeasured rate, so `--drafter` may not be token-identical to plain
  greedy even after C-11/M-13. Measure per-row vs batched agreement over ≥10⁴ real positions.
- `tokenizer/bytelevel.go:400-428`: normalizers other than NF{C,KC,D,KD} (`Strip`, `Lowercase`,
  `Replace`, `Prepend`) are silently ignored; `fromGGUF` keeps the first id for a duplicate token
  string in `vocab` and the last in the added trie (`tokenizer/gguf.go:119-123` vs `:197-201`);
  `streamTokens` drops the decode error (`internal/serveapp/openai.go:1073`, `:1099`) — as of the
  pre-R-08 shape, one sampled id past the tokenizer table blanked the rest of the stream with
  `finish_reason:"stop"`, because the whole-sequence `DecodeContinuation(ids)` re-decode errored
  on EVERY call from that point on (the bad id stays in `ids` forever). The 2026-09-03 R-08
  rewrite decodes incrementally (`DecodePiece(id)`, one token at a time) as an unrelated
  side effect narrows this: a single bad id now empties only that one token's piece and every
  later good id still decodes normally, so the failure mode shrinks from "blanks the rest of the
  stream" to "silently drops one token's text" — still a dropped error worth fixing, not the same
  severity.
  `compileObject` ignores a `properties` value that is not an object (`constrain/schema.go:196`).
  JPEG parity (Go's `image/jpeg` vs PIL/libjpeg chroma upsampling) is ungated — the preprocess
  goldens are PNG. `LoadQwenPreprocessConfig` reads only `min_pixels`/`max_pixels`, not the newer
  `size:{shortest_edge,longest_edge}` keys.
- `docs/task-w4a8-neon-bandwidth.md:98` and `docs/task-oracle-refforward.md:15` cite concrete files
  under aikit's `docs/internal/`, which `CLAUDE.md:169-172` calls a forbidden destination "including
  in another repo"; whether that directory is gitignored in aikit could not be checked here.
  `docs/cuda-backend.md:26`, `:161` claim Windows for the CUDA backend; nothing cross-compiles
  `cuda/` for windows and `cuda/doc.go:10` names only `libcuda.so.1`. `cmd/gate/gpu.go:973-1028`
  (`cudaPTX`) rewrites the committed PTX in place with no signal handler (an interrupted run leaves
  a dirty tree, reported INCONCLUSIVE — visible, not silent). `metal/moe.go:538-544` and
  `metal/gemma4_moe.go:333-336` `panic` on a `pread` error during expert paging in a serve process.
  `cuda/go.sum:15-16` and `metal/go.sum:11-12` carry `go.uber.org/goleak` hashes no go.mod requires.
  The derived text cap (`ctx × maxTokBytes × 4`) × 128 in-flight bodies is not bounded against RAM
  on a 1M-context model — a design boundary worth a startup warning.

**Coverage.** Read in full: every non-test `.go` file in the five modules except `gpu/cmd/chat`,
`metal/cmd/*` and `demo/gemma-web/`; every `.cu` except the test-only bench variants; the MSL and
WGSL sources; the workflows, all five `go.mod`/`go.sum`, `RELEASING.md`, `CLAUDE.md`,
`cmd/gate/*`, the lint, the ledger, the registry scripts, `docs/env-vars.md`, `docs/server.md`,
`docs/api-tiers.md`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/README.md`, `docs/giw-bundles.md`,
`docs/cuda-backend.md`, the four queues, `docs/spec/README.md`, and the prior audit's dispositions.
Tests were read where a gate's ability to fail was in question (~90 of ~660), and sampled by grep
for skips, env reads, sleeps, tags, hardcoded paths and `*ForTest` otherwise. The eleven per-slice
reports, with their own "files read" lists, "checked and found correct" sections and unconfirmed
leads in full, were delivered alongside this file and are not committed.
